# Gaps

> **Last reviewed:** 2026-11-01

Every known shortfall, one row each, ranked within each section by user impact. This is the work list. The target is the reference app's desktop edition, and M1 sets which release. The reference app was not inspected, nothing was launched or measured, and our output has not been opened in it by the project.

## Top risks

| # | Gap | Why it ranks here | Doc |
|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | The real reference app unverified: nobody on the project can run it, so we cannot prove the reference app accepts our files | The project's largest risk; files may open with a repair prompt and we would not know until someone reports it. Every assumption about the reference app's behaviour is listed in the project's list of assumptions to check | [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md) |
| T2 | A diagram opens into the model, draws (`crates/render`) and shows in the window, but the drawing has never been compared with the reference app's | Everything the product is for | [roadmap](roadmap.md) |
| T3 | No lawyer has reviewed the project, and legal review is pending | Affects what can ship | [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md) |

## Features

| # | Gap | Evidence | User impact |
|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | The ShapeSheet model exists, a reader fills it (`crates/vsdx`, `file.open`), and `crates/calc` works its cells out and scores them against the saved values, `_ELLIPSE_THETA` and `_ELLIPSE_ECC` are worked by the rules fits on saved values, and the oracle compares their cells by the ellipse the row's `C` and `D` describe together (the other end of an axis, the other axis with the reciprocal ratio and any angle of a circle agree; a ratio held as its reciprocal does not), counted in `calc.agreed_by_curve`, not yet scored against the sample files; not worked: `THEMEVAL` under a custom colour scheme (65535) or for the bevel, glow, reflection, soft-edge and sketch properties, which are `Unsupported` and never counted as agreed; the nine path functions (`crates/calc/src/fn_path.rs`, on `diacraft_geom::build_row_draws`) and `TEXTWIDTH` and `TEXTHEIGHT` (`fn_measure.rs`, laid out by `crates/text` in the faces the caller gives, the bundled ones otherwise) are worked and not yet scored against the sample files, and a cell that measured text in a stand-in that is not the name's open metric twin is left out of the score under `text_stand_in` (harness schema 5), because its widths differ; `TEXTHEIGHT` and `TEXTWIDTH` carry a small term of 5E-5 inch inside the drawing scale for a text that holds a character (a constant that ties with the samples' drift of up to 7.1E-6 inch at the oracle's 1E-4); `CEILING` and `FLOOR` work on the number shown in its unit (is our rule, no sample), and so do `INT`, `INTUP`, `ROUND`, `TRUNC`, `MODULUS`, `LN` and `LOG10` (our rule, no sample tests it yet; `SQRT` and `POW` are powers of the unit and read the same on the stored and the shown number); `THEMEPROP` on a sheet that reads no theme is scored and gives 2 for `Embellishment`; the leave-out for stale colours is narrowed to the cells whose saved value is the file's first theme part's reading and not the sheet's own, and the harness reports how many (`calc.left_out.stale_theme_colour`) and by which functions (`calc.stale_theme_colour_by_function`); the report's schema is 7; `THEMEVAL` under an explicit theme, `THEMECBV` and `THEMEPROP` work from the theme part's lists, which `crates/vsdx` reads under the same budgets; a run over the sample files read them with no panic, abort or timeout, and the reader's caps are now set beside the real maxima (`vsdx::Limits`: 8,000,000 formula nodes, 4,000,000 cells, 2,000,000 shapes, 12,000,000 XML items kept, 6,000,000 in one part, 512 MiB of XML, 512 MiB of pictures, and **a memory budget of 2.6 GB** that holds the file, the package inflated, the tree of the part being read and the model, each charged by its real bytes before it is made, so that a read past it is refused in words as `too_much_memory`: **a parse is bounded before its tree is made**, by the items the pool can hold at the dearest a tree can be (368 bytes an item, which a test holds every kind of tree to) once the room for its text is taken out (twice the part's text, and a copy of it for a part in UTF-16), **and each read is given what the process leaves of the budget**: what every session in it holds, the open drawings' files and models and the open stencils' models, in a ledger of the engine (`diacraft_engine::memory`: per process, since the window reads a file in a session of its own while its session holds the drawing), **and what a read that is running has reserved for itself** (a reservation of all that is left, taken in one step after the file is loaded and held until the model has a hold of its own, and given back on a refusal, a panic or a cancel; it counts for the reads and not for the edit guard of a session, which would let go of every undo step for an edit made while a cancelled Open still parses; so that a read that finds the budget held by a read that is running waits for it (up to 30 seconds, and takes no part of what a drawing gives back meanwhile; `stencil.open`, which the window runs on its own thread and must not wait, is given a slice of 192 MiB beside the read, one at a time, out of the 0.47 GB margin, and is refused in words that say a file is being read when the stencil is past it: over about 4,500 masters of a typical shape, which main opens while a file is read; the window asks for the tiles of such a stencil once, and not in every frame, and they are boxes until the read ends) and is then read against what that read left, as the real holds say, and refused in words as `out_of_memory` where the two do not fit together or the wait ends: two small files are both read, one after the other, and two reads of 80,000 shapes at the same moment, as when Cancel is followed by Open while the cancelled thread still parses, are one read and one refusal, and neither aborts; the file's own bytes are loaded before the reservation, so that a thread blocked on a dead disk holds none of the budget, which leaves a file in memory beside a read that took the budget in the moment before it is refused, covered by the 0.47 GB margin for a file of ordinary size), with a refusal that says what is held, **as `out_of_memory` when the file would be read alone and as `too_big` when it would not** (the reader gives no figure of what a file needs, so the engine scales what a read that was cut short had charged for the XML to the rest of the XML of the package (the central directory gives it; the pictures the read had kept are added once and unscaled, and pictures it had not reached are not counted) and tells a file too big only when that is clearly past the budget: an estimate that ran 4 to 24 percent under what a read alone charged, on drawings of 50,000 and 60,000 shapes in pages that are alike (measured on beast, 2026-11-21), so that it errs toward the file); **a stencil that the window cannot open again in the session a drawing is read into is said** (the status bar, and `stencil_notes` in the answer of the open), and the tiles of a stencil file that were asked for while a file was being read are drawn when it has ended; the hostile cases of `timing.rs` and `memory_price.rs` (four parts at the items budget, the most empty cells, a gigabyte of XML, seven pages of unknown elements and a page of chains of nested elements, a page of 250 MB of UTF-16 beside 2.5 million cells, and a second file of 80,000 shapes read beside a drawing of 80,000) are children of the suite that end as a read or a refusal in words under `ulimit -v 3000000`, the widest address space being 2.38 GB (measured on beast on 2026-11-20, release; `crates/vsdx/src/limits.rs` has the table); **that is measured and not proved**: the budget leaves 0.47 GB of the 3.07 GB for what no price counts (the allocator's slack, the process's 143 MB, the walks of a tree once it is parsed), and a read of a kind that no case measured may use it up; **what each kind of thing a file repeats is charged is held to what the model keeps, counted** (`crates/vsdx/tests/it/memory_counted.rs`: 52 kinds and two whole files, at counts one more than a power of two, the charge never under the bytes kept and 24 for each allocation; eight kinds were short, by the 16 bytes an allocation that glibc's own rounding is under that, and a face's attributes by a list priced as made whole where it is pushed into, so a string and a buffer are priced at that figure now, which charges a shape of a diagram about 4 percent more; the law holds an eighth of the bytes kept besides, for the size classes of mimalloc, the allocator the app and the command line run on, and every kind meets it at these prices, while the whole-model codec that the engine weighs a drawing with prices a node of cells at the law, 1,344 bytes where glibc's chunk is 1,168, so the save's check of a `.vsdx` of diagram shapes over three pages admits 69,000 and refuses 70,000, where 76,000 were saved); text is charged at nine eighths of its length in the whole-model codec, which the `.diacraft` format and DiaCraft's data in a `.vsdx` are read with: a cell of a 10 KiB formula, a run of 64 KiB and a kept text of 1 MiB were charged 0.455, 0.893 and 0.889 of the law of the memory tests (the formula's tree holds its string again, and is charged for the strings it copies now), and a drawing of text alone holds a ninth less of it; the VSDX reader met the law already; the most a drawing of diagram shapes can have depends on how its shapes are paged, because the tree of a page is held at 368 bytes an item while it is parsed, where it is some 140: 78,000 shapes in pages of 6,000 and 72,000 in pages of 8,000 (84,000 and 80,000 before a change), 80,000 in pages of 4,000, 77,000 in pages of 11,000 and 65,000 in pages of 13,000 (78,000 in pages of 13,000 were read before the fix round of the review, about 140,000 before the budget, and 150,000 aborted), and the figures rise when the container's parser makes its lists to the size they need and the price is derived again; each file's charge is in the report for the next run over the sample files (`Stats::memory_peak`, `max_tree_bytes`, `memory_bytes`)), and a rerun confirms them, and says which rule of the ZIP reader, if any, refuses a real file; **the model holds everything the file has that no field of it reads: every part of the package the model does not rebuild (unknown parts, custom XML, comments, data connections, validation and extensions, other programs' solutions, a VBA project with its signatures, embeddings, the thumbnail, digital signatures) with its name, content type, role, relationships and bytes in `Document::parts`; the relationship rows no field of the model says, the content-type rows no part needs, the rest of the property parts, the custom properties and `windows.xml` in `Document::vsdx`; the part names of pages and masters; and the attributes, declarations, comments and children of each place the reader types and gives no field to (the roots, `Colors`, `ColorEntry`, `FaceNames`, `StyleSheets`, `Rel`, `Shapes`, `Text`, `Connects`) in the VSDX side of the extension slots, so that the report's skipped list is empty and `no_position_drops_silently` fails on any position that is not in the model; what is held is charged to the read's budget before it is kept (about 4 KB for a drawing with no part to hold, and the bookkeeping of each held part), **and the bytes of a held part live once: they are the package's, moved into the document when the read is done (the bytes the document does not keep are let go first, then one `Arc<[u8]>` copy of a part is made at a time, and the pool is asked for the largest of those copies before any part moves, so a file whose copy has no room is refused in words and the bytes are never held twice in the model, and **a save's check of the file it made reads it without moving the parts into a document** (`read_back`), so the baseline a save hands back holds the model's own `Arc`s and not a second copy of every held part: two parts of 256 MiB are opened, edited and saved twice in a child under `ulimit -v 3000000` (`vsdx_held_parts.rs`))**: three parts of 256 MiB and five of 200 MiB, stored, and a gigabyte of pictures of which three cannot be copied into the model (held as parts, 786 MB), end as reads under `ulimit -v 3000000`, the last of them in the 2.38 GB it needed before (measured by tests built in code, on beast on 2026-11-21, on the test profile); the oracle's report gains `stats.held_parts` and `stats.held_bytes`, new fields that change no score**; **the VSDX writer writes a model that holds anything of a VSDX package from the model alone, with no file to splice into** (the held parts from the bytes the model holds, every relationship row under its `Id`, **every name a part of the model goes by reserved before the writer names a part of its own, each page and master with the rows of its own part (carried to the new name, their targets taken to where they lead, when it must be renamed) and the rows between pages and masters made from the model**, **every row a page had written while the content stamp says the drawing is as it was read** (a row to a master no shape on the page uses, or to a background page that is not the page's, was left out of an unedited visit in 7 sample files; after an edit those rows are made from the model), **a document part that had no `FaceNames` given none** (it was given an empty one, which the schema refuses), every part the writer makes from fields given the content type of what it is, the pages and masters under the part names and ids they had, the slots, the properties and `windows.xml`, and the content types; a model whose held parts would take more than the address space of a process to write and read back is refused in words; a save into a file now writes a change to the properties and to the unknown elements of the document), and **how the file was spelt is recorded in the model and written back as it was** (that `dc:title` was not there, where `xsi:type` and `xml:lang` were, the namespace of each part, a `PageSheet` that was not there, a text before a `Rel`, the order of the fields of `core.xml` and `app.xml`, the order of the kept nodes of a shape and of its sheet), **and what the model cannot say is written by one convention** (the choice of a `Default` row against an `Override` for a part's type, which the content check is to compare as effective types; the page-name list of `app.xml` when its groups cannot be told apart, and a list that never named the pages is rewritten when it differs from the model's pages, as the model holds no earlier names to tell an edit from a list that was always so: the evidence run measures how often a package differs from the one it came from); **DiaCraft's data is written where the file has its part when another program's solutions share the index, and a model with none leaves the file's as it is**; **a save into a VSDX file and its check of the file it made share the bytes of the parts the model holds** (`Package::read_knowing` and `Archive::holds`: an entry whose row states the size and the checksum of the model's part, and whose data comes to both when it is read through the archive's buffer, is the model's bytes and not a copy inflated beside them; any other part is inflated as before), so that the parts the model has held are not held three or four times over: three stored embedded objects of 256, 240 and four of 192 MiB, which the branch refused, are saved at 1,901, 1,789 and 1,837 MiB of address space where `main` takes 2,417, 2,273 and 2,416, two of 256 MiB beside 20,000 shapes, which aborted, at 1,728 against 2,210, and three of 200 MiB of noise at 2,309 against 2,909 (measured by tests built in code under `ulimit -v 3000000`, on beast on 2026-11-21, on the test profile); the check reads the file once, and a check that runs out of memory says so in the words of a save; **a page or master part that another page or master still names stays when one of them is taken out, with what it reaches** (a save into the file took the part and the object it held out, and the rows of the others led to nothing: M10); the catalogue of cell classes covers the batches with a spec file that are not named in a test reads both, and a cell no batch lists resolves as a root-style cell, marked `listed: false` in `Resolved`; the caps (65,536 styles and masters, 1,000,000 shapes a part, 1,000 pages, 200 levels) are project rules that Task 5's run over the sample files confirms; a write judges its cell with a scan of the sheet's sections and rows (the second review measured 65,536 writes, one per master or style, 5.2 s; all 278,528 cells of a 16,384-row section 9.8 s) and `effective_members` costs 90 ms an instance at 1,000,000 omitted members: Tasks 6 and 8 measure their own use; **an empty string that a function takes where it wants a number is 0, and `#VALUE!` no longer** (made an empty `Prompt` of a `User` or `Property` row the empty string where it read as the number 0, and `MAX` given it was `#VALUE!` where the file's cached value is 2, in 2 cells of 1 file; is `unknown` and says the cell "may count as no value at all", which no sample tells from 0, so `MIN(5, empty)` is 0 by our rule and not 5, and the `Unreadable::EmptyIsZero` arm of `crates/calc/src/convert.rs` is where it flips; a word and a string of spaces stay `#VALUE!`, and so does the empty string in `+ - * /`, `^`, the unary signs and `%`, a comparison still counting it as 0; the sample files were not measured again by this task); **the oracle harness (`apps/diacraft-cli/src/oracle`) joins the reader to `calc`'s one comparer, begins every run with a salted control (a document with known-wrong values that must all differ; a calculator that hands back the saved values fails it and the run has no score) and reports every cell as compared, left out by reason, not calculated or holding no formula; no score over the sample files has been recorded** | `crates/doc` | No real documents yet |
| F2 | Settled: an edit no longer audits the document. A page name is checked by the write that sets it, a cap by the door a document enters (`Document::check_limits` for a reader), and `Document::validate` is a pass over the page list for tests, which no edit runs | `crates/doc`, `crates/engine/src/session.rs` | None |
| F3 | Text layout and the font database exist (`crates/fonts`, `crates/text`) and the renderer draws them. Open: vertical text (`TextDirection` 1) is laid out down the block and measured by `TEXTWIDTH` and `TEXTHEIGHT` as it runs across (two sample files saved an empty vertical block that way; text with characters follows the same rule, which no saved value has tested), an East Asian letter upright and the others on their sides (the rule is ours and unverified, and a line under the text lies on the baseline side, the left, for all, where the Text dialog's page puts it at the right for Japanese and Korean text); the faces drawings name are mostly Microsoft's and are never bundled, so on a machine with no metric twin installed the stand-in is the bundled Ubuntu Light, which measured 6 to 11% wider than Carlito, the open face made to match Calibri, on seven labels at 12 pt (the bundled monospace Hack, 29 to 54% wider, is for monospace font names only), so a label can wrap into more lines than the original and a block sized from a stored width can overflow; the optional craft-fonts input (`CRAFT_FONTS_DIR`) follows a sibling's manifest format and has never been run against the real checkout, which this machine lacks; the specs leave open how a tab stop works on a centred or right-aligned line (the stops are placed as if the line began at its left limit) and whether a line ending in a forced break is stretched when justified (it is); a font database holds at most 1 GiB of whole font files and reads at most 512 MiB of `cmap` tables, and its scan of the font folders stops after eight seconds, but a folder on a mount that hangs still blocks it, so Task 10 builds it off the thread of the user interface | `crates/fonts`, `crates/text` | Text on real drawings can break and measure differently from the original until it is compared with saved `TEXTWIDTH` values (estimated, not measured against any saved value) |
| F4 | The renderer (`crates/render`) draws what the specs say, by pixel tests of its own and no reference image from the reference app. Open: **a themed value the theme cannot answer** reads the root style's cell or the table's colour, and the frame says `ThemeUnanswered`: a file with no theme part, a part whose list or entry the reader could not read, a custom colour scheme (65535), and the properties not worked out (bevel, glow, reflection, soft edges, sketch, the East Asian and complex fonts, `UseGroupGradient`); every other themed fill, gradient, line, line pattern, text colour and shadow draws from the part; **EMF, EMF+ and WMF pictures draw** (`crates/metafile` read to a display list and drawn through the same `Target` as everything else, so the window, PNG, SVG and PDF all get them; the EMF+ half is played and the GDI half only when the EMF+ half cannot be), and a picture left out in part gets one row, the first of `gdi-fallback`, `emfplus-misplaced`, `record-skipped`, `glyph-index-text`, `font-missing`, `bitmap-undecodable`, `cut-short` and `cap-reached`; still open: **hatch, texture and pattern brushes** (a Dual file falls back to its GDI half, which has pattern brushes of its own that are reported, not drawn), EMF+ records outside those the sample files use (`FillEllipse`, `DrawString`, `DrawLines`...), `SELECTCLIPREGION` of a WMF, `EMR_SETTEXTJUSTIFICATION`, GIF and TIFF bitmaps, the made-up bold and slant of a missing face, a **path gradient** drawn as a radial one, a **gradient or dash** of a stroke by a radial gradient as its first colour, text written as outlines in every output, and a placeholder only for a header no reader takes; the metafile caps are in `crates/metafile/src/limits.rs` (4,000,000 records, 1,000,000 items, 8,000,000 points, 40,000,000 units of work, 64 clip parts, 4 levels of nesting) and the renderer draws 400,000 items of metafile pictures a frame, decodes at most 64 million pixels a picture, a frame at a time in the time the frame has (one forced decode for the whole frame, and the rest of a picture's bitmaps marked not drawn after eight frames), and the cache weighs a picture by the most its bitmaps can come to; **line ends** are our own marks drawn as shapes them, in the boxes measures for 32 of the 45 numbers and in the boxes DiaCraft's own rule gives the other 13, a size class outside 0 to 6 drawn as class 2 with a note, the `.odg` save writing the same 45 marks as DiaCraft's own markers in the same boxes at every class from 0 to 6, and, for the custom end (254), writing number 4's marker and carrying the cell by DiaCraft's extension, and writing no marker for a class outside 0 to 6 or a number past 45, whose cells the extension carries (F23), and a path whose every segment has no length gets none (the frame says so); **a compound line** (double or triple) draws as one line; **a line narrower than a device pixel** is drawn one pixel wide, in its full colour, by the window and the PNG, and at its true width in an SVG and a PDF (a project rule; its dashes and line ends keep the line's own weight, a weight of 0 is a pixel on a raster and a quarter point in a file; the hatches of a fill pattern and the made-up bold are left alone; whether the target app's own window does the same is unverified); **a number in `TextBkgnd`** is the colour one less than it (our rule: 2 is white); **hatches and stipples** are our own artwork, stipples a flat mixture; **a rectangular gradient** is 24 nested rectangles, and the gradient along the path draws as the centre one; **a blur** is a Gaussian layer, for 400 shadows a frame, its reach cut to 320 pixels past the shape and the layers of a frame held to 32 million pixels in all, and a shadow past either drawn cut or hard-edged, which the frame says; **inner shadows** draw as simple ones; **a picture's adjustments** (brightness, contrast, gamma, blur, sharpen, transparency), GIF and TIFF are not applied or decoded; **a frame is held to** 1.5 s, 400 ms of text layout, 400,000 shapes, 4,000,000 geometry rows and 400,000 laid-out characters, and says what it left out; **`Font` names** are stood in for as `text` says, so labels wrap where the original may not; **a shape whose drawing meets a bug is lost alone** (`ShapeFailed`, with the bug's words as the sample; the frame's net, `Recovered`, stays for a bug outside any shape), and the window counts both as panics. The reference app was not inspected: every choice of the renderer that no spec settles is in the project's list of assumptions to check | `crates/render` | A drawing can differ from the reference app's in the cases named, and nobody can say by how much until the render oracle (Task 8, step 4) and a person who can run the reference app have looked |
| F5 | Export (`crates/render` `export`, `crates/svg`, `crates/pdf`, `diacraft-cli export`, the engine's `file.export`) writes a page as PNG, SVG and PDF through the renderer's own walk, tested by reading each file back (the PDF also with poppler's `pdftotext`, `pdftoppm` and `pdffonts` where they are installed, and the SVG with LibreOffice by eye), never by comparison with the reference app's. Open: **one page at a time** (the window's Export, in the File view and the View tab, writes the page on view; no all-pages PDF, no page range, no paper sizes or print scaling); **a blurred shadow is hard-edged in a PDF**; **SVG text names a face the viewer may lack**, so its look depends on the viewer's fonts (outlines are the choice for a file that must look the same everywhere); **a made-up bold or slant, ligatures, joined and right-to-left scripts are outlines in both**, so they cannot be selected or searched; **the PDF has no tags, bookmarks, links, layers or PDF/A**; **a picture is written at its decoded size** (up to 64 million pixels as a PNG), and a file over 512 MiB is an error | | A drawing leaves the app as a picture from the command line, the control channel and the window (File, Export, and the View tab's Picture), one page at a time |
| F6 | The clipboard, group and ungroup commands (`docs/editing-commands.md`) are within one document and one set of masters: a drawing that is made or opened empties the clipboard, so a paste in another drawing is not done (the matcher it needs, is there); a shape with a picture is pasted on its own page only (the writer adds no relationship to a page part); a group that is an instance of a master cannot be ungrouped and a member of one cannot be copied alone (the cells they take from the master would be lost); a connector cannot be carried out of a flipped group; Paste Special, Paste as Hyperlink and Paste Special and Paste as Hyperlink are not done, and the system clipboard gets the text of the labels only. The reference app's own paste offset and group naming are not known: they are ours | | A paste across drawings, a picture across pages and a master group's instance cannot be moved by these commands yet |

| F7 | A stencil file opens (`stencil.open`, `stencil.list`, `stencil.icon`, `stencil.close`, `shape.drop {stencil}`; `docs/editing-commands.md`, "Stencil files"), tested only on packages built in code. What a drop leaves out: **a master with a picture, an object, an embedded package or another reference to a part of the stencil's file is refused** (until the writer can copy related parts; Task 6 counts how many real masters this refuses); **a stencil's theme part is not copied** (left it for the writer to be able to add a theme part, which it can; which part to bring, and what a master selects, is a later task, F13), so a dropped master's themed cells read the drawing's theme, and shapes from a themed stencil take the drawing's colours; **a master of several top-level shapes drops as a group** whose frame (the joint box of the members on the master's page, pin at its middle) is ours and is on the check list, and one of more than 5,000 top-level shapes is refused; **a master named by `USE()` is not followed**; **a master is dropped at the size it has**, whatever the target page's drawing scale; a master of more than 5,000 descendants is dropped without the members below the top level listed; the stencil's data connections are not copied; the open stencils together may hold what the reader limits for one file (512 MiB of XML, 12 million items, 512 MiB of pictures), counted by what each model took and not by its file. **a master whose kept text declares more than 64 namespace prefixes on one element, or 256 in scope, is refused**, and a kept attribute under a prefix bound out of sight of the kept text is judged closed (the prefix `r`, and the ten attribute names of the relationships namespace, **and four more, `topLeft`, `topRight`, `bottomLeft` and `bottomRight`, pending a spec check**: the copy of the schema read is the first edition of ECMA-376, and the check is to cite those names and the Strict URI). **The declarations of the elements above a kept node within its sheet are followed, but only to add a relationship binding** (a binding there to another namespace is not believed, since the writer need not give it back at that element, so `r` and the names of the namespace stay relationships: a master that keeps an `r:id` under an `r` rebound by its shape is refused) (the shape, sheet, section, row and cell, in nesting order), **and those of the shapes above it (a group) and of the part's root are not**: a prefix bound there is judged closed, as one bound out of sight. A master is matched to the drawing's own by `UniqueID` and `BaseID`, and the drawing's master is used as it is, so a master the drawing has changed is not brought back from the stencil. The window opens a stencil file (More Shapes, Open Stencil), and no real public stencil has been opened | | A stencil with pictures or with a theme looks plainer or drops fewer masters than in the application that made it; the frame of a dropped group of several shapes may differ from the application's |
| F8 | **Settled on the counts of the specification, with the copy of the geometry kept.** A member made from an instance (`shape.group`) holds `Width`, `Height`, `PinX` and `PinY` as formulas of its group (`Sheet.<group>!Width*<factor>` and `Height`; 14,596 to 14,777 of 16,020 nested instances in the samples) and a local copy of its master's `Geometry` (kept so that a reader that does not know the master, LibreOffice in the checks, draws the members; 103 of 114 unresized samples hold none, whether the reference app minds is unknown). The members of a dropped master of several shapes are tied to the group instance, those of a master of one group shape hold the copy and no size cells. `docs/editing-commands.md`, "Snap, grid and guides") are in the engine. **The window** sends `snap: {zoom, view}` with its moves, resizes, turns, drops and connectors' free ends, so they snap by the document's settings, guides and other shapes included, and a box pulled to a guide is glued to it; its previews call the commands' own functions on a page the window keeps and with the words the command is sent, and land where the release does: a move is `snap::snap_ext` with the box `bounds_of` makes, the points `own_points` gives, the turned shape's own `frame` (`frame_of_shapes`), Shift's `lock` and the `view`; a resize is `snap_resize_ext` with `keep` and `view`, a turned or flipped shape along its own axes; the rotation handle is `snap_turn`, sent as `shape.rotate` with `snap: {zoom, radius}`; a drop pulls the master's own connection points with its box (`dropped_points` for a stock master, and `dropped_points_of_file` for a master of a stencil file, which reads them off the shape a drop on a copy of the document makes), and the drop is the ghost: a release before the reader has answered, when the ghost went as the pointer made it, is sent without `snap` and lands where the ghost was; an arrow key is `shape.move` with `snap: {nudge: {tick}}`, `tick` being one tick of the ruler as the window draws it at the zoom, and Shift with an arrow key is a plain move of one screen point, and an arrow key asks the reader for the page before it goes and again after, so that the next press finds it ready; Shift is the engine's `lock` (the page axis the whole drag has gone farther along, a tie to x, the free axis snapping and a box pulled to a guide held); a corner keeps the proportions with no key and a side with Shift (`keep`, the engine working the box out and snapping inside the proportions); Alt held turns snapping off for a drag (a corner then still keeps its proportions, by the window's own box), and the first frames of a drag, before the page's reader has made the index of its shapes and its targets ready (on a thread of its own), go as the pointer made them and are sent without `snap`, as the preview shows them; the marks it draws are the engine's, each by its segment (a line from the dragged shape to the nearest shape on it, along a turned shape's own side too; an equal gap as a double arrow with its length, a turned row's label along the row; a place as a ring; a size and an angle with their labels, none overlapping another; a guide along no axis is drawn like the others and is not taken hold of); the grid it draws is the snap's, at the drawing's scale; it draws the page's guides, makes one by a drag out of a ruler (`guide.add`), moves one by a drag (`guide.move`) and deletes a selected one (`guide.delete`, refused with a word in the status bar when shapes are glued to it); View > Guides hides them, a setting of the drawing's window read from the file's `ShowGuides` (on where it has none) and kept by a save. **The Snap & Glue dialog**, from the Visual Aids launcher and Alt+F9, holds the Snap and Glue switches and every option `snap.settings` has but the dynamic grid: General has each switch at the head of its targets (the flags of `SnapSettings` and `GlueSettings`), Advanced the thirteen shape extension lines; the boxes start from `snap.settings`, OK sends one `snap.set` with what was changed (no undo step: the Snap & Glue options mark the drawing unsaved and Ctrl+Z leaves them) and Cancel nothing; the isometric angles are left out, as no setting holds them, and the six snap strength sliders (below) are its Advanced tab's second half. The Visual Aids group's Dynamic Grid is the document's `dynamic_grid` (one `snap.set` a press), Connection Points a window setting (`view.connectionPoints`, read from the file's `ShowConnectionPoints`, off where it has none, not saved) that marks the shapes' points with every tool, and AutoConnect is quiet (no setting and no window arrows). **Snap on or off is visible in the window** (DiaCraft's own, beside the dialog, which the reference application's desktop View tab and status bar do not have): the Visual Aids group has a Snap box and a Snap to Grid box that show the document's `snap` and `grid` options and send one `snap.set` each, and the status bar a Snap on or off mark that does the same for `snap`; the snap strength is the document's own setting (below). The window's own pick of a connection point or a whole shape under a connector's end takes nothing with the Glue switch or glue to connection points off (whole-shape glue under that option is ours). **Snap strength is a setting (ours).** The six strengths of the Advanced tab, Rulers 4, Grid 5, Guides 10, Points 10, Geometry 8 and Extensions 13 screen pixels (0 to 999), are part of `SnapSettings` and so the document's own: `snap.set` takes `strength_ruler`, `strength_grid`, `strength_guides`, `strength_points`, `strength_geometry` and `strength_extensions` (a value that is not a whole number from 0 to 999 is refused in words) and `snap.settings` answers them. `DocumentSettings` has no element for them and the specification puts them on the application's settings object (where the program keeps them is not published), so DiaCraft keeps them as attributes of the `snap` child of its own data, where the switches are (a VSDX file's solution part, a `.diacraft` file's document data, an `.odg`'s document file), written only for a value that is not the default. Every kind pulls within its strength at the zoom of the query (`craft-snap`'s `Reach::Screen`), and **the grid and the ruler no longer pull from any distance**: a drag farther than 5 pixels from a grid line, or 4 from a ruler tick, lands where it was put (a behaviour change). Points and Guides moved with the specification's defaults (12 to 10 and 8 to 10), and the dynamic grid's alignment, margins, spacings and sizes follow Extensions (13, were 6: R81, with Q4 leaving the alignment box's own strength open), while a turn keeps its 6 pixels for another shape's angle. The dialog has the six sliders with a number box each (0 to 30 on the slider, 0 to 999 in the box; a box that says no whole number of pixels is outlined and OK is refused in words). There are no extension lines yet, so Extensions pulls the dynamic grid only. The limit on a resize's fallback past a collapsing line is for a kind that pulls from any distance, and no kind of the engine does now: at the default strengths a second grid or ruler line is a full pitch (16 points at least) past the first and out of reach, so the fallback is not asked, and at a strength past a pitch the next line in reach is taken, as a guide's is (`snap_repin.rs`). **Every Snap & Glue option the specifications publish is a flag of its word in the file** (`SnapSettings`, `GlueSettings`, `SnapExtensions` and `DynamicGridEnabled`), written with every other bit kept and read back; a new drawing is given the four words at the defaults of the documented values and the sample majority; the Snap and Glue switches stay in DiaCraft's own solution part because that the dialog's boxes are the flag 32768 is only our reading; the windows part's copies are not read or written (which copy the reference app applies when they differ is open); the extension flags are settings only, as nothing draws extension lines. **What a shape being dropped, moved or resized is pulled to is not specified** ("separate options pull shapes to rulers, grid, alignment boxes, guides, geometry, vertices, intersections and handles", and nothing on which points of the shape are pulled, by which switch, or from how far): we pull a box by its nine places and by its connection points, to the connection points, vertices, handles, outlines and outline crossings of other shapes, by the Snap switch and the options of `SnapSettings` (a connector's end by those of `GlueSettings`) (ours), with the object snaps of CADCraft (`snap/osnap.rs`); a place that is a target pulls both axes and the grid does not pull that move, snapping to connection points, vertices, outlines and intersections is on by default for a placing, a shape's own vertices and handles are not pulled, and a resize pulls lines only. **Glue to a guide is written and followed**: a box that `shape.drop`, `shape.move`, `shape.resize` or `shape.place` pulls to a guide with glue and glue to guides on is held by its `Alignment` cells (`INTERSECTX` and `INTERSECTY` of the guide, `_MARKER(1)` in the other four), its pins' formulas and a `Connect` row, and follows the guide by recalculation when it moves or turns, with the connectors glued to it placed and routed again; a write of a held pin that takes its glued edge off the guide's line releases the shape whole and its cells keep their values, while a pin written back on its line (a nudge along the guide) or a write of the free axis of a hold to one guide holds it again where it is; a turn (but a half turn of a shape pinned in its middle) or a flip releases it; every copy is released; a shape that goes releases the shapes held to it, every alignment cell naming it made its value, `GUARD` and all, and so does a guide grouped; a page that grows on the left or the bottom leaves held shapes to their guides; the share form, a single guide cell with no row and a line's end on a guide are read and kept and never written; a turned or flipped shape, a member of a group and a guide whose line misses its pin are not held; the place the formulas name (the box's middle) and every rule above that the spec does not give are ours (the check list); `guide.delete` refuses a guide that a row or a formula names, and `shape.delete` of it releases its shapes. a shape held to a guide is not saved (a new one with a warning, one of the file refused in words). **Snapping goes through `craft-snap` (a dependency from the one-bit fork of craft-libs, pinned by revision):** `snap::snap` gives the library the page's targets once for a drag (kept on the `SnapPage`, by the shapes that move) and tells what it gives in the old words; the old function is kept test-only as the oracle of a seeded harness (`snap/equal.rs`) that holds the adapter equal to it on random pages under the old policy. **Built of the design's fifteen changes:** 1 (`shape.move` takes `snap: {lock: "x" or "y"}`: the move keeps to that axis and the free axis snaps; a shape the budget cannot pay for, one that draws no path and a group of more than 256 shapes keep the box round their corners and are counted, `bounds_by_corners` for a target and `snap.corner_boxes` in the result of `shape.move` for the moving shapes), and 15 (a shape whose `LinePattern` is 0 offers no alignment lines and stays in equal-gap rows; a pattern set nowhere keeps them). **Fixed after the review:** the outline budget of a call is its distinct shapes (a box with places of its own is asked twice, and each shape's outline is built once, as the old function read it); a command takes only the words of `snap` it has (`lock` and `nudge` are `shape.move`'s, `keep` is `shape.resize`'s, `radius` is `shape.rotate`'s, `zoom` and `view` every command's) and refuses the others in words; a resize with `keep` of a shape too thin for its proportions to fit the page is refused in words; a size match on the axis a kept side resize grows about its middle is told, with the middle as its anchor; a turned shape far from the origin is still turned (its sides are square to a billionth of their length and the rounding of its corners); `shape.place` and `snap.at` align a turned shape by the box `shape.move` uses, `snap::bounds_of`; the marks of a call that asks the library again are those of the queries that found its hits (128 of 1,991 lines snapped to on 6,000 seeded pages had none). **Row 3 and the library's three settings (`craft-snap` at d9c34cd29):** the pin moved to the fork's `craft-snap` head after the review of the library's places and marks; the adapter's harness gives the same classes at both pins on its 13 pages (the earlier pin's `turned` row was recounted at the commit before this one as well: 2,486 equal, 3 ties, 7 flips and 4 bands). **Places on a line of travel:** `Policy::ADOPTED` sets `locked_places` and `nudge_places` to `OnLine`, so a connection point, vertex, handle, outline point or crossing pulls a move held to an axis where it lies on a line one of the box's places travels along (the box lands on it, the other delta 0 to the bit) and stops an arrow key as the nearest stop of any kind, a better ranked place farther on never taking a nearer one. **A line mark to each side:** `line_span` is `EachSide`, so a mark runs from the nearest shape on one side of the box to the nearest on the other, through it. **Turned shapes' own sides:** a call that is told its subject's frame (`Extras::frame`; `snap::frame_of_shapes` makes it for one shape turned by other than whole quarter turns, and `shape.move`, `shape.place` and `snap.at` pass it with the box round the paths) aligns the shape's own sides in up to four directions, the objects offer their own lines (a shape turned a quarter, half or three-quarter turn from the subject's offers the same lines), a guide along no axis is a target (`SnapPage::slanted`: for a subject with a frame, never for a point, an unturned box or a connector's end) and a snap to it writes no hold; a hit along such a line has a `normal` (degrees, in the JSON) and no `axis`, and a mark has `axis` null; a row of shapes turned alike is measured and marked along its own direction; a turned subject is asked once (the library takes the combination that snaps the most directions within the larger reach of its candidates, so two page lines each within reach but farther than that together are not both taken, as a box square to the page has each axis taken apart), because a move along one axis would take its own sides off their lines and the marks would no longer be where the shape lands. **A turned or flipped shape's resize:** `snap_resize_ext` and `shape.resize` map the handle to its side through the corners, so a flipped shape's handle is the side it is on, the frame's sides move along its own axes with the angle and the fixed sides unchanged, a corner or the middle of a moving side meets a page line, an own side meets the like side of a shape turned alike, a width or height meets another's, and `keep` keeps the shape's own proportions; the answer is `Snapped::frame`; a shape whose corners on the page are no rectangle, or that is a member of a turned group, is not snapped. **Exact far out:** a frame's angle is the cells' (`Angle`, and a half turn for a flip in x) when the corners agree with it to the rounding of their numbers, and a guide's direction is the shape's turn applied to the way its line runs, so own sides and slanted guides meet to the bit at a million inches and a billionth of a radian off is another direction. **Words (the F1 re-review):** `snap.at` refuses `connect` beside a box, `edges`, `nudge`, `keep` and `rotate` in words (it is the end of a connector, a place), and `snap.at` and `shape.place` tell `snap.corner_boxes` as `shape.move` does. **Cost (counted, `snap/turned_cost.rs`):** a turned subject among 5,000 shapes turned 30 and 75 degrees gathers nothing and costs 346 steps of bisection against 321 for a square one on the same page (five thousand directions a hair apart: 522; five thousand shapes on three own lines: 555 and 3 line steps), and a subject turned 1e-6 to 1e-2 radian is never thrown along a line; measured on a loaded machine, later queries of a drag take 6.4 microseconds against 4.4, and the first, which builds the targets (60,000 lines against 30,000), 81 ms against 45. **The library moved on (`craft-snap` at 32408ec64, the fork's head after a change):** of the two differences from the old function that the harness recorded at d9c34cd29, a long pull beside a hit at rest is gone (a candidate dropped where the box lands, such as an equal gap that no longer repeats, is replaced by the next place in reach, tried alone and in the pairs it was in: `a_pull_beside_a_hit_at_rest_is_made_by_both`, and a case of it is now a failure of the harness) and the marks of two spacing hits at once are drawn for each direction from that direction's own hit (four of the five cases of the mixed pages are gone); one case of that class is left, held to the number 1 (`a_spacing_hit_whose_neighbours_left_the_row_is_told_without_its_gap_while_another_pair_holds_the_same_centre`: under the old row rule and the marks of what was snapped to, the other axis's pull takes the box out of the row of the neighbours the hit names while another pair holds the same centre, and the library tells no gap and draws no mark for it; the adopted rule tells and marks the gaps the box has; the case also holds at the earlier pin). The old function's equal-gap claim that fails where the box lands is dropped by the library now, so that class holds 18 cases of the harness's pages (the earlier pin had 3: there the library repeated the claim, and the case was equal). A resize whose nearest line, or width or height, would leave the box no size takes the next line of its kind in the way the box grows (one line past), so hold (`a_guide_that_would_leave_the_box_no_width_is_passed_over_for_the_next_guide_in_reach`); a grid line that would flip the box was followed by the next grid line, which outranked a nearer ruler line (a grid line ranks before a ruler line, and both pull from any distance); **A later pin of the library (`craft-snap` at 2cec704b6)** limits that fallback, for a kind that pulls from any distance (the grid, the ruler), to no farther than the nearest line was: the side goes to the nearer ruler line or stays where it was dragged (`a_grid_line_that_would_flip_the_box_is_followed_by_the_nearer_ruler_line_and_not_by_the_next_grid_line` and `with_the_ruler_off_a_grid_line_that_would_flip_the_box_leaves_the_side_where_it_was_dragged`, both red at 32408ec64 with the side at 16, 4.79 inches away); a guide or an alignment line keeps the rule, the next line in its own reach however far the first was, and another kind's own nearest line is not limited. The harness's classes are the same on every page (the counts of equal, ties, tolerance, flip, band, anchor and naming ties, edge of reach and unmarked are as at 32408ec64; the resizes with a hit fall by 17 in 42,900); of the 49,885 resizes of the cross-pin comparison 292 differ from 32408ec64 under the adopted policy and each changes only a grid or ruler hit (250 lose one, 42 take a ruler hit for a grid hit, 0 a farther line), and 30 differ from d9c34cd29, the pin before a change: 22 are an exact tie of a lattice (the pointer midway between two lines, the one past the collapsing line as near as it) and 8 a guide or alignment line within its own reach. The engine's nudge asks with no alignment reach (`Policy::ADOPTED` is the whole page), so the library's fix for a nudge that has one changes only the policy of before the library that the harness uses. **Built by (the fix round of the review):** the Text tool's drag is a box dragged out from the corner the press landed at, its moved sides snapped as a resize's are, to the width or height of a shape in the view, with a size mark on both boxes (the Text case; `text.box` takes no `snap`, so the box shown is the box sent), a master of a stencil file brings its own points to the drop preview (`dropped_points_of_file`), the angle mark of a turn is drawn at the shape's pin (`pin_of`) and not at the middle of its box, and a release that changes what it moves by a billionth or more is sent (a change under a millionth was left unsent, though its preview showed it). **Measured:** a page of 100,000 shapes costs one kind check each (1 to 2 ms) and the frames of the first 5,000 (10,000), and every result says `truncated`; reading every frame costs 8 to 12 microseconds a shape (40 ms at 5,000 shapes, 90 ms at 10,000, 150 to 290 ms at 20,000 and 0.8 to 1.3 s at 100,000 on a loaded 32-core machine), the library's targets about a microsecond a shape on top, and 1.3 to 1.8 KB a shape for the index and the targets together (the document itself takes 1.7 KB a shape); a snapped `shape.move` on a page of 5,000 shapes costs 50 to 75 ms where a plain one costs 4 to 5. The cap is 10,000 (it was 5,000, until the window made the page's index ready off its own thread; about 90 ms and 16 MB, which the window's reader pays on its thread and, no command pays: the session keeps the page the reader made, one for each page, by the document's revision and its snap settings and about 64 MiB by an estimate of what each holds (`SnapPage::held_bytes`, priced from the process's measured resident memory: an object 1,600 bytes where the first prices, 2.4 times too low, had 256, so a page of 10,000 shapes is charged 19 MiB and holds 8.5 to 15.6; checked when a read is kept or found, so the sum can pass the cap by what one page grows to after it is kept), and a release, an arrow press, a turn, a resize and a drop find it; a command on a page nobody made ready reads it as before, and an edit, a new drawing and an opened one drop the kept pages. Counted: a command on a ready page of 10,000 shapes makes no page read, no index and no set of targets (`snap::counts`). Timed on the review's pages, a release is 5.8 ms beside a plain move's 5.8 on 10,000 upright shapes (101 ms on a page nobody made ready), and 11 ms beside 7.5 on 10,000 turned stock shapes (348 ms cold, the reader's work 331 ms), on a loaded 32-core machine). The boxes round the paths of 10,000 turned stock shapes cost about 86,100 points and 121,000 elements. **The page budget is 32 times a query's (it was 16 times, set for 5,000)**: 131,072 points and 524,288 elements, so a page of 10,000 stock shapes, every one turned, is paid for in full (at 16 times 7,617 of them were, and 2,383 kept the box round their corners and said `truncated`); a crafted page spends the whole budget in some 150 to 320 ms on the reader's thread (the review's measurements, against 134 to 215 at 16 times). A page of turned shapes with more points than the budget pays for keeps the box round the corners of the last of them, counted (`bounds_by_corners`) and `truncated`. **Built by the fix round of the narrow re-review of F2:** a drop makes the page ready (the index of its shapes and the set of targets) from the document as it is, before its edit (a drop snaps the shape it has just made on the draft of its edit, the one command that asks a read with a draft, and an index made there held that shape in a read the session kept past a batch that failed, so the next snapped move lined up with a shape that was not there; the page is made ready for the one query the drop makes, so a page whose settings snap to no shape still reads none, and every command that snaps is tested with a batch that fails after it); a page made ready for one drag is not ready for another drag's shapes (the engine holds the one set of targets it built last), so a third drag at one revision builds nothing on the window's thread; and the page of 10,000 turned stock shapes of the R45 tests is built in one pass (0.6 s where it took 72 to 94 of the runner's 120). |

The samples spread from 4.0E-5 to 6.2E-5 inch at 1:1 and drift by up to 7.1E-6 with the size and the line count, so a formula that tests a measure for equality with a fixed height can flip on a size where the file's own measure is on the other side (shows the case at 15 pt). The sizes and line counts at which the real term crosses a fixed height are unknown. Evidence: `crates/calc/src/rules.rs` `TEXT_MEASURE_TERM`, `fn_measure.rs`. | | A sample set that settles the term's dependence on size and lines |
| F21 | **Rounding functions and units.** `CEILING` rounds the number shown in the unit the number carries (samples settle this). `FLOOR` does the same by our rule (no sample), and so do `INT`, `INTUP`, `ROUND`, `TRUNC`, `MODULUS`, `LN` and `LOG10` (the number shown in the argument's unit, internal units for `DL` or no unit; our rule, no sample tests it; `INT(1.5 ft)` is 12 stored inches); `SQRT` and `POW` work on the stored value, which is the same result for a power of a unit. A `multiple` or a `MODULUS` divisor with no unit counts in the number's unit; one that carries a unit of its own, a reference to a typed length cell (a quantity in `DL`) included, counts as its stored value shown in the number's unit; one of another measure than the number's counts the same way and is no error (all ours). The result of `LN` and `LOG10` of a length not in inches is the logarithm of the number shown, in the argument's unit. A public file with such a formula and its cached value would settle each. Evidence: `crates/calc/src/fn_math.rs`, `rules.rs`. | | Samples with these functions on non-inch lengths |
| F22 | **Stale colours: the second reading is a hypothesis.** The narrowed leaves a colour out when the file's first theme part gives the saved value and the sheet's no-theme reading does not. 'The first theme part' is our guess at what a stale value was saved under; a scheme cell the sheet holds none of selects it, and an outright 0 does not. The rule can mask a no-theme bug where the first part's colour equals the true no-theme one (an Office `dk1` or `lt1` against `Dark` or `Light`), and `calc.stale_theme_colour_by_function` is where a jump shows (per file; the aggregate does not sum it). The live mode keeps one memo of the second reading for the whole walk (about twice the first reading's work, linear in a chain). **Memory.** In the live mode both memos are held for the whole walk, so the peak memory of a file with many stale cells doubles (3.4 MB at 1,600 cells), and a large live run reaches `memory_exhausted` sooner; the CLI's `oracle` runs in the cached mode, which memoises nothing. Evidence: `crates/calc/src/recalc.rs`, `eval.rs`, `fn_color.rs`. | | A sample that shows what a stale colour was saved under; the aggregate summing the tally |
| F23 | **OpenDocument Drawing (`.odg`) is under way** (the ODG plan). The package layer landed first (`crates/odg`): `sniff`, `Package::read` (exact names; the manifest by namespace; an irregular `mimetype` read with a warning; an OpenOffice.org manifest DOCTYPE carrying only an external id read and ignored, every other DTD refused; encryption refused before any other entry is inflated; signatures and macros detected and kept as bytes), `copy_unchanged`, and `diff_packages`, an element-level comparison by path, with moves and white space told apart, at linear counted cost; `diacraft-cli roundtrip` and `compare` take `.odg` and `.otg`. A copy keeps each unchanged entry byte for byte but drops the archive comment, bytes between entries and ZIP64 end records. The reader's first part landed next (`diacraft_odg::read`): styles (root, style sheets, local cells), pages and master pages (background pages), layers, the plain shapes (rectangles, ellipses and circles with their kinds, lines, polylines, polygons, paths, regular polygons), frames with text or a picture, text with runs, lists and fields, and the shape index. Custom shapes, connectors, glue, groups and objects are numbered and kept, unmodelled, until a later change. Its cost is bounded for a hostile file. A picture part is shared among the frames that name it. Trees are capped at 4,000,000 items and text at 64 MiB. The model is charged by its real bytes, and every budget at once, with all of the package's 1 GiB parts the read reads, peaks at 2.66 GB, under 3 GB (measured on beast, 2026-11-19, release: the 21 memory cases of the reader's tests each peak under 2.7 GB and each finishes under `ulimit -v 3000000`; the largest also finishes at 2,800,000 and aborts at 2,700,000; a read inflates only the parts the drawing names so parts nothing names cost nothing). A save of that file is refused before it reads (`write_limit`, with nothing scanned or copied): its estimate is 6.51 GB (6.11 GB when an item was 54 bytes) against the cap of 2.9 GB and the process peaks at 2.66 GB, the first read's (`write_memory.rs`, measured on beast on 2026-11-19 on the release build; the case held 720 MB of parts nothing names, which a read does not inflate, until a later change, so it read in 1.98 GB and was estimated at 3.97 GB), and the 31 cases of the writer's memory tests each finish under `ulimit -v 3000000`, every save they make read back by the reader. **The model a read makes is held at 64 bytes an item, counted**: `memory_counted.rs` counts what the model and its index keep for 40 kinds a drawing repeats (shapes of every kind, text, styles, definitions, pages, master pages, layers, faces, package entries, user-defined fields and kept markup) at counts one more than a power of two, and holds what the read says the model holds (its items and its pictures, the engine's hold of the drawing and twice over the save's gate) to the law of the memory tests, the bytes kept, an eighth of them and 24 for each allocation; an item was 54 bytes, set by the growth of the process, and the count found seven kinds the reader charged too few items (a user-defined field needed 105 bytes an item, a package entry 95, an empty page 88, a paragraph with a span 79, a kept hyperlink's wrapper 70, a connector 64.1 and a glued connector 64.3), which are charged a quarter above what they keep now, so that no kind needs more than 64; a save admits 308,000 rectangles of a page in a package and 302,000 in a flat document (about 349,000 and 342,000 at 54 bytes an item), and the engine saves 300,000 where it saved 350,000. The reader charges text at nine eighths of its length (a paragraph of 64 KiB was charged 0.991 of the law and a kept text of 1 MiB 0.894), and DiaCraft's cells by what their sheet reader prices them at (a cell of a formula of 5,000 arguments was charged an eighth of what it keeps); a rectangle holds no text, so the saves above do not move, and a file of kept markup holds 1.4 to 3.0 percent more items. A style's property values are parsed once when the style is read, and the names it holds (a dash, a gradient, a marker, the styles its DiaCraft links name) are looked up once, so a long value costs one read, not one per run, paragraph, list item or shape. Lists are read in bounded work, and a style's text is one shared value in every cell. A shape-style pair compares its cells in constant work: a style's long text is one shared value, told the same or apart without a byte read, and equal tab stops are one shared list. Over the census of sample files the usable packages read with no crash, timeout or memory abort; a few are refused as the package layer refuses them, or at the 20,000,000-item cap, where the refusal names path points. A lighter geometry row is queued so that both read. The reader now reads the rest of a drawing. **Custom shapes:** enhanced geometry with every path command, equations worked out once in dependency order (cycles are errors, found in linear time), modifiers, handles (a handle with a range is kept and not editable), glue points, text areas and mirror flags. `ooxml-` types with no path come from a preset gallery ported from a sibling app; any other type is drawn as its frame's box. **Connectors:** glued by shape and point, with the formulas `connector.add` writes and the route from `svg:d`. **Groups:** with their members. **Objects it cannot draw:** a `Foreign` shape on the frame, drawn from the replacement image and counted by kind. Only hyperlink wrappers (`draw:a`) are still kept whole. Formulas are charged by their parsed size, so a hostile file is refused, not let run out of memory. Over the census of sample files every outcome is as before, and nearly every shape is now modelled: the custom shapes, the connectors and the groups are all read. **The writer is behind the engine**. `file.open` reads an `.odg` and `file.save` and `file.save_as` write one by splicing the edit into the file's own bytes (`docs/editing-commands.md`, ".odg files"); the window's Open filter takes `.odg` (not the default), and the CLI and MCP reach it through the same commands. A drawing is saved as the type it was read as, whatever its file is called. The session keeps the writer's index, so the same drawing saved again is no edit. The engine's new shapes are flattened into a copy for the writer. **Nothing on a new shape is dropped without a word:** the copy carries what the writer writes, and each kind of what it does not write (a line end, rounded corners, a shadow, text formatting, a text block that is not the whole shape, the text of a connector, shape data, the layer, any other cell or section) is a warning of the save, with how many shapes and the first. A shape an earlier save flattened is given again until what it was made from changes. What the engine holds beside the drawing (the undo steps, the pages of the last save, the shapes it flattened, the shapes the copy replaced) is measured, a group is counted by its members, and each copy is charged before it is made; a refusal for memory names the limit and what is held. **The count is an estimate of the model's own types, 2.5 to 4 times the real use, so a save that would fit can be refused:** one whole-drawing edit saves at 200,000 top-level shapes and is refused from about 250,000, and about 100,000 formatted group members in one save are refused (a refusal is clean, never an abort). What the writer cannot write is a refused save in its own words with the way out (undo; a refusal for memory has its own words), and a curved connector is written as cubics (it warned when it was made while the save refused it, and every master of the built-in stencils is written). **Not yet** at (since done: a new `.odg` drawing, the saves across formats and templates, `.otg`; a curved new connector, written as cubics; the text of new shapes, with a style sheet's text formatting still told): a new `.odg` drawing and saving across formats; templates (`.otg`); a new shape in a group; a curved new connector (the spline is not raised to cubic pieces); a turned or flipped new shape; a change of the document's styles (wrote the rest of this list: all thirty of the built-in shapes, a dash, a gradient, a hatch and a change of a page's own cells, which DiaCraft's extension and document file carry); the text, text blocks and formatting of new shapes (told, not written). Each of these is refused at save with the way out and not told when it is made, but the two kinds above. A connector of the file glued to a new shape is refused by the writer itself. **The eight faults the milestone-1 measurement found in the save are fixed**. A part of ODF 1.0, 1.1 or no version gets no `xml:id`, and a connector in it gets no `svg:d` and no `svg:viewBox` (checked against OASIS's schemas of those versions), and a save never raises the version. An earlier fix had glued such an end to the whole frame, and the engine never placed a whole-shape end of an `.odg` connector (the reader's route marker made it take the connector for one of its own, glued by formulas), so the end stayed where the file had it: 16 ends of three files 0.25 inch behind a 0.25 inch move. Both are fixed: an end of an `.odg` connector glued to the whole of a shape is placed when its shape changes, where LibreOffice puts it (below). **2026-11-22:** the full gate's check of the sample files found the rule for an end glued to a shape wrong for `.odg` connectors (9 ends right before a change and 0.27 to 1.42 inch off after, 12 off on both commits, 19 on another side than LibreOffice's): LibreOffice places an end glued to the whole of a shape itself whenever it loads the file, by a rule of its own, so an end of an `.odg` connector glued to the whole of a shape now goes where LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 puts it (`crates/engine/src/draw/odg_glue.rs`, measured as a black box: one of the shape's four standard points, by the best right-angled route for `standard` and `curve`, by distances along the axes for `line` and `lines`), and stays the VSDX rule. Measured: 9,130 of 9,166 standard and curved connectors glued to the whole of two boxes (the rest ties between two squares), 3,318 of 3,318 straight and 3,306 of 3,318 three-line ones, 42 of 42 to turned, elliptic and custom shapes, and, of 32 drawings DiaCraft saved after a move, LibreOffice puts every end where the save states it (worst 0.0000 inch). Not done: a point glued to a glue point that faces away from the shape, which LibreOffice reaches by a route round the shape (one drawing of 66), and the ties between two squares, where LibreOffice's own choice wavers (96 % of 1,875 measured go the way of the glue rule). A turn of a frame of the file glued at its `draw:glue-point` is refused in words, as for a custom shape. The model writer writes a group's `draw:glue-point` elements before its members and a picture's unchanged ones after its picture (LibreOffice drops a frame's point before its content), which a connector names (it dropped the first, with a note, and refused the second). Measured: a shape of each kind that holds a `draw:glue-point` (text and picture frames, rectangles, ellipses, circles, polygons, polylines, paths, regular polygons, custom shapes, groups) moved and resized, and four the file turns moved, each saved: LibreOffice ends 24 of the 26 connectors itself on the moved point (worst 0.0000 inch; it draws no regular polygon) and draws all 26 on the end the save states. Not done: a shape the model writer writes turned or flipped since it was read keeps its `draw:glue-point` elements as read, which LibreOffice places on the turned box (the save into the file refuses such a turn). A way out (`draw:escape-direction`) reads as the page's own direction on a turned or flipped shape, as LibreOffice's right-angled connectors leave such a point. A `draw:transform` whose translation is a plain number (`matrix (a b c d 0 0)`) reads it as hundredths of a millimetre, as LibreOffice does, where the reader dropped the whole transform, and a move writes it back in that form. A new shape whose connection points are not the four side middles (a right triangle) has them written as its geometry's own glue points with its style saying so (`diacraft:glue="own"`), and a new connector to one names it (24 of 32 such connectors ended 0.75 inch from the session's ends in LibreOffice before, none past 0.001 inch now). A turn of a shape of the file that a connector is glued to at a `draw:glue-point`, or at a standard point of a polygon, a polyline or a path, is refused in words, since LibreOffice would place the point anew on the turned box; a resize keeps such points, their rows being sums of the shape's size. Not done: an arc's glue points (LibreOffice puts them on the box of the arc it draws), a polygon whose points start away from its view box's corner (LibreOffice draws it moved to the frame's corner, DiaCraft where the view box puts it), the inverse of LibreOffice's placement for a turned shape, and a new shape's text alignment (a paragraph style that says none is left-aligned by LibreOffice and centred by the session: a new triangle's text is 0.49 inch apart). A page written as an empty element, a `content.xml` with no `office:automatic-styles` and a manifest with the OpenOffice.org document type declaration now save. **The three features that block v0.1 are written**. **Line ends:** a new connector's `BeginArrow`, `EndArrow` and their sizes are `draw:marker-start` and `-end` with a width (the spread times `S` at the weight) and a centre, naming DiaCraft's own `draw:marker` in `office:styles` (a marker the file holds under the name is used as it is, whatever it draws), and so is an edit of an arrow cell of a shape of the file (a style two shapes name is cloned; a weight edit writes the widths of the style's own markers again). **Every number of the line ends is written:** all 45 line ends in every size class 0 to 6 (class 6 at 18 pt) are DiaCraft's own markers, drawn as one path of the union of the mark's pieces, named by number and band class (`DiaCraft_20_end_20_16_20_t23`) with the display name always written, and read back by number and class within 0.1 pt of a class's width (after LibreOffice's 0.001 cm rounding); a marker no style names after a save is taken out; the custom end (254) is written as number 4's marker and its cell (254 and its formula) is carried by DiaCraft's extension, and a number past 45 and a size past 6 have no art, so carries their cells and writes no marker for them (the art is ours, the boxes of 8, 18, 19, 31, 32, 34 to 38, 40, 44 and 45 are DiaCraft's own rule, not measurements). The engine's copy of a new connector now carries its ends. **Retext:** a paragraph is compared by its characters and the cells of its rows, never by the `IX`; a run with a row is a span of an automatic text style (the paragraph's own, else one the part holds with the same attributes, else a new one named by R27), a paragraph's style changes only when its row does, a paragraph is added or taken out (as a list item beside one with its row, and else outside any list; a list's row that no neighbour has makes a `text:list` with an automatic list style), a paragraph the edit did not change keeps its bytes, and a paragraph written again keeps the span of each unchanged run; a font the file does not declare is declared, and a text colour saves; a bookmark, a reference mark, a soft page break and a text field stay in a paragraph written whole, at the offset R10 gives them; what is still refused is a link, a change mark or an index mark, a format that cuts through a field, a paragraph between two items of a list where neither has its row, a list item whose settings change, a text format set where no character is (an empty shape or an empty paragraph), a bullet of more than one character, and a shape whose file holds no `text:p`; paragraphs put in at one place are written in the model's order, and a run's palette colour is written `#rrggbb`; the formatting of a new shape's own text is still told, not written. **A new shape with a reorder** in one save is written, and a new shape on a page whose shapes carry `draw:z-index` (the order the reader will read is checked, and a page whose values repeat is refused in words). The flatten audit follows a style sheet's text rows, a connector glued to a shape an undo put back is refused with a redo as the way out, and the page's connector style warns when it is set. **`.otg` drawing templates are read and saved.** A template reads as a drawing does and says it is a template (`sniff` gives `Template`), and a save keeps the kind the file says it is, with every rule of the splice. `WriteOptions::variant` asks for a kind, and a save that asks for one always states it: the other kind (a template saved as a drawing, a drawing as a template) changes the media type in `mimetype` and in the manifest's root row and nothing else, for an edited model and for an unedited one, and the kind the file already says is written with a `mimetype` first and stored when the file's was not. A file with no root row to state another kind in is refused in words before it is read (`write_media_type`). The comparison counts the data descriptor's bit on an entry a save rewrote as the save's own (16 of the 19 real templates a census run found carry one on their manifest), and a numeric reference of any length in a manifest's path is read as the XML parser reads it. A census run of the 21 real `.otg` files read them and passed. Mutation of the changed lines left no survivor, hand-built fuzz runs of the reader and the writer found nothing (10 minutes each, and 5 more after a change landed), and LibreOffice 26.2.7.3 converts a template saved as a drawing to a PDF (it opens; the picture was not compared). The engine half (opening a template as a new drawing, the built-in templates as `.otg`, the default switch) is a later task's, and what `meta.xml` and `settings.xml` should say of a template is a spec question. `.odg` is not yet the default in the app, and nothing here was checked against what a producer shows. **Flat drawings (`.fodg`) are read and saved.** One XML document is read as the package that holds the same elements (`sniff` tells it by its root within the first 64 KiB, `read` takes any XML file, in UTF-16 too): the first of each child wherever it stands, one container of automatic styles searched before `office:styles` (a name the container holds twice is one warning), inline pictures decoded within the media budget and measured from their characters first (a linked picture is a placeholder, counted in one warning), an inline object kept with its frame and never read as part of the drawing, DiaCraft's extension (the one child in its namespace) as the model's own data, and a declaration of empty Basic libraries is no macro (in a package too). An unedited save of a flat file is its bytes; an edit is spliced into the document as a package's `content.xml` and then `styles.xml` are (DiaCraft's generator in `office:meta`, made as the first child when there is none; new styles in the one container, made before `office:master-styles` when there is none; the extension last), a template's kind is stated on the root, a new picture is written inline, and the line-end markers (the names, widths and upkeep) and the zero-width elements of a paragraph written whole are written as a package save writes them (the same edit saved as a package and as a flat document gives the same markers and the same paragraphs, tested). A package can be written as a flat document (`WriteOptions::form`): its parts become the children, its pictures go inline; what Q8 gives no rule for (the two parts' automatic styles share a name, an object, code, a file nothing places, a flat drawing saved as a package) is refused in words. LibreOffice 26.2.7.3 opens the files with the exact media type and converts them to `.odg` (5 of 5, read back through our reader), and writes no output for a wrong, a missing, a template or another kind's media type (4 of 4). **A flat drawing opens everywhere and is saved as a flat file.** `file.open` tells a flat file by what it is and not by its name, reads it as the package that holds the same elements (the same pages, shapes, looks, texts, glue and picture as a package of the same pieces, tested, and under each kind of edit the engine has), says `form: "flat"` and gives first, among its warnings, what Save does with it; the CLI's `run`, `export` and `mcp` and the window (whose Open dialog lists `.fodg`) reach it through that command. **Save keeps the file's own form:** `file.save` of a drawing that came from a flat file writes a flat document in place, whatever the file is called (unedited it is the file's own bytes and no edit, an edit is spliced into the document and reads back, a second save is no edit, and what the flat writer refuses, as the master a stencil shape brings or a page added, is a refused save in words with the way out of a flat file). **`file.save_as` was not available for it until a later change** (the command's `enabled` said no, with one reason that `engine.commands`, MCP's `enabled_only`, a refusal and the window all gave: the window greyed Save As out from it and showed it as the tooltip and the note; before a change `file.save_as` to an `.odg` name wrote the flat document under that name and called it an `.odg`; it is available now, as the sentence on says); `file.save_as` of any drawing to a `.fodg` is refused as not offered. A drawing opened from a flat `.fodg` is saved by Save As as a `.diacraft` file (which reopens as an equal model), as an `.odg` or an `.otg` package written from the model (a flat file is no package to splice into) and as a VSDX type across the formats; `file.save` still writes the flat file, and the flat file is left as it was by a Save As. It is the way out of what the flat writer refuses (a page added, the master a stencil shape brings): the refusal says so. What a flat file holds in its own form comes with the drawing: the reader of a flat file now holds `office:meta` (the title, the properties, the generator and the other fields) and `office:settings` as it does for a package (both were dropped, so a package made from a flat drawing had DiaCraft's generator and no settings), a picture held inline is written as an entry of `Pictures/` under the writer's own name (it was written as an entry and a link called `office:binary-data`, and two pictures were one name), and the content stamp of a flat file is pending as a package's is.
