The `.odg` is the same content as the flat file by the content check for a drawing the writer writes as read (tested; a flat file of another version, or with no settings part, is the same content since the check follows; a drawing of two pages that share an automatic style is the same content too: the copies the writer gives the second page count as one and DiaCraft's own markup on them counts as nothing). **Open:** the markup of a flat file's containers that no field reads (`office:scripts`, an element of the body or the styles that the reader does not know, the attributes of a container below the root) is not held, so a Save As to `.diacraft` or `.odg` does not carry it and says nothing: the reader of a package holds it by the part it stood in, which the one root of a flat file does not say (the root's own attributes are held, as the roots of both parts) (gated it off for a flat file, which was never saved as another type); a flat file made by LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 (headless, on a copy) saved as an `.odg` differs from it in five children (`office:scripts`, the font declarations, the common styles, the automatic styles and the master styles), where the writer says in its note that it does not write the named definitions and the style families of other kinds, and one with a picture is written too, with the body as a sixth difference; LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 opens both packages and exports each to PDF (headless, on copies, with a profile of its own; measured, inferred from a sample, after a change on main). `roundtrip` of a flat file reads it and writes it again unedited to a `.fodg`, `compare` says its parts are not compared (`flat_not_compared`), and a hostile flat file (a DOCTYPE, UTF-16, another kind of document, a tree past the depth cap, a file cut off) is refused in words by its class with nothing of the file in them. LibreOffice 26.8.1.1 as a black box converts a flat drawing and an edited, saved one (a move, a retext, a restyle, a duplicate, a text box, a taller page) to `.odg`, PDF and `.fodg` (6 of 6), its `.odg` holds each edit, and our reader reads it with no warning. **Not yet:** saving a drawing as a flat file by Save As (has no rule for the automatic styles the two parts share: no Save As writes a `.fodg`, and a drawing opened from a flat file is saved as a flat file by Save alone), a stencil shape dropped into a flat drawing (its master has no place in a flat file, so the save is refused), a list of recent files (the window has none), DiaCraft's document-level data in the extension of a flat drawing (the document file is a package entry, so a flat drawing has no place for the masters, themes, colours and document and page cells: a change of them is refused in words, and only DiaCraft's own data is written as the extension), a frame that keeps a second image whose href a conversion would inline (refused: the kept element would change). **The ShapeSheet XML codec is carved out of the VSDX crate** into `crates/sheetxml` (layer 2), so that the `.odg` format can carry DiaCraft's cells in a namespace of its own: how a sheet, section, row and cell are read and written, the namespace scope, the budgets of cells, items and formula nodes, and the patch of a sheet in a tree. The VSDX reader and writer use it with no change in what they read, write or cost (every test of the crate unchanged; the outputs of every test of the workspace hash the same before and after when a package is hashed by its parts: the bytes of a package differed from one run to the next on the base too, because the order of the zip's entries varied, a fault that predates this task and that has fixed: the VSDX writer listed the relationships parts it made in the order of a hash map, and now lists them by the place of the part each belongs to, so one model saved twice is the same bytes). It also reads a cell given as attributes and says which cells differ from what a reader would rebuild; `odg` uses it. **What ODF cannot hold is carried**. A shape's own automatic style carries, as attributes of DiaCraft's namespace (tier 2), the cells and sections the markup cannot say, the text with its markers, the names, the links to a master and what the reader rebuilds that the model lacks. A new shape is written with them only where its cells differ from what the reader would rebuild, and is read back from the bytes made and compared before the save is made. An edit of a shape of the file keeps what its style carries current and adds what the edit says, so the writer no longer refuses a user cell, a section it does not write, a handle's section, a formula in a style cell, a hatch, a dash, a palette colour, a shadow or a transparency past 1: the extension carries them. One document file, `DiaCraft/document.xml` (listed in the manifest; LibreOffice drops it and the reader says nothing is wrong), holds the masters with their bodies, the themes, the colours, DiaCraft's own data and the document and page cells the markup cannot say. Two shapes that carry one `diacraft:id` (a LibreOffice copy and paste) keep tier 2 for the first and are read from their markup for the rest, and the save names them. Every string a document file puts into the model, every subtree it keeps whole and the file's size are charged to the reader's budget before they are copied, and the tier-2 decodes of a save share one budget. **The document file, version 2:** the masters with their bodies and the sheets of the entries (`docsheet`, `page`, `group`, `style`) are the whole-model codec's (`sheetxml::model`, the vocabulary of `docs/diacraft-format.md` section 3: a shape directly in its master, a kept element as data, the slots an `.odg` and a newer build fill carried too), a picture a master shows is one `blob` element of the file however many shapes show it, shapes nest to the model's 200 levels (it was 90) and the file is read at the container's depth ceiling; a file of version 1 (no release wrote one) is kept as it is and not read, with one note (`diacraft_file_old`), and a save that has something to write makes a new file of version 2; a master the codec finds damaged is left out with a word and no error; a save holds the memory of the trees it is going to make (counted from the models, before the first is made) from the room of its memory gate. **Kept markup:** the reader keeps the markup of a shape's, a group's and a page's element that no field reads (its attributes and the children kept whole) in the `odf` slot of the object, and no longer in the VSDX `raw` and `passthrough`; the splice writer compares and refuses by the slot, a paste, a duplicate and the copy a save flattens carry it (the ids of the file's own among its attributes stay with the original), and the slot is charged to the reader before it is made (a shape's costs two items and a page's three, so a shape that keeps an attribute costs two items more than before). **Every extension slot and every kept part of a text is a figure of the memory gate** (those of the document, pages, masters, styles, sheets, sections, rows, cells, pictures, connections, faces and texts, the document's parts and custom properties, and the VSDX held data), so masters that hold their text in slots are refused by the gate and no longer abort the save, and the loop that makes the masters' trees stops when the file would pass 64 MiB. **The writer asks the reader:** before a document file is written, the file made is read as the reader reads it (the reader's own functions, over the pages and style sheets the markup gives after the save, the masters read, charged and let go one at a time), so a save is refused in words, with nothing written, for a file the reader would refuse: for its items (the reader's items for a drawing, less what the rest of the file takes: the reader now counts what the document file cost it, `ReadWork::docfile_items`, and a package that has no document file yet is counted 64 items for the manifest row that will list it), for more masters than a drawing holds (65,536) or colours (4,096), said before a tree is made, and for more bytes than a part of the package may be (the container's cap, which a caller may lower); its depth, nodes and widest value were held to the parser's limits already, and a test compares the writer with the parser at each. **The document's own data:** the reader keeps in the model what a drawing's package and its parts hold beside the drawing, so that a `.diacraft` file made from an `.odg` loses none of it. **The font faces** are the font table (`Document.fonts`): one face for each distinct `style:font-face` of the two parts' declarations (an equal declaration in the other part is the same face), known by the family it names (quotes off, which is what a run's font cell holds) and held exactly in its `odf` slot, attribute for attribute and child for child; at most 65,536, and one more refuses the drawing in words. **`meta.xml`**: `dc:title` is the title, the fields both formats say are the properties (the description, subject, first keyword, initial creator as the creator, `dc:creator` as who saved it last, the creation, modification and print dates, the language), each `meta:user-defined` is a custom property, and every other field is kept whole where it stood, tied to the number of typed fields before it (a second title, a field with an attribute of its own, the generator, the statistics and a comment among them); a `meta.xml` of another shape, or past the 4 MiB of a small part, is kept as bytes. **`settings.xml`** is kept as a whole tree (as bytes past the 4 MiB cap), the roots of the five XML parts hold their attributes and namespace declarations, `mimetype` its text, and every manifest row is held once: on the entry it names, or by the name it gives. **Every package entry no field reads is a part of the model** (`Document.parts`, files and folders, tagged `.odg`, with its media type, the other attributes of its row and its role: a macro project, a signature, an embedded object, its replacement, the thumbnail, a font), **its bytes the package's own, never a copy**: a picture a frame shows, the XML parts and DiaCraft's own document file are not parts. **Cost.** Each value is charged to the read's items before it is made (a face 3 items and its declaration's own, a property and a row 1 to 3, a settings tree 3 items an element), and an entry's bytes to the media budget (`Limits::max_media_bytes`) twice over: by the package, from the size its directory states, before the entry is inflated, and when the part is made. An entry that does not fit refuses the read (`xml_too_complex`, "bytes of package entries kept") and none is left out; a damaged one is left out and said (`part_not_kept`). **The reader inflates every file now** (it inflated only the files a frame names, so the census's embedded fonts cost nothing); the largest entry is held twice for an instant while the package makes its bytes shared, and the model holds one copy (measured under `ulimit -v 3000000`: a `settings.xml` of 64 MiB and a font of 64 MiB each peak at about 141,000 KiB and leave 68,000 KiB held, one copy of 65,536 KiB). On a drawing a LibreOffice-like file makes, the change is about 3,000 items more of the 20,000,000 (0.015 percent: the settings tree is most of it) and the bytes of its thumbnail and other non-picture entries against the media budget; no sample drawing was measured. A flat drawing keeps its faces alone. **The package data follows the file where a model writer needs it**: after a splice save the model still holds the package data of the file as it was read (the save's own `meta:generator`, the renewed thumbnail and the rows that name what it adds are in the new file only), and it cannot be made to follow at the save (an undo would bring the old data back from an earlier version of the drawing, and a read of the whole file after every save would cost what the save does again), so `diacraft_odg::read_package_data` reads the file the drawing stands on as it is now and `PackageData::put` puts it on the model a `.diacraft` is written from (`save_native`, which a drawing opened from an `.odg` reaches once Save As `.diacraft` is lifted for it), whatever version of the drawing that is; an entry that is the same bytes keeps its allocation. A file that cannot be read again beside the drawing refuses the save in words. **A save writes one field of `meta.xml`, the generator, and it is the save's own and not the drawing's** (it says DiaCraft wrote the file, so a `.diacraft` made after a save carries `DiaCraft/` and no longer the producer's); the creation and modification dates, the editing cycles and duration, the statistics, the title and the user-defined fields keep their bytes, so the modification date is not renewed by a save. The named definitions and the other data the save writes into `styles.xml` and `content.xml` are not package data and are a later task's. **Not built here:** the held values, the definitions, the layer set, the names and units, the page layouts, the other style families and the text's paragraphs, and the lift of the refusal of Save As `.diacraft` for a drawing opened from an `.odg` (below; F30). A master or a sheet the codec finds past a cap of the model (shapes nested over 200 deep, a kept element nested over the codec's depth) is left out with a word and is no refusal of the drawing; a master with no declaration of the namespace of its own is given one in place, when it is reached and charged; the root says the version that is written, and a save that replaces a file of an older version says so (`document_file_replaced`). **The held values and the definitions:** the reader fills the `odf` slots with what the cells can only approximate and what no cell reads, each value charged before it is made. **The document's:** the named definitions of `office:styles` (markers, gradients, opacities, hatches, dashes and fill images, as written and in file order; a fill image's bytes are the package's own, and its entry is then no part of `Document.parts`; at most 65,536 of a family, and a file with more is refused), the layer set, and every style element that is no style sheet, kept whole by container (`common`, or `auto` for the automatic styles of either part, so that a flat drawing reads as the package that holds the same elements), family and name; **an automatic style** of the graphic, paragraph and text families is kept as what no cell reads (its attributes, the properties' attributes and children no cell reads) and not at all when it holds none, since a style of its own for each shape would otherwise be charged twice; **a style sheet's own slot** holds its names and family, the properties of its elements that no cell reads (keyed by the element) and the lengths written in `px`; **a page** holds its master page's name and attributes, its layout's name and the layout's properties no cell reads, its drawing-page style, the layout's units and the stamp of its cells. **A shape** holds the name of its automatic style, its units (a length no cell spells in its unit: `px`, or another unit than the cell that takes it) and the held values of R22 from its own automatic style and its own element (the fill, the foreign dash, the line join, the foreign markers and the width of one of DiaCraft's own, the shadow, the text's justify, padding, writing mode and tab stops; the frame's transform, a circle's centre form and the layer; the connector's route and type, a rectangle's radii, a plain shape's own element, a polygon's view box, a path's data, and the enhanced geometry's tie to the `Geometry` sections and the `ODFEq` rows), **each stamped by the list for that value** and not the group's (`stamp_all` joins the stamp of cells and of sections), a style's values made once and shared by every shape that names it; a glue point's attributes sit on its `Connection` row, stamped by the row whole; a picture's `draw:image` attributes; **a text** holds one paragraph for each `text:p` of the file with the style it named, the stamp of its row and the values of its style, a run for each `text:span`, and a bookmark, reference mark or page break as a zero-width `OdfRaw` part that the splice writer leaves out. A held value is **copied, not lifted** out of the shape's kept markup (the splice writer reads it there and an unedited save is the file's bytes), and a writer or a comparison ignores the `.odg` data of a style's or a text's slot. **What it costs** (measured, `tests/it/held_measure.rs`, items per shape before and after): a plain styled rectangle 80.2 to 82.2, a line 145.6 to 147.6, a custom shape with text 316.1 to 323.2, a turned rectangle 84.2 to 87.3, a rectangle with an automatic style of its own 91.2 to 94.2 (and the save's estimate 1 to 3 percent more), a plain rectangle with no style three items more, so 350,000 of them no longer fit the 20 million items (330,000 do); it is not won back. **Not built:** the held values of a common style (a style sheet holds what no cell reads, and not the properties its cells only approximate: a hatch fill, a foreign dash or marker, a shadow), `text:h` and the markup of a list, and every use of a held value by a model writer. The engine follows: all thirty of the original stencil masters and the page's connector style are saved, and the warnings for them are gone (the twenty masters of the new stencils were behind a gate until a later change, which saves them). **Not yet:** the style sheets' cells and `NameU`s, a group's rename and a shape of the file put into a group or a group of the file taken apart (refused in words: saves new groups and carries a group's data and `ID` in the document file, found by the group's name, which is unique on its page; an entry with no `nth` is read for a group only while no other group of the page has the name, and a name another program shares leaves it unread, with a warning; a copy of a group that holds an object is refused as a copy of an object is, for the members of a new group are looked at as the shapes of the page are), the slot that keeps another format's own data in a drawing, and the fingerprints of tier 3. The writer still refuses a glue made anew on a connector of the file and a new shape in a group of the file. A new picture of a flat drawing carries no tier 2: a cell of it that no style attribute says is refused in words. **The fixes of the four full reviews:** a line end on a line whose weight is more than a million points (13,888 inches) is refused in words in an edit of a line the file holds and in a weight edit of a line that names a marker of ours, and in a new line its end is carried with no marker and its cells in tier 2, as carries an end the table has no art for (a width of `inf` or of hundreds of digits, and a band class that was no number, were written before), and a size cell that is no class is refused there and not taken for 2; the 1,024-byte bound on a marker's path is on the markers we draw, not on one the file holds under our name. A package written as a flat document refuses, in words, a part whose root has a second child of one name, a child that is foreign or belongs to another part, or an attribute other than a namespace declaration and `office:version` (all three were dropped without a word), and an image that holds inline data beside an `xlink:href` that names a picture of the package; a `draw:fill-image` is given no `draw:mime-type`. A retext holds the tokens of a paragraph (a token for each piece and four bytes for each character room) to the memory a save may hold while it plans the paragraph, and gives them back: a paragraph that does not fit is a `write_limit`; a paragraph of 1,300,000 bookmarks (the most the reader's item cap admits), 600,000 paragraphs that each hold one and 50,000 changed fields save under `ulimit -v 3000000` in child processes (peak 0.83 GB at the most, measured on beast on 2026-11-19 on the release build); the notes of fields written as plain text are one for each kind of field in a shape (42 at most), not one for each field; a range that is not in the text, or a tag that is not text, is a fault (`write_mismatch`) and not a paragraph with no element in it. The renderer reports a size class outside 0 to 6 only for a number it draws a mark for. A soft page break between paragraphs is an anchor at the start of the paragraph after it and follows the block mapping (items 1, 3 and 5) as a bookmark does. **Known limit (owner ruling):** the upkeep of R28 stays narrow: a marker of our name that no style names, which the save did not orphan, stays in `office:styles` until a save orphans it. **The review's fixes:** a formula set on a pose cell with the value the same is an edit and is carried by the extension (it was dropped, with the save reporting none); a formula set on the end of a connector whose glue is as it was is refused in words (a connector's ends are the glue's); a shape that was read as a copy of another (a LibreOffice paste) and is edited gets an `ID` and data of its own and no longer repeats the first shape's; the tier 2 an edit writes is read back as a new shape's is; an entry of the document file that names a page cell is ignored; the document file's page and document-sheet entries are charged by the sections they walk, and the reader holds at most 256 themes. **A new picture in a package:** a new picture shape (a foreign shape that holds a PNG, JPEG, GIF, BMP or EMF, told by its bytes) is saved as a `draw:frame` whose `draw:image` names a new entry `Pictures/image<n>.<ext>` and a row of the manifest with its media type; the name is the writer's own (never the model's part name), the same bytes are one entry (a hash, then a comparison of the bytes, each charged), an entry the file holds is never reused, and the entries are put in before the thumbnail with the rows the manifest had left as they were. What a frame's markup cannot say (the `Img` cells, a layer, a link to a master, an outline the reader gives a frame none of, a section of its own, a style cell with a formula, a custom line end) is tier 2 as a new custom shape's is, the rebuild of a picture frame being predicted as the reader gives it (no outline, the four standard connection rows) and every cell and section of the model read back in a property test (400 cases); a flat drawing's new picture carries tier 2 by the same rule (it refused those cells before). A turned new picture is written as a turned custom shape is (`draw:transform`, no tier 2; a flipped one is refused in words). A connector is glued to the four points a frame has and a fifth is refused in words. A picture of another format (WMF, SVG, TIFF) is refused in words, and so is one past a cap the reader holds a package to (one entry of 256 MiB, 50,000 entries, 1 GiB over the package, the manifest's rows and bytes) or that deflates past the ratio the package layer reads; the memory of the deflated copy and the archive is taken from the save's room before either is made (a 256 MiB picture saved 0.80 GB resident, three of 250 MB 1.72 GB, four refused as a limit, each under `ulimit -v 3000000` in child processes, measured on beast on 2026-11-19 on the release build: `picture_memory.rs`). Ten thousand pictures in one save, and a package that holds ten thousand, cost in proportion to the pictures and the entries (counted). **Not yet:** a VSDX picture saved as an `.odg` was proved here on a model shaped as the VSDX reader gives one and not through the engine (the engine's VSDX to `.odg` route came); a copy of a picture frame the file holds is written again as an entry of its own; a picture frame deleted leaves its `Pictures/` entry in the package (an object's entries go with it, a plain picture's do not: names only the object's); a package whose `content.xml` or `styles.xml` is in UTF-16 refuses a new picture in words (a save of such a package is refused by the check of the bytes made anyway). **The review:** every `xlink:href` of both XML parts is reserved, in the two spellings the reader tries (percent-decoded and then stripped of `./`, and as written), before any picture is named and whichever part the save changes, so a new picture never shows in a frame or a fill that named its name before (it did in four cases: a master page's frame, a fill image, an untouched `content.xml` beside a picture on a master page, and `%2E/`); a copy of a picture is charged the hash of its bytes to the scan and the comparison to the copies it saves (10,000 copies of one 64 KiB picture are saved, where they were refused at the scan's 1 GiB); a manifest the save made past its row cap is a limit in words; the reader and the writer judge a picture by one table (`crates/odg/src/picturetype.rs`); an arrow size beside no end (a size alone, a size beside the end 0) is tier 2 on every new shape, where it was counted as written and dropped without a word. A new picture is **refused in words** when the file holds a broken picture link: an `xlink:href` of a `draw:image`, `draw:fill-image`, `draw:stroke-image`, `style:background-image` or `text:list-level-style-image` that is a path in the package and names no entry of it, in `content.xml` or `styles.xml` and in any spelling the reader resolves (as written, with `./`, percent-encoded). LibreOffice 26.8.1.1 does not open a drawing that holds such a link beside a picture the package holds, whether DiaCraft saved it or it was made by hand (measured: a missing name, a plain name, `./Object 1` and the encoded forms fail; an IRI with a scheme, a path from the root or out of the package, an empty href and a link at `ObjectReplacements/` open), and the old behaviour (the new picture took the link's name and showed in the broken frame) was a silent wrong picture. The words name the broken link, LibreOffice, and the way out: remove or repair the broken frame, save, then add the picture (a broken frame the same save deletes is still the file's). A file with a broken link and no new picture still saves, and a hyperlink or an object's href is no picture link: the reservation of the names every href uses stays as the guard for those, for the entries and for the manifest's rows. **Pages in the writer:** a page the model adds is written as a new `draw:page` with its new shapes, naming the master page a page of its layout size has or a copy of one made as R13 copies for a resize; a page it takes away is taken out with its shapes, the styles and the package files only they named (the master pages stay); a page it puts in another place is moved whole, with the edits made inside it in the same save; a page it renames has its `draw:name` written, and a page with no name that moves is given the one the reader gave it; the pages are matched with the file's by `ID` (the index records each entry's), the document file's page entries follow the names and places, and a model of more pages than the reader reads (1,000) is refused before the file is read. **Not written:** a background page added or taken away, a page put after a background page, two pages under one name where the save wrote one of the names, a new page that holds markup or a relationship, and any page change in a flat drawing (refused in words). **Review fixes:** the name of a page is compared with the name the file reads it under, so a rename taken back in a later save and a page renamed after the save that added it are written (and saved again are no edit); a page of the largest `ID` is saved like any other; a page added reads the document's `Layer` rows; a flat drawing's reorder is refused in the words of a page put in another place; a page taken away leaves its master page and layout, **a project rule that goes against the specification**. Measured, by the tests of `first_save.rs` and `read_object_labels.rs` in `crates/odg/tests/it` and of `placeholders.rs` in `crates/render/tests/it`; seen in the window: the first save of a drawing that has no file yet (a new drawing, or one made from a template) writes the picture of its first page as the thumbnail though nothing was changed, **once a new drawing can be saved as an `.odg`: on main no command makes a drawing that has no file and an `.odg` package, so the writer's `first_save` and the engine's save of such a drawing are built and run by tests that make one, and the window and the command line do not reach them yet** (`WriteOptions::first_save` and `first.rs`: a package with none gains it with its manifest row and no other byte changes; one that has a thumbnail keeps it, by V13; a save that is not a first one and is no edit is the file's bytes; a flat drawing has none to write, by V31); an object DiaCraft cannot draw and has no picture of (a table, an embedded object, a 3-D scene, a dimension line, a callout, a form control, fontwork) is read as a `Foreign` shape with a `User` row `ODFObject` that names it in the words of the format's rule, and is drawn as its frame's outline, a pale fill, a cross and its name on a plate, never mirrored, one finding in the frame (`SkipKind::ObjectPlaceholder`, "N objects DiaCraft cannot draw, shown as labelled boxes"); a read says once for the document how many custom shapes of a kind it does not know are drawn as boxes (`unknown_shape_type`); the line end finding says a size was estimated and not that another kind of end was drawn. **Not yet:** a label for an extruded shape other than the nearest (3-D scene), for a chart other than an embedded object, and for a comment or a media object (the reader makes none); a copy of an object is refused in words, with a replacement picture or without, and so is one an undo brought back after a save deleted it (owner ruling B1: the object's element and files are not copied yet; a copy of a plain picture still saves), and a new EMF picture is typed `image/emf` (RFC 7903, owner ruling C2) while a row a file gives as `image/x-emf` is kept as it is. A fix round: a copy of a text frame whose text box holds the frame of an object, as a child of the text box or in a paragraph, or one an undo brought back, is refused in the same words, since the model keeps nothing of a frame in a paragraph (the reader gives the shape a `User` row `ODFTextObject`, which a copy carries); the look for an object is charged to the flattening's steps (a shape with sections pays a step for each). Measured, by the tests of `first_save.rs` and `write_new.rs` in `crates/odg/tests/it`, of `placeholders.rs` in `crates/render/tests/it` and of `odgfile/tests.rs` in the engine: the labels of placeholders have a layout budget of their own (`Limits::max_label_chars`, `Limits::label_time`) beside the file's text, so that a page of tens of thousands of objects still draws its text (27,000 objects of fifteen letters took it); **a copy of an object DiaCraft cannot draw, or one an undo brought back after a save deleted it, is refused in words when the drawing is saved as an `.odg`** until the object's element and files are copied, since the save could only write a plain shape that draws nothing; an edited save writes no second manifest row for a thumbnail the manifest lists already; a first save whose picture cannot be made says that no thumbnail was written; a part whose `office:automatic-styles` is written as an empty element (`<office:automatic-styles/>`) takes a new shape's styles in one edit and no longer refuses the save as "two edits change the same bytes". The thumbnail an `.odg` save draws is held to a budget of work and to no time: the same drawing saves to the same bytes however loaded the machine is. A trip through `.diacraft` writes the shapes the second session has no file for with the lengths they resolve to: until a later change the copy a save flattens rounded each to nine places of an inch, so 2cm came back 2.0000000005cm, and a change the gate planted on such a value did not raise the content check's count, since one place is one difference however much it differs. A field on a master page keeps the text the file saved through an edit, and a field a refresh changed keeps its element in a save over the file. | | The reader, the writer and the default switch **(2026-11-16): what a new `.odg` drawing could not save is saved.** `theme.apply`: the theme part and the page's scheme cells were carried; the `Theme` style sheet is an empty common style at the end of `office:styles` with its sheet in a `style` child of the document file, a shape that follows it names it as the `style:parent-style-name` of its automatic style, and the themes a file carries are read when the file is opened (so `theme.list`, a second `theme.apply` and `theme.variant` answer as in a session that never saved). `snap.set`: the four Snap and Glue words are a `words` child of the document file. `guide.add`: a new shape that is turned or flipped is written (`rotate` in radians, then `translate`, and the mirrors of the geometry, odg-shapes), and a guide is a shape of no size whose style says `diacraft:type="Guide"` and gives it no fill and no line, so that LibreOffice draws nothing. The masters a file carries are kept by every save (the copy of a save took every master out, so a file of three held none after one edit); the masters a drop brings are carried too: each is written into the document file, the shape made from it keeps its link, and a file read again has them. **Still not saved:** a shape held to a guide (a new one is saved with a warning that the hold is lost, one of the file is refused in words), a change of a shape's links to different style sheets, a style sheet of the file changed, a flipped new picture (a frame has no mirror the reader knows; a turned one is written). **2026-11-16:** in a file that has a default graphic style (a common style named `standard`, as LibreOffice writes) the `Theme` style takes it as its parent, in the model and in the file, so LibreOffice draws a shape that follows the theme with the drawing's face and not a serif of its own (in a file with none, a hand-made one or a new drawing's blank package, the style has no parent and the shapes that follow it are drawn with LibreOffice's own defaults); a theme part is named `theme<n>.xml`, so that no part, entry or string of a saved `.odg` names a VSDX package's path (the theme's own DrawingML bytes still declare the format's `vt` namespace, whose URI holds the product's name, in the base64 of the document file: a question for the owner); and a shape dropped, drawn or made after `theme.apply` keeps its three links to the `Theme` style in the file (its automatic style names `Theme` as its parent), so it follows `theme.variant` after a reopen, **and LibreOffice then draws it from the empty `Theme` style's chain, with no theme colours, as it draws the file's own shapes that follow the theme** (before, the new shape carried the theme's colours as constants and followed no variant). A save that adds a line end's marker and a style sheet to a styles part whose `office:styles` is an empty element was refused (two edits of the same bytes) and is made. **The review is answered:** a style sheet of the file is named by the name its own markup gives it, so a parent never names the `NameU` the document file restored (`My Theme` for `My_20_Theme`), a second style of that name is refused, and the links of each shape whose parent was written are read back (a parent that names no style was saved and read back on the root, with no word); an undo of a saved `theme.apply` is saved by taking the empty common style, its document-file entry, the theme part and the shapes' parents out of the file (it was refused, and only a redo saved); a `Theme` style another program's save left empty (it keeps the empty style and drops the document file) is filled in by `theme.apply` and its cells are carried by the document file; the command line's export opens an `.odg` as `file.open` does (`odgfile::read_odg`), a theme that cannot be read is a warning of the open, and a name's characters are the XML 1.0 ones (`ª`, `µ`, `²`, `½` are escaped). **Still not saved:** a shape whose three links are not all the `Theme` style (said in the save); a change of a cell of a style sheet whose markup holds a property (only the cells of an empty common style are carried: telling which cells of a style have a property of ODF is a task of its own); LibreOffice saves a guide's frame as 0.001 cm, so a guide a LibreOffice save has touched moves by 0.0002 in on the next open (it stays a guide and draws nothing), and it drops the Snap and Glue words. **Tier 3, LibreOffice edits win; with the fix round after its review:** beside each tier-2 item that rests on a native output (a text, a name, a link to a master, a `Geometry` section, eleven style cells) the writer puts the fingerprint of that output (`diacraft:fp-<item>`, 16 hexadecimal digits of FNV-1a), and the reader leaves an item out when its output changed in another program (an edited path, a retyped text, a rename, a colour or a line end set elsewhere; the markup then stands), keeps it through a move, a resize and every rewrite a LibreOffice save makes with no edit, and gives a carried pin the markup's left edge plus the pin's carried place, by the turn and the flips the markup has (a move in LibreOffice is kept and the pin stays in the shape; a carried turn that is the markup's, a formula `30 deg` say, is no reason to leave the pin alone, and a carried pin is compared by the place worked out with the markup's size). **A save keeps the fingerprints current:** it takes one out only where it rewrites the output, and stamps the one an item lacks from the file's own outputs (a name given in DiaCraft to a shape of a file, so that a rename in LibreOffice is told; any item of a file) on every save that writes that shape's extension; the fingerprint of a custom shape's outline is made again from the path an outline edit writes. A link to a master that went stale keeps the shape's copies of the master's shape data and user rows where the document file still holds the master. Measured on LibreOffice 26.8.1 through UNO: 21 scenarios, each as the rules say (a move, a resize and every save with no edit keep every item; a retyped text, a rename, a colour, a line end set to another marker and an edited path are told; a name DiaCraft gave is told renamed; a linked shape whose outline DiaCraft edited is told stale by LibreOffice's path edit; the pin of a turned shape follows a move of 5 cm). **Known limits of tier 3:** an item that an edit adds to a shape whose style carried no item of its kind (a link, a geometry section, a text, a colour or a line cell; the name is stamped for every shape), a shape DiaCraft has not edited since a save, a cell the save rewrites natively and an outline written as a polygon's points, a path's `d` or modifiers have no fingerprint until a later save writes that shape's extension, so an edit of another program in between is not told; a carried `s-Control` (a handle moved with no modifier following) has no fingerprint of its own either, so a change of `draw:modifiers` in another program leaves the carried handle position over the one the markup gives until a later save writes the extension again, and a resize in between works the modifier out from the old position (the reader's own handle cells are shares of the frame, so a resize alone no longer rewrites the modifiers; the handle's drag, once a command writes one, must write the cell as a share of the frame too); themes against `loext` theme colours and style-sheet cells have nothing to compare (no native output on main); after a LibreOffice save the document file is gone, and a stale link has no master to copy shape data from (the shape keeps what it holds, and the read says so once); whole-shape fingerprints for groups are not written; a rotation in LibreOffice of a shape whose `Angle` is carried as tier 2 is not told (sizes and the ends of a line are judged, angles are not, and the pin is derived only while the carried turn is the markup's); and a text that holds a field whose shown text another program refreshes on a save with no edit is told stale (of the probe do not cover it). **A new shape whose cells disagree is refused in words:** a shape whose pin is not the centre of the frame its size and the place of its pin give (a width set and the place, half of it, not calculated again: a model no command makes) would be read back with the centre as its pin, because the reader keeps a carried pin only where it puts the frame's edge where the markup has it (tier 3); the writer says so before the file is made, and not by the check of the bytes (`WORDS_PIN_DISAGREES`). **The splice writer keeps and drops what R8 says:** a fill picture that is dropped takes every `draw:fill-image-*` attribute and `style:repeat` with it; a connector routed anew loses its `draw:line-skew` with the old route **in every version of ODF**, since the skew goes with a path written anew or left out (the skew row), and **a connector no edit touched keeps its path and its skew** though a later rebuild of line jumps wrote its rows again in the engine's spelling (a hop one edit made and the next took away, a point on a leg dropped): the save compares the corners of the route on the page with those of **the route the file holds** and not with the baseline's rows, writes the route anew only when they moved, and says that a hop in the model, which an `.odg` has no place for, is not written, keeping the file's path **and its corners in the index, in the page as that save leaves it** (the next save, once the hop is gone, compares the model's route with them and writes it when it is not the file's: the review's Repro 1 and 2; **a change of the page's height keeps `PinY`, so every shape and every connector end moves down the page by the change, and a connector no edit touched keeps its own path through it, shifted by that change** (the first absolute `M` of the form DiaCraft and LibreOffice write moves and its relative steps do not, in a path of absolute points every point moves, `svg:viewBox` stays as it was and `draw:line-skew` is kept, so a program that works the legs out from the type and the skew draws them where the file had them; a route with a hop that is kept is shifted too, and said once, in the save that kept the path for it, and **not named again** at the saves after it that change the height (a connector whose path has two subpaths, which reads as a route with a second `MoveTo`, is no hop and is shifted and never said); a path the reader cannot read (it breaks SVG's syntax) is kept as it is and said, and one the reader reads that begins with no move has `M 0 <shift>` put in front of it so that it moves exactly; a number that moves is written to four places, or to the places it had when it had more (up to ten), so that a degenerate arc stays one; the writer reads a path with the reader's own tokenizer and is held to a step for each byte of it; the text a shift makes is charged to the memory a save may hold before it is made, as an upper bound, and what is kept is charged as it is written, escaped; a connector with no `svg:d` has its ends moved and none made; a connector an edit touched is routed anew as before, and a change of width moves nothing; the corners the index keeps are the same whatever the height, since the path goes with the page); a new connector glued to a shape whose `xml:id` or `draw:id` an earlier shape of the page holds, or whose id is empty (the reader reads `draw:end-shape=""` as no shape), is refused in words, since the file would read the glue as that shape's or as none, and no spec says what to write for one (a question open); a shape's id is written by the name the reader gives it (a literal tab or line break in it is a space), so the end finds the shape again; a connector routed anew in a part of ODF 1.0 or 1.1 (or one that declares no version) is written as its ends alone, since those versions have no place for a path or a view box on a connector (its skew goes as it does in every version), and the save says so in a note that names the connector; the room a save has for the styles it copies is the lesser of the reader's two caps on a tree (`max_read_xml_nodes` and `container.xml.max_nodes`), as the reader's own is; a path edited has its `svg:viewBox` written with its `svg:d`, as the file's own box written again as four numbers and spaces with its left and top kept (`10,20,1000,1000` comes back `10 20 1000 1000`, and a box whose numbers that spelling would change is left as the file has it), which is not the `0 0 W H` of unless the box's left and top are 0 (the path row), while a polygon's edit keeps its view box as spelled; a new shape is written on the layer its `LayerMember` names (`layout` only when it is on none), by the layer set of the markup the reader resolves a `draw:layer` against (the page's own, else the document's) and only where that row has the name the model's row has, so that a layer renamed by an earlier save, or the rows of a page the save adds, never leave the shape on another layer or none when the file is opened again (the cell is then carried); an edit of a paragraph's or a run's cells writes only the attributes of the cells that changed, into the style the file has, where it stands when nothing else names it, else into a copy of it, so what no cell maps and each value whose own cells are unchanged (a held underline's style, colour and width while `Style` bit 4 and `DblUnderline` are, `lowercase`, a percentage position, `start` and `end`, a line spacing, the tab stops and their leaders) stay as the file has them. **A style is edited where it stands when nothing else names it** (no style is renamed) **and copied when another paragraph or run does**, and a copy holds the whole style, so its items are charged before it is made against what the reader reads of one part: a retext that would make a file DiaCraft then refuses to open (thousands of runs of one style, each given a row of its own) is refused in words, and the copy is named by R27 and never by the name of a common style. **Two cases still rebuild the text style from its cells and lose its kept values:** a paragraph that names a common style or none, and a run that does not lie wholly in one span naming an automatic style of the part (a run across two spans or inside two, or in a span naming a common style); a later fix, for 0.2. The place of the pin edited as a constant (`LocPinX`, `LocPinY`), with the pin compensating, is carried as tier 2 and no longer lost on reopening, but a group's, which no entry of the document file carries, is refused in words (a group's frame is the box of its members). The reference app was not inspected, and the output has not been opened in it by the project. **What a `.diacraft` must not lose:** the rest is held, so that nothing an `.odg` holds is lost by Save As `.diacraft`: a common style holds the values its cells only approximate (a hatch, a gradient or a picture for a fill, a dash or a marker the drawing does not define, a shadow, the text area's justification, padding and writing mode, and, for a paragraph or text style, the alignment spelled `start`, the line spacing, an underline's, a line-through's and an overline's colour and style, a lowercase transform, a position in percent and the tab stops with their leaders) with the stamps of its own cells, and a value no cell can show is kept as written; an automatic graphic or paragraph style keeps its text values and an inexact tab stop element in its remainder; the document's containers keep their attributes (among the roots, named `<part>/<element>`) and the children no field reads (`office:scripts`, a child of the body, the drawing, the master styles or a layer set, a master page's own layer set and, when it holds no shape, its children), and a page keeps its own layer set when its rows do not say all of it; a text keeps its heading's element, a paragraph's own attributes, the lists round a paragraph (each held by the paragraph that opens it), each hyperlink, field and span with an attribute of its own as a run with its element, and, for a text with no characters, its paragraphs whole with the shape's markup. **The named data follows the file** where a `.diacraft` is written, as the package data does: the definitions, the layer set, the other style elements, the font table and the containers' markup are read from the file on disk, since a splice save writes markers, page layouts, styles and font faces of its own into it. **Measured first:** each construct was read from an `.odg` and sent through the whole-model codec (`held_trip.rs`), and nineteen of twenty were lost before the task and none is now. **Limits:** a list or an item that holds no paragraph is not held, a comment inside a paragraph's text is dropped (as before), a flat drawing holds none of the containers' markup but its root's attributes (so a Save As of it as `.diacraft` or `.odg` does not carry it, as above; the root's attributes), the tab stops of a common style are held whole with no stamp (R22 writes them as held on every save), and an automatic graphic or paragraph style's text values are held with the style and not with a stamp (there is no paragraph or run to stamp them by). **Cost:** a child of a container that nobody reads is 13 items alone and 3 inside an element that holds it with the others, so a file of 3.9 million filler nodes in a `styles.xml` and a `content.xml` costs about 12 million items held as two elements, and the reader's every-budget case was retuned (49,000 corners in each of two polygons, the filler in one element each); a list item and a hyperlink cost the same each, linearly (`held_trip_data.rs`). **The codec:** `held` and `held-style` in a style, `element` and `level` in a paragraph, `element` in a run (3.15, 3.18 of `docs/diacraft-format.md`), and the table of what the model costs gives them a figure (`PARAGRAPH` 272, `RUN` 160, the four new kinds 96, each measured). **Not built here:** a model writer of an `.odg` that reads these values, and the VSDX save across, which must take the model through `with_file_package_data` as `save_native` does. **The `.odg` model writer, at package and document level (`diacraft_odg::write_with(doc, None, ..)`).** A drawing with no file is written from the model alone (`crates/odg/src/write/model/`), in three steps: the plan reads what the model asks (the first page that is not a background sizes the package, the kind is the one asked or the model's own `mimetype`, else a drawing), `crates/odg/src/base.rs` (`compose_base`, harvested from the shelved template package) makes the package and the model's `meta.xml` (the title, the properties, the user-defined fields, the fields kept whole at their places, the generator), `settings.xml` (the held tree), manifest (the held attributes of its rows) and package entries (`Basic/`, `Scripts/`, the folders and files of objects and their replacement pictures, `Configurations2/`, fonts, signatures, any other, each with a row) are put in it, with `office:scripts` and the other pieces of the containers that no field reads back in `content.xml` and `styles.xml` at their places and the attributes the model holds for each root; the splice writer then writes the pages and shapes onto that package as new ones, so what it writes and refuses for a new shape is what this path writes and refuses (and -3 close those). `WriteError::NoOriginal` is gone (`SaveNote::SignatureStale` stays: a signature is kept and the save always says it no longer matches). **`WriteOptions::content_unchanged`**, as VSDX's: true, `meta.xml`'s generator and the thumbnail are the model's, as read; false, they are DiaCraft's generator and the thumbnail passed, as an edited save writes them. No clock is read, so the dates are the model's. **Every part says the version the package says, 1.4**, whatever the file the model came from said: a root row of 1.3 over parts of 1.4 is a package LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 refuses to load (measured). **Refused in words, and never left out:** the parts of a VSDX file but its thumbnail, the parts of a newer DiaCraft, a document property ODF has no field for (category, company, manager, the base of hyperlinks), the markup and the package data of a VSDX document that no field reads (an `.odg` carries them in DiaCraft's own folder, which writes), a drawing with no page or a first page with no size, an entry name no package can have, a part named as one the writer makes, more parts than a package holds or rows than a manifest may, and a settings tree or fields of `meta.xml` past what the reader reads of a small part or nested deeper than 512; a model whose parts would pass five times over the memory a save may hold is refused before a byte is made. **Said in a note** (`SaveNote::DocumentDataUnwritten`): the named definitions, the layers beyond the five the package has, the style elements of the other families and the pieces of the containers a part has no place for (writes them with the held values). **Measured, on the engine's own drawings** (`odg_model_measure.rs`, 65 drawings: a rectangle in six forms, a text box, two connectors, a group, a second page, a page of another size, and every master of every built-in stencil), **before: 65 of 65 refused** (`write_no_original`); **after: 63 written** (3 plain, 59 with the note `outline_approximated`, 1 group with `group_frame_not_kept`) **and 2 refused, in words** (a new line glued to a shape, and a new connector whose route is not a path the writer writes). **On drawings an `.odg` reader made** (`model_measure.rs`, 24 hand-written packages of one construct each), before: 24 of 24 refused; after: 22 written (15 with `outline_approximated`, one object with `new_shape_unwritten`, all with `document_data_unwritten`) and 2 refused (a second page of a file holds the markup of its element: writes the held names). **LibreOffice 26.8.2.2** (headless conversion, own profile, `lo_model_write`): 11 of 11 files written from the model open (a drawing with shapes, an empty one, a template, one with every field, a macro entry, an object, a configurations entry, and the model of a file read back), and the control, a package whose manifest omits `meta.xml`, does not. The 100,000 entries of one container, the 100,000 attributes of one container and 20,000 package entries are written in one pass each (counted in `model_hostile.rs`); four children under `ulimit -v 3000000` refuse or write parts of 800, 570, 400 and 200 MB without an abort (`model_memory.rs`). **Not built:** the writing of the held values, names and units, text, the settings' own items (scale, snap and grid) and the engine's use of the writer. The VSDX application was not inspected, and nothing of ours was opened in it. **Shapes and held graphic properties on the model writer.** The two refusals left are closed: a new 1-D shape that is glued to a shape is a connector (`connector.add` writes neither route cell), and a connector whose route is curved (the engine's quadratic `NURBSTo` rows) is written as the cubics it is, with its own rows carried; **no drawing of the engine's 65 and no vector of the 24 is refused now** (65 written: 3 plain and 62 with `outline_approximated`, one of them with `group_frame_not_kept`; 24 written: 8 plain, 15 with `outline_approximated`, one with `document_data_unwritten`). **Pages and document data:** a page the `.odg` reader made is written with its held master page (`draw:name`, the attributes and children the reader kept, and each master page it names with the attributes it had), and the model's layer set, named definitions (but a fill image), other style families (page layouts, list styles, common text styles, defaults) and font faces are put into `styles.xml` and `content.xml` of the package the splice writes onto (`write/model/document.rs`), so `document_data_unwritten` is now said only for a fill image, the shapes' own automatic styles that no shape took up, and pieces of the containers a part has no place for. **Held values:** the held fill (a hatch, a gradient or a picture, and the name of an opacity gradient), a foreign dash and join, a foreign line end and the width of one DiaCraft drew, a shadow, and the text area's alignment, padding and writing mode are written into the shape's automatic style while their stamps match the cells (`write/held.rs`), and are not once a cell changed; an undo makes them good again. **What a shape keeps of its file is written back on the model path** (its attributes and its children kept whole, an object's element among them), and a custom shape's own `draw:enhanced-geometry` is written while its stamp matches, in place of the placeholder rectangle. (the security scan's three findings on that task), kept markup that XML or its namespaces cannot hold is never written: a kept name that is no qualified name, or a value with a character XML 1.0 does not allow, is refused in words; a kept prefix is written only where it stands for the namespace in force where the pages are (the root's and the containers' declarations the model holds, `write/kept.rs`), declared again on the markup where the place binds it otherwise, and markup whose prefix nothing there binds (one only a sibling's kept element declares, or one that no element declares: the one the file bound on the shape's own element is kept with the markup that uses it) is left out and counted in `new_shape_unwritten`, as a document piece whose prefix nothing binds where it goes is in `document_data_unwritten`; a kept attribute the writer writes itself under another prefix is not written a second time; and a page's kept children, its attributes and a master page's are taken from the 16 MiB cap of the document's data by their size, and charged so, before they are copied. A declaration a page, a group or a shape made on its own start tag is kept with the markup that uses it (the block of that task, below), so a name under one that rebinds a prefix of the root is written in the namespace it was read in. **A VSDX OLE object** is written as `draw:object-ole` with its class, then its replacement picture, with the entries `Object N` and `ObjectReplacements/Object N` and their rows (`write/model/objects.rs`); an object that is not carried is refused in words, and a replacement DiaCraft does not draw (TIFF) is not carried. **The engine's own losses** (`odgfile/flatten.rs`): the copy a save hands the writer carries what a new shape holds (rounded corners, a shadow, a moved text block, `Property` and `User` rows, the layer, any other cell or section, the text whole), so the warnings of those kinds are gone; two kinds remain and are said (the formatting a style sheet gives the text, and the link to the Theme style of a shape whose three links are not that style); of the four silent drops, the page's `DrawingSizeType` is carried by the model writer and still dropped in a save into a file (a flat drawing has no document file to carry it), the label cells of a connector with no text stay dropped (carried, they made the second save of a drawing an edit), a guide's fill and line are unchanged, and the masters a drop brings are written into the document file; `stencil_gate` was closed. **The `.odg` model writer writes a shape as it was read** while the stamps of its held values hold. It writes the element the file had (`draw:rect`, `draw:ellipse`, `draw:circle`, `draw:regular-polygon`, `draw:polygon`, `draw:polyline`, `draw:path`, a frame that shows a text box, `draw:line`, `draw:connector`, `draw:custom-shape`, a picture or object frame; a plain shape whose outline was edited is a custom shape again), the style name the file gave (one style for the shapes that name it; a shape whose style cells were edited takes a style of its own, under a name no shape holds, and has the file's name again when the cells come back; a shape that named none and has nothing for one gets none), its lengths in the units the file wrote them in (the unit its cell shows, else the unit the shape held, else the page's), its frame as written while the frame is the one it has (a moved shape has the last translation moved, a resized or turned one is written from the cells), its layer and `draw:text-style-name` only where the file had them, no empty `text:p` the file did not have, and a connector's path and `draw:type` while the route's rows, the ends and the route kind are as read. A held value whose stamp holds says the cells it stands beside, so a colour (the reader makes them in capitals), a dashed line's `LinePattern` (`draw:stroke="dash"` is now a held value, stamped by it) and the six cells of a shadow (its stamp covers all of them) are no tier 2, and the attributes of the style's graphic properties that no cell reads (`draw:auto-grow-height`, `draw:textarea-horizontal-align`) are written with it from the kept style of that name. **A second model save is the first's bytes** for the 24 vectors (the default `settings.xml` is the text a read gives back; and a shape whose outline DiaCraft's data carried holds no value of the element's outline, since the second save of such a shape read the placeholder outline as its own and lost the outline). The window settings the model holds are written as the `windows` child of DiaCraft's document file, and no longer refused. **Of the 24 vectors 23 have the file's content in `content.xml` and `styles.xml`** (0 before, with 2 to 13 differences each); the one that stays holds a run style (bold text in a span: the text writer gives the run's style every attribute its cells say, and the file's said the weight). A shape that holds a name or a value the writer does not write (an attribute name that is no qualified name of ODF's namespaces, a value XML cannot hold) is written as a new shape, and the save counts what it held that was not written. **Not built:** what an automatic style kept beside its graphic properties' attributes in the writer's namespaces (other properties elements and attributes in other namespaces: counted in the note), a plain shape's glue points (carried in DiaCraft's data), a shape whose links name different style sheets (`diacraft:links` and `diacraft:filled`), and the master pages no page names. LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 opens 25 of 25 files made so (the 24 vectors and an object) and refuses the control; the VSDX application was not inspected, and nothing of ours was opened in it. **Text.** The model writer writes a shape's text from the model. Each paragraph is a `text:p` (a `text:h` where the file's was one); each run of a `Character` row that writes an attribute is a `text:span` of an automatic text style; a `Paragraph` row that writes one names an automatic paragraph style; a bulleted row is a `text:list` with an automatic list style, and **a list style the part holds with the same content is that style** (a file DiaCraft wrote holds the one it made as the document's data, and a save that made it again added a second each time); white space is `text:s`, `text:tab` and `text:line-break`. **A carriage return is a paragraph break** (`CR LF` is one), and the exact text is carried in DiaCraft's data. The page number, page count, file name and date or time fields (`PAGENUMBER()`, `PAGECOUNT()`, `FILENAME()`, `NOW()`) are `text:page-number`, `text:page-count`, `text:file-name` and `text:date` (or the `text:time` the file had), as the reader reads them; any other field (a page name, a shape data field, a cell's value) is its shown text, and the save says so (`field_as_text`). **The names, held values, lists, headings, links and marks the reader held are written back as read while the stamp of the row each rests on still holds:** a model paragraph is the file's paragraph when the stamp of its characters is the one the file's had, its style name, its held values (an alignment spelled `start`, an underline's colour), its lists (a numbered one, a nested one, a list's own attributes), its `text:h` and the elements round its runs are then written as they were, a new style never takes a name a file's paragraph held, and from the cells (a new name) when the stamp does not hold. **A text the file can say whole carries none of itself in DiaCraft's data** (the text and its `Paragraph`, `Character` and `Field` rows are what the reader gives back, each row confirmed by a held stamp); one it cannot (a carriage return, a tab stop marker, a row with a cell no attribute holds, a formula or a font that is a number the document's table does not name, a row no held stamp confirms (a text DiaCraft made), a field it cannot hold) is carried as before, and the file shows it all the same. A list is at most 256 levels and as deep as the files the reader reads; a held element that crosses another is not written, and the save says so (`text_elements_not_written`). **Every paragraph, run, list level, field and tag is charged before it is made:** a field finds its row by a map made once for the shape, the colour and font tables are indexed once for the save, the sweep that opens and closes a paragraph's elements is linear (a stack, a count of the elements that hold text as it stands), a held element that crosses another or is nested deeper than the files the reader reads allow is not written, and the matching of a model paragraph to a paragraph of the file skips a candidate with a run past the paragraph's end, charges the runs it hashes and is held to eight times the paragraphs, the held runs and the characters / 64 (50,000 fields, 10,000 elements that cross, 10,000 nested to the depth limit and one past it, 100,000 held runs beside one paragraph and beside 20,000 short ones, 100,000 runs naming faces in a table of 10,000, 100,000 one-letter runs and a paragraph of 10 MB, each beside a plain yardstick of the same size, timed by `#[ignore]` tests: what the security scan named was quadratic or uncharged before, and a paragraph of 100,000 held runs beside 20,000 short ones took 49 s against 0.04 s). **The splice writer no longer refuses a text with a page number, page count or file name field** (its shown text is among the paragraph's characters and the element stays, as any field's). Measured on 17 texts of every kind: before 3 refused (two whose held elements the carried text cannot hold, and a carriage return) and none said by the file; after none refused and 11 said by the file; the engine's 65 drawings and the 24 vectors stay 65 and 24 written. LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 opens 10 of 10 vectors as written and 5 of 5 DiaCraft-made texts, reads the same paragraphs, runs, alignment and list levels from 9 of 10 as from the originals (the tenth shows a file name and a clock) and refuses the control; the VSDX application was not inspected, and nothing of ours was opened in it. **Not closed:** the splice path still refuses, in words, an edit of a text that holds a carriage return, a tab stop marker or a cell no attribute holds (it has no place in an edited shape's data for a text's rows); the text formatting a style sheet gives (the common style a style sheet becomes holds graphic properties only: its `Character` and `Paragraph` rows have no place there yet, and the engine's flatten still says it); a held element that crosses another or is nested deeper than the reader reads, and the elements of a paragraph of more than a million held runs (all said as `text_elements_not_written`). **The `.odg` trip through `.diacraft` ships safe (the owner, 2026-11-22: "Ship safe, finish in 0.3").** The model writer writes a bitmap fill's picture: the `draw:fill-image` definition, its `Pictures/` entry under the name it was read from (a name the package cannot give it is `Pictures/fill<n>`, and two definitions of one picture share the entry) and its manifest row (found the pictures of 89 of 93 visits lost with no word). It writes **the style sheets an `.odg` reader made as the common styles they were** (`write/model/sheets.rs`: names, family, parent, the graphic, paragraph and text properties of the cells, the held values whose stamps hold, the properties no cell reads and the children), in the package the splice reads, so that DiaCraft's document file carries only what that markup cannot say (they were written empty, their cells in the document file alone, in 703 of 719 visits: another program drew every shape that named one without the look it gave). A picture on a style sheet is written with the style sheet as its parent (it was refused: 279 visits), a held bitmap fill keeps its repeat mode, and a paragraph's or a run's automatic style keeps what no cell reads (the sizes of the Asian and complex scripts were dropped with no word). What is written is what the ODF 1.4 schema takes (OASIS's RelaxNG through `xmllint`): a drawing with no settings gets a `settings.xml` whose root holds nothing (an empty `office:settings` is refused), a shape's title, description, event listeners and glue points come before its text (a frame's after its content), a handle says `draw:handle-position-x` and `-y` beside the deprecated `draw:handle-position`, and the first shape of a page comes after the page's forms. The content check reads `office:version` counts as nothing; an empty `settings.xml` or `meta.xml`, and its manifest row, as absent, and the reader the VSDX data of a group with no name whose stamp more than one such group of the page has is left out with a note. **The content check follows (second round):** DiaCraft's own carrier (the entries under `DiaCraft/`, their manifest rows, and the attributes and elements of DiaCraft's namespace) counts as nothing when only the written file has it and what the source held is compared as written, so the check is oriented and the first file is the source; copies of an automatic style count as one; a reference from one automatic style to another (a list style, a data style, the text style of a list level, a number format) is resolved to the content, a circle and a chain of more than 64 are compared as written, and `style:parent-style-name` never is (§19.510); and the table of references follows the ODF 1.4 schema (the notes page and the handout master ask a drawing-page style; a hyperlink's visited style, a conditional paragraph, a section, a ruby and the lists of class names added; the data styles and `number:num-list-format` are definitions). **It follows:** `manifest:version` counts as nothing with `office:version`; the generator of `meta.xml` and the order of its fields, the manifest's row order and its rows for an entry only the writer's package holds or a plain folder that files stand under, and the names of automatic styles (matched by content, a common style's name kept, a style whose property changed one difference in that style) are the same content; an empty `office:settings` under a flat root is none; a layer set added where the source had none, `meta:user-defined` fields added, `dc:creator` or `meta:initial-creator` taken out, `meta:creation-date` added, `meta:print-date` taken out and DiaCraft's own markup still count. A group with no name whose data would need an entry found by its place is refused in words that name it. Measured: the engine's 65 drawings and the 24 hand-built vectors are all written; LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 draws the bitmap fills written (a shape's, a style sheet's, two shapes') and the style sheets pixel for pixel as it draws the sources. **The losses an evidence run found:** a style that names its gradient, hatch or picture and says no `draw:fill` of its own (LibreOffice's `Filled Blue`), or names one beside `draw:fill="solid"`, keeps the name and says no kind of its own after a visit (it was written `solid` with the name gone), and a line end of another program's marker named alone is given no width the cells work out; the crossing to VSDX refuses, in the visit's words, a picture the reader does not draw (an SVG, the metafile LibreOffice writes for a table's or an object's replacement), by the `.odg` writer's own rule (`diacraft_odg::model_refusal`); the rows of a text that ride without the text are stamped with the formatting the markup says (`fp-format`), so a bold turned on or off in LibreOffice is read back, and a mark that is off (`Case`, an underline, a line through, an overline, a position at 0) is no cell of that stamp, so a LibreOffice save with no edit changes no shape's formatting; the manifest lists `DiaCraft/` whenever the save writes an entry of DiaCraft's, the document file alone included (LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 dropped `DiaCraft/document.xml` when the manifest listed the entry and not its folder); a shape's style cell with a formula is written with its value and the formula rides beside it, and a custom shape's rows ride beside its path when the reader would not give them back (`Width*0`, `Width*1`). **A finding of the full gate:** the model writer labels every part 1.4 and writes the style sheets natively, so a style of a file labelled 1.0 or 1.1 (whose schemas take any attribute and child of a properties element) wrote four forms ODF 1.4 refuses into a valid source's visit; each is now written in 1.4's form, `text:enable-numbering` left out, a `text:list-style` with no name named `DiaCraft_20_list_20_<n>`, a negative `text:min-label-width` at 0 in its unit, a font size of 0 (a style's or a run's) at a tenth of a point, and the source's form rides in DiaCraft's data (the style's entry of the document file, `older` children; the `Character` rows), so a DiaCraft read gives it back; the save card says it in the values group. A source with none of the forms is written byte for byte as before. Hostile input: a style of more than 4,096 such records is refused in words, each walk is charged to the save's steps before it is made, and the reader keeps at most 4,096 records a style, an item each, and gives them back by maps in a walk it charges. Measured: the drawing and its five variants and our own six validate against the ODF 1.4 RelaxNG schema after a visit (all but the control were invalid before), read back with the source's values, and LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 opens each. **For 0.3:** the same-content trip (a bold style writes every attribute of its group, a text whose paragraph style holds text properties rides in DiaCraft's data with its size on a span, a shape's held glue values are not stamped again after a trip, a default layer set is added where the file had none, the manifest's version), and the refusals counted that remain in words (a tier-2 read-back, a view box, page markup, a drawing with no page, a picture format, a style made twice, a prefix binding). The VSDX application was not inspected, and nothing of ours was opened in it. **The check after a save:** after a save through a model writer (the VSDX writer, the `.odg` writer, and the `.diacraft` writer for a drawing of another format) the engine reads the file back as an Open reads it and compares it with the drawing, class by class, and the save result's `checked` field says each class that differs with the count of its items (`pages`, `shapes`, `shape-numbers`, `geometry`, `text`, `text-formatting`, `pictures`, `objects`, `macro-project`, `fonts`, `line-ends`, `gradients`, `style-content`, `style-sheets`, `masters`, `layers`, `connections`, `formulas`, `hyperlinks`, `fields`, `user-cells`, `shape-attributes`, `properties` and `kept-data`), and the save card says it in words after the note's sentences; the same content in the forms a format holds (a unit, `Width*1` for `Width`, a value a style sheet gives, a link to a style sheet) is one line, "written as values the file format holds", and a formula that does not come back as a formula is counted as lost even when its value does; a save into the drawing's own file is not checked in 0.2, and a check that cannot finish says "the file was written; DiaCraft could not check it" and why; the Compatibility Checker before a save stays 0.3's. Measured (EVID2): the `.odg` trip through `.diacraft` differed in 1,096 of 1,191 trips by value and a crossing in most, and the warnings named none of a lost picture, frame image, paragraph, table, object, font, master page, shape kind, geometry or formula count, which the check now names. **The read-time drops the check cannot see** (GATE.ea42: font declarations in 104 files of the plain visit, style properties in 423, master pages and layouts in 27, element kinds in 14) are kept or named at the open. A master page that holds no shape is kept whole and written back with its children, and its drawing-page style in `styles.xml`; a style's second spelling of a font family and the type of a line that is off are kept beside their cells. The open says the rest, a line for each class with its count, first among its warnings: the shapes of kinds kept only as placeholders (a dimension line, a callout, a form control, a 3-D scene), which a copy writes as plain shapes, the shapes in hyperlinks that a copy writes over the shapes that were over them, the font declarations that repeat a name, the default tab distances, and a flat file's markup that no part of the drawing holds; a save into the file keeps them all. Not reproduced on hand-built vectors: a font declaration lost (every one comes back, a used or an unused one, though in both parts where the file had it in one). **The check on the corpora:** the `.odg` model writer writes a connector that another connector is glued to with the id that end names (it wrote none, and the glue was lost); a drawing object a paragraph holds (a picture anchored as a character) is held whole where it stands and written back whole (it was written as an empty frame with its picture and its own paragraph after it, inside the paragraph); a VSDX file written for a drawing of another format keeps its creation date; the check judges a glued end's formulas as its connection, a connection between the shapes paired, the cells, rows and sections the file adds, and the marks and objects of a text, and `explain` traces every class. Not reproduced from the classes alone, and asked for as vectors: a line end's width lost, shape counts and geometry that differ unnamed, and what `file.open` gives beyond the VSDX reader. Found and not fixed: a trailing space LibreOffice writes at the end of a paragraph (drops it). **The gate's vectors:** a save from the model keeps the members of a group that holds no attribute of its own as they were read (they were flattened with the group, and their styles lost their parent and with it `standard`'s fill colour, line colour and shadow; the check saw the same content, which DiaCraft's own data carried); the reader holds a line end's width and centre flag beside an empty marker name (they were held by nothing, and the end read its parent's width); the paragraphs a picture frame's `draw:image` holds are kept whole on the picture and written back inside it, through `.diacraft` and VSDX, and the check counts them in `text` (they are not the shape's text, and DiaCraft does not draw them); and the check pairs the rows and sections of one key in order, a deleted one with none, so that a VSDX file with two `Paragraph` rows of one `IX`, two `Paragraph` sections, or a deleted `Geometry` row of the number a row with no `IX` reads as, written back as it was, names nothing.
