What `file.open` gives beyond the VSDX reader was the check's pairing: the reader, the `.diacraft` file and the VSDX writer keep both rows and both sections, in order. **A save after a Save As to `.odg`, with no edit, is no edit.** A VSDX drawing saved as an `.odg` was refused on its next `file.save` ("a change of the document's own fields (a property)": the version of the program a VSDX file names, which no `.odg` holds, is no longer compared), and then written again whole (the model writer's index held its own copy of the pages, not the drawing's); a drawing made from a built-in template was refused ("(the elements of its root)": every save into the file now leaves DiaCraft's own skeleton out, as the first did); a page given a size lost its `DrawingSizeType` on the next save (a save into a package keeps it now; a flat file still leaves it out); and what the Save As was asked to leave out (`drop_macros`, `drop_objects`) went back into the file or was refused, and stays out now. **What a Save As leaves out stays out of every save into the file it wrote, in every pair of formats, and the drawing keeps it.** A VSDX file written from the model with `drop_objects` or `drop_macros` (from an `.odg`, a `.diacraft` drawing or a new one) was refused on its next Save ("a change to the picture of a shape", or "this file holds a macro project" for the project of another format in a type that holds none). Now the drawing becomes what the file reads as with what was left out put back on it (the shapes that showed an object of an `.odg`, the macro parts, the files of objects: `across/dropped.rs`), the file remembers what it was asked to leave out (`OpenFile::asked`), and each save into it is given the drawing as the writer was given it (the objects as their pictures, the macro parts out of a type that holds none) and compared with the baseline made the same way, never with the whole drawing: a save with no edit is no edit and the same bytes, and an edit is written with what was left out still out. A Save As from an `.odg` to another `.odg` took neither option; it now leaves out what the file carries of another format (a VBA project, VSDX objects that ride in it) by writing the file from the model, as a Save As of a VSDX file to an `.odg` is, so a drawing the `.odg` model writer refuses (one that holds the style sheets of a VSDX file) is refused in words with nothing written; a Save As to a macro-enabled type keeps the macro project, as it did, and one that leaves out nothing the drawing holds is still a splice. A `.diacraft` saved afterwards from an `.odg` that left something out holds it still (the package data put on the drawing was the file's, which lacks it). **Two saves that found and left open are closed.** An object of a VSDX file saved as an `.odg` with the object left in (with or without `drop_macros`) was written a second time, in DiaCraft's own folder, by the next save, which counted as an edit though nothing was edited: the model writer writes a carried object as an object frame and takes the VSDX embedding (and the relationship that named it) out of what rides, and a save into that file was given the embedding as riding. The file now remembers it (`LeftOut::carried_objects`), and each save into it is given the drawing as the file holds it (`diacraft_odg::without_carried_objects`, which makes the model writer's own walk): the Save is no edit with the same bytes, and an edit leaves the object in the file once. A `.diacraft` drawing that holds an `.odg`'s object frames, saved as a VSDX type with nothing left out, was refused on its next Save, because only a drawing read from an `.odg` adopted the model the file reads as: the gate is now the working file (`across::adopts_by_working_file`): a drawing read from an `.odg` always, a `.diacraft` drawing, or one with no working file (a new drawing, or one made from a template), when the writer was given pictures an `.odg` held, and a VSDX drawing never, so no blank drawing's save changes (a new drawing holds no picture an `.odg` held). **A page sheet's sections are compared by their names** (from the check of the sample files of a full gate): a VSDX file whose page sheet has a `User` section before its `Layer` section, saved as `.odg`, answered `edited: true` to an unedited Save with the bytes the Save As wrote, because the reader gives the `Layer` section back first and the entries of DiaCraft's document file were compared in the order each model has them; the comparison now takes a sheet's sections in the order of their names (two sections of one key keep their order), and a save that writes the file keeps the model's order. **The two cases found and left are closed.** A VSDX file written from an `.odg` or `.diacraft` drawing that holds an object frame (carried back as an embedding) held the object twice after an *edit*, in the embedding and in the `Object N` that rode in DiaCraft's part (an unedited Save was right; 12 chains of the edit grid): the drawing keeps the part the embedding was written from, and the splice writer, which judges what rides by comparing the drawing with the file's baseline (which holds that part too), rewrites DiaCraft's part from the drawing on any edit and put the object's file in it. The VSDX writer now says which parts it wrote as embeddings (`diacraft_vsdx::Written::natively`), the file remembers them (`OpenFile::natively`, kept through every save into it), and each save into it is given the drawing and its baseline without them (`across::natively::without_parts`): the save with no edit is no edit with the same bytes, an edit is written, and the file holds the object once, as an embedding. The drawing keeps the parts, so a `.diacraft` saved from it still holds the object. A drawing made from an `.otg` template that holds `.odg` frames (it has no working file) was refused on its next Save after a Save As to a VSDX type, by the same cause as the `.diacraft` drawing: the gate (`across::adopts_by_working_file`) now takes a drawing with no working file when the writer was given pictures an `.odg` held, so a blank drawing's first and second saves are unchanged in every type (measured for a new drawing, a drawing made from an `.otg` of rectangles and one made from a `.vstx`, in each of the types a Save As writes). Found and not fixed: a picture inserted from a file into a new drawing, saved as a VSDX type, is refused on its next Save ("a change to the picture of a shape": the drawing holds the picture as inserted and the file reads it back with a relationship; it holds no `.odg` data, so the gate does not take it; measured on the unchanged tree for a new drawing in the six VSDX types, not for a `.diacraft` drawing); and a VSDX splice stamps `Application` on every save, which a file another program wrote shows after a model save. **The four `.odg` findings of the gate on `76278d7e`, each a vector of the gate's composed set.** **A group's own `draw:glue-point` elements are what the markup gives on the model path:** the `Connection` rows of a group (the four standard ones, then the rows of its own glue points that are unchanged since they were read, each with its element held under the row's stamp) are no data of the group, so the model writer makes no entry of DiaCraft's document file for them; the group below a group that holds `draw:id`, which leaves as it was read and so with no name, was refused for an entry found only by its place (the two files of the gate that finished before and were refused at the second leg). **The runs of a paragraph end where its characters do:** a last run that closed after the white space the reader takes off the end of a paragraph ended one character past the text, the writer passed by the paragraph it was read from, and wrote it with none of its hyperlinks (4 in, 2 out). **The runs the file had inside a stretch of one row are written as they were:** the model holds three runs of one style as one stretch, no span of the file covered it, so the text rode in DiaCraft's data whole and the paragraph read back lost the markup's runs (its hyperlinks among them) for the next save; a stretch the file's spans tile, each with the row's stamp (or, for a span that holds only a hyperlink and so has no row of its own, the empty row's, over the characters of the element), is written as those spans, each under its name. **A carried `Connection` section keeps the element of each glue point it still is:** a custom shape whose geometry lists glue points and that has one of its own has its section carried, which replaced the rows the markup gave and with them the element each held (53 of the 152 `connections` alarms of the gate that mean the same); a carried row that is the row its element gives (by the stamp the element holds) holds it again, a row that was moved holds none. **The check tells a paragraph's runs by the characters they cover:** runs that are elements (a hyperlink, a field, a span with an attribute) are looked for in the file at the same characters, in order, and a hyperlink the file lacks is counted in `hyperlinks`; a plain span is the values it holds, told over the characters it covers by a sweep that is charged for each stretch before the values are compared (the security scan's finding on the first version), so runs that the writer joined or split are no change, and a held value that does not come back over its characters is. Not done: the other 99 `connections` alarms of the gate were not traced; the writer still carries a custom shape's whole `Connection` section when it holds the geometry's listed rows beside rows of its own (it cannot tell by the markup that the listed rows are unchanged). **The two losses of the gate on `29a46bbf`:** **A connector end's glue index stays what the file had it** (the alarm was right): a custom shape of a file whose `draw:enhanced-geometry` lists glue points has the four standard rows and the listed ones from row 4, and is written with the geometry element the file held, which keeps the file's glue ids; the connector glued to it was numbered as if the writer had written the shape's rows as its own points (row *r* is id *r* + 4, the second case), so an end on id 4 came back 8, 5 came back 9 and a standard point 2 came back 6, and each visit moved it 4 more. The writer now takes what a connector's target keeps of its file's markup before the page is written, so that a connector that stands before its shape is numbered as the shape is written, and a shape that writes its file's geometry has the numbering its rows say: four standard rows and the listed ones from row 4 are the reader's numbering (glue id *g* is row *g*), and the listed ones from row 0 are the style's `diacraft:glue="own"`, which the model writer now says again (a visit left the marker out, so the connector read back on another row, and the check named it). **A flat drawing's in-text links are kept** (all 8 flat files of the gate's sample files that hold links lost them, the package form kept them): the root of a flat file is the root of both parts and its namespace declarations were not held, so the package made from the model bound no prefix for a link's `xlink:href` (nor for an attribute of another vocabulary on a geometry element) and the model writer left the markup out without a word; the reader now holds the root's attributes but `office:mimetype` as the roots of `content.xml` and `styles.xml`. Not done: the template's user fields lost on a resave, and the markup of a flat file's other containers, which is still not held. **A later fix:** **Kept markup whose prefix an element below the root declares comes back** (the plain visit left out, with no word of the save, a link whose `xlink` prefix the paragraph, the shape or the page declared, an element of another vocabulary in a paragraph, and a span's, list's or field's attribute of one, and a shape's own attribute and child of one; the check named them `hyperlinks`, `text-formatting` or `kept-data`; and a name under a prefix a shape rebinds was written in the root's namespace): the model holds the declarations of the root and of the containers down to the one that holds the pages (a flat file: its root), and no declaration a page, a shape, a paragraph or a span makes on its own start tag, so a name used under one was in no namespace the writer knew. The reader now gives each element it keeps whole (a shape's children, a page's, a mark or an object in a paragraph), each text element it keeps (a link, a field, a span or paragraph with an attribute of its own, a list, an item) and each properties element of an automatic style it keeps the declaration of every prefix it uses that an element above it made below the held ones (put first among its attributes), and a shape's kept attributes the declaration of their prefixes beside them (`xmlns:p` among them), so each is written where its prefix means what it did (XML Namespaces 1.0); a declaration that binds what the model's own bind is not added, and each one added is charged as items and bytes before it is made. **What cannot be written is said, never skipped:** an element of a text whose prefix nothing binds (one the file left undeclared) is left out, or a paragraph, span, list or field is written without its attributes, and the save says how many in the note `text_markup_unbound` (before this task it left them out with no word; `text_elements_not_written` is the note of the elements that cross another); a shape's own geometry element that cannot be written (its outline is then made again from the rows) is counted with the shape's other unwritten elements in `new_shape_unwritten`; and an attribute of the properties of an automatic text or paragraph style that is in a namespace of its own (a producer's `loext:` or `officeooo:`) or in none, which the model writer dropped with no word of the save and with no class of the check, is written again on the properties element it was read from, with its declaration, and one that cannot be (no namespace to put it in, or a prefix the part has for another namespace) is counted in the note `style_attributes_not_written`. Not done: a style whose properties hold nothing a cell reads is not written when the run or paragraph that names it has no row of its own to name it by, the attributes of the style element itself and the children of its properties elements that no cell reads were not looked into, the marks of a text that a save into a file copies into a new shape (its text there is plain characters, as before), and the markup of a flat file's other containers, which is still not held. **A sweep of saves** ran random chains of saves in one session (from a new drawing, an `.otg` and a `.vstx` template, and `.vsdx`, `.vsdm`, `.odg`, `.fodg` and `.diacraft` files holding plain shapes, a group, a glued connector, text with a link, a picture, an embedded object, a macro project of each language and user-defined fields; through every type a Save As writes, with and without `drop_objects` and `drop_macros`, with moves, texts, pictures and deletes between; `crates/engine/tests/it/resave_sweep.rs`) until they found no new class, and closed the classes it found, the picture inserted into a new drawing saved as a VSDX type and the template with user-defined fields among them (`docs/editing-commands.md`, names each). Not done: a flat `.fodg` saved in place refuses a master or a picture the session added, in its writer's words; an `.odg` keeps no link in a paragraph it edits; a glued connector whose other end's shape was deleted is read back from an `.otg` at another place than the drawing has it; an embedded object new to a page of an existing VSDX file is refused in words. **The two regressions of the gate on `44f9d1ae`:** **A frame of two images keeps both** (13 files and 22 pictures of the gate's sample files lost them on the plain visit of a flat file, and the check named `pictures`: the alarm was right): the reader shows the first picture it draws and keeps the frame's other images whole, so an SVG or a PDF among the kept children is another image of the frame; the model writer took any kept image for the frame's own picture as soon as the markup that binds its prefixes could be written (made `xlink` and `loext` writable), wrote no picture of its own and dropped the PNG (the package form lost it on a visit too, and so did a frame whose first image was an SVG, a PDF or another PNG: the old class of `fodg-frame-svg-then-png`, `-pdf-then-png` and `fodg-frame-two-inline`). A kept image is now the frame's own picture only when its link names an entry of the model that holds the picture's bytes (a carried object's replacement), and a picture frame writes its own picture among the kept images where it stood in the file: the reader gives a kept child of a frame `after` 0 when it stood before the child the frame shows and 1 when it stood after it. **A link stays a link:** a flat drawing's relative link (`plan.svg`, `Pictures/p1.svg`) is a file beside the document, and the same text in a package names an entry the package has not (ODF resolves an IRI with no `../` inside the package; LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 said a load error for two of the sample files and for the vector with a bare link, and opens the package made now for that vector: headless conversion to PDF of a copy): a kept image whose link is a path in the package that the package written has no entry for is written as a link out of it (`../plan.svg`). **An extruded custom shape that lists glue points gets no marker the file did not have** (the sample file `1b96a5515890d1c7`; the check named `connections` 4 and `formulas` 6, and a connector end on id 4 was written 8 and came back 4 on the next visit): the reader stops at an extrusion, a fontwork or a view box with no width or height before it reads the glue points the geometry lists, so the shape has the four standard rows and the listed points are no rows of it; the rule of the rows no `draw:glue-point` made are as many as the geometry lists counted them as if they were and wrote `diacraft:glue="own"`, and now counts the points the reader makes rows of (`reads_no_glue_points`), the same for the box with no width that had the same bug. Built in code from the vectors' structure (`crates/engine/tests/it/picture_frame_images_and_extruded_glue.rs`, with a check without LibreOffice that every name of an entry the content refers to is one) and at the crate's level (`crates/odg/tests/it/frame_images_and_glue_rows.rs`); red on `44f9d1ae`. **Found by the fuzz run of this task, present on `44f9d1ae`:** the check of the styles a save made looked each up by name alone, so a style of another family with the name of a graphic style the save made (a presentation style `gr1` beside the graphic `gr1` of a frame) refused the save as its own fault (the model writer's `a style the save made is not there once`); each is now looked up by name and family, as ODF's names are unique within a family (`frame_images_and_glue_rows.rs`, four families). Not done: a link the writer made a link out of the package differs from the file's text, so the check names `kept-data` (1) for it; a kept image's link is asked of the package only when the save adds a picture of the model's (a frame with a kept link and no picture of its own is written as it was); and a file with `diacraft:glue="own"` on an extruded shape (written by `44f9d1ae` only) is read as the marker says, its rows from 0, and none are listed. **GATE.b94e.F1 (the two regressions of the gate on `b94eb83b`):** **A `.diacraft` file written before the id moved keeps its preview** (see F30; the cause was the document part's bytes, which hold the earlier namespace where a write puts the current one, and not a comparison of the namespace itself: every other place that names DiaCraft's namespace was read, and each either writes the current one by design or already takes both). **An attribute of ODF 1.0 is no longer written where ODF 1.2 and 1.4 have none** (15 of the gate's `.odg` outputs held `text:enable-numbering` on the paragraph properties of an automatic style, which began to keep, and 9 of them failed the schema of the version they say; LibreOffice loaded them): an attribute of a namespace of the standard's own, other than `fo` and `style`, on the properties of an automatic text or paragraph style is written in a part of ODF 1.2 or later only where the schemas give it there (`text:number-lines`, `text:line-number` and `text:relative-tab-stop-position` on a paragraph's, `text:display` and `text:condition` on a text's); `text:enable-numbering`, which no later version has, rides in the attribute `diacraft:older-enable-numbering` of the same element and is read back as the attribute (as a style sheet's forms of an earlier version ride in the document file), and any other such attribute is left out and counted in `style_attributes_not_written`; the model writer, the only writer of this remainder, labels every part 1.4 (a part of ODF 1.0 or 1.1, whose schemas take any attribute there, would be written as it was; none is written today), and an attribute of another namespace (a producer's) is foreign markup and is written as before. The OASIS RelaxNG files of ODF 1.0, 1.1, 1.2 and 1.4 differ in no attribute or element name of the text, drawing and style vocabularies that a drawing holds, except what a properties element of 1.0 and 1.1 takes by its open content, so no other name of those versions needed this; the names of `fo` and `style` on those two properties elements are not checked against the schemas (they were written as they were read before a change too). **A flat drawing whose root binds the prefix of its extension to the earlier namespace** (the extension declaring nothing itself) is read with the extension holding its own declaration of the current namespace, so a save that writes it writes it there and no more under the root's old binding. Built in code from the vectors' structure (`crates/native/tests/it/old_namespace.rs`, `crates/engine/tests/it/old_namespace.rs`, `crates/engine/tests/it/odg_keptns.rs`, `crates/odg/tests/it/keptns.rs`, `crates/odg/tests/it/old_namespace.rs`), red on `b94eb83b`. |
| F24 | **Shape operations are in the engine and in the window.** Union, Combine, Fragment, Intersect and Subtract (`shape.union`, `shape.combine`, `shape.fragment`, `shape.intersect`, `shape.subtract`; `docs/editing-commands.md`, "Shape operations") run on `craft-pathops`, from the one-bit fork pinned by revision until storytold/craft-libs#677 merges; then the pin, the `linesweeper` patch and the `deny.toml` source move. The window has them in the Developer tab's Shape Design, Operations menu (`operations.*`): the shapes go in click order or, for a rubber band, back to front; an entry works when `check_shape_ops` allows it; the engine's words say why one is quiet or refused; a command on a selection of another page than the one shown is refused; each is one undo step; and the Developer tab, off until turned on, is turned on in File, Options, Customize Ribbon. **Not yet:** Join, Trim, Offset and Update Alignment Box (drawn quiet) and Fit Curve; distinct icons for the five entries; and the other sections of Options. A menu or split button in the card of a folded ribbon group opens over the card, and an entry chosen, or the command of a button, a tool or a toggle in the card, closes the menu and the card, while a check box ticked leaves it open; a gallery is drawn by the same code, but no gallery works yet, so none is tested. A crossing-heavy star of about 2,000 edges costs about 15 s in one sweep until the library fix in the pull request is pinned. The shape operations' own rules, the window's and those the spec leaves `unknown` are ours and unverified. | The shape operations' work | The other operations and the star's cost wait for later tasks |
| F25 | **The first additional stencil batch is a subset.** The batch supplies eight original decorative symbols, eight fixed-proportion block arrows and four basic Gane-Sarson elements, including a real Data Flow connector. Control handles, separate identifier compartments, automatic numbering, validation and a Gane-Sarson template are not added. Existing templates keep their explicit stencil lists; the window still lists only open stencils. All fifty-eight built-in masters, the twenty of this batch included, are saved to an `.odg` and read back as the session had them, and VSDX save/reopen is supported. | Checked batch spec, engine/UI tests and native captures | Use these masters in a VSDX drawing or in one that came from an `.odg`. No complete stencil/template parity is claimed. |
| F26 | **A shape does not snap to the centre of an empty page.** The reference app's dynamic grid also snaps a shape to the page's centre when the page is empty (left it for after the adoption). Not built: the page's centre lines are not targets. | | Place such a shape by hand or with a guide at the centre. |
| F27 | **A shape does not snap to a container's margins or centre lines.** The dynamic grid snaps to containers' margins and centrelines in the reference app. Not built: a container's margins and centre lines are not targets. | | Use guides. |
| F28 | **Settled.** Two distinct finite points give a line, drawn clipped to the window, whatever the size of their difference or of the window over it; two limits of rounding, not of size, remain (below). `InfiniteLine::clip` now works from whichever of the two points is nearer the window, along the points' direction scaled by a power of two (which does not round; half of the difference when it passes the largest number; raised where the smaller part would turn subnormal), with each edge's distance from that point taken at full size where it is a number and from the numbers at a quarter of their size only where the difference passes the largest number (when any is over a quarter of it); each end is in the window and exactly on one of its edges, and on the line to within a rounding of the largest of the numbers given. Two cases pass and a third still does, and Q6 is not changed (points that differ by rounding noise still give a line at the noise's angle). The old code could also put an end outside the window when one point was very far from it ((1E299, 0) and (0, 0) with a window from x 10.1 to 16.4 gave an end at (0, 0)); that is gone. Tests: the vectors, the far-point, Q6 and point-in-the-window cases (`crates/geom/tests/it/ellipse.rs`) and five property tests over numbers of every size, including the subnormal and the largest (`hostile.rs`: `a_clipped_line_is_finite_on_the_line_and_in_the_window` (finite, in the window, on an edge, on the line to 1E-12 of the largest number, and never lost when a point is in the window), `a_line_through_the_middle_of_the_window_is_kept` (the same for lines made through a point in the window from points up to 1E308 away), `a_line_crossing_the_window_is_kept_when_neither_point_is_in_it` (a line through the origin and a point `q`, over the window from `q`/2 to 2`q`, known in every digit), `a_line_of_any_size_is_cut_where_the_same_line_of_ordinary_size_is` (the same cut as the ordinary-size line at any size) and `a_line_with_a_part_of_a_few_subnormal_units_is_cut_at_its_exact_chord` (a line through the origin with one part of 1 to 6 of the smallest numbers and the other of any size, from ordinary to the largest, in windows whose edges give its exact chord, cut to that chord to 1E-9 of the big part and a unit of the small, turned four ways)). Main fails the first, the second and the fifth of the five, and the far-point, point-in-the-window and wider-window cases; the other two properties stop a regression against main. A fix round answered the review's two findings: the first version scaled the larger part of the direction to [1, 2), which turned a smaller part under 2^-1074 of it to 0 and lost lines that cross a window off the axis they leave by 1E-24 over 1E300 (5E-324 over 4, window from x 1E-300), 114 of 200,000 hostile inputs, which main keeps; the scale is now raised where it would turn the smaller part subnormal or to 0, and both findings and the third property test are red on that first version. A unit test of which point the line is worked from (`nearer`) walks pairs of a grid round six windows against a plain clamping distance. The narrow re-review of round 1: where a number passed a quarter of the largest, the distance from the point to an edge was taken from the two numbers at a quarter of their size, which turned a position of one or two of the smallest numbers (5E-324 each) to 0, so that a line was lost, or cut to one point or to a wrong end, where main is exact (the points (0, 0) and (1E308, 5E-324) and a window from x 1E300 to 1E307 and y 0 to 5E-324 were lost; about 0.1% of a sweep of window edges at the boundaries); the distance is now the difference at full size where it is a number, divided and then scaled; where the difference passes the largest number it is taken from the two numbers at a quarter of their size, and where only the quotient does, it is the difference scaled by the quarter and divided by the direction, so that no unit of it is lost (a first form of this, which divided at full size and scaled, put the smaller part's edge after the larger part's where its quotient passed the largest number at full size and not at the quarter, and the end on the wrong edge: the first property found it at 4.8 million cases of a run of 20 million, and a unit test pins it). With it the harness's lost lines and wrong ends over a chord longer than 1E-10 of the largest number are 0 in 3.51 million random and boundary cases of the review harness (two seeds; the windows of no width or no height whose numbers are under 1E-317, off the line by under a unit of the smallest number, are as they were), and the ordinary-size sets are bitwise as they were; the test of the three inputs, the fifth property, the test of a window wider than the points and the test of an ordinary line at a subnormal height (which an always-quartered scale fails) are new, and the first two fail on round 1; the properties pass at 20 million cases each, in four runs. The guard in `scaled_to_unit` against a way of 0 is gone (a way is never 0 or infinite: a unit test over 83,000 pairs of the points of a grid pins it). What remains: a line that crosses the window by less than about 1E-10 of the largest of the numbers given may still be missed (the numbers place it to 1E-16 of that largest number, and main loses most of these too); and where the difference of two coordinates passes the largest number its half gives the direction, and half of an odd number of the smallest numbers is rounded to an even one, which can lose or widen a chord of any length (the old code gave no line at all where a difference passes the largest number, so no line is worse off). Was: **An infinite line can vanish at extreme coordinates (task, queued).** `InfiniteLine::clip` (`crates/geom/src/infinite.rs`) steps along `p1 - p0`, so two finite points whose difference is too large to hold, or whose difference is tiny next to the window, give an infinite direction or parameter and `None`: the line is not drawn and nothing warns. Nothing crashes. No file is known to have such a row (the guides use 0 to 232.3); a damaged or hostile file can. Fix: draw the line clipped to the window; must pass and V4 must keep passing. Q6 (points that differ only by rounding noise) is a separate decision and not part of the task. | Found by a Kani model-checking harness on 2026-11-20 (`clip_keeps_a_line_through_the_window`, a law over the eight numbers of a clip, which did not finish; proves it over families of lines in `crates/geom/src/kani_proofs.rs`, `docs/development.md`, Proofs); counterexamples replayed on the real code | None known: no file has such a row. Before the fix a guide or infinite line in a damaged file could be missing from the page. |
| F29 | **Number edge cases that Kani found in `calc` and `units` are fixed.** (a) `MODULUS` works from the exact remainder `n % d`, moved by the divisor when its sign differs (the divisor's sign, kept: `MODULUS(-7, 3)` is 2 and `MODULUS(7, -3)` is -2 as before), and a result that rounds to the divisor is the largest number below it in size, with its sign: `MODULUS(-1E-20, 1)` and `MODULUS(-1E-17, 1)` (the band reached about -5.5E-17) are 0.9999999999999999, `MODULUS(-2, 3E38)` is the largest number below 3E38, and `MODULUS(1E308, 1E-308)` is 3.5E-309, no longer -infinity. (b) The fold of an angle into a turn (`wrap_turn`, shared by `BOUND` of an angle, and by `GRAVITY`'s angle and limits) goes through the exact remainder by the `f64` turn and is 0 for a result that rounds to a whole turn, so it lands from 0 up to but not including `TAU` at every finite angle: `BOUND(-6691341.024733973 rad, 2, FALSE, 0, 0)` is 6.28318530659751 (it was -9.3E-10), `BOUND(2.0074633507824903E+34 rad, ...)` is 4.58 (it was -2.3E18), `BOUND(-1.5E-323 rad, ...)` is 0, and `GRAVITY(90 deg, -1E-15 deg, 180 deg)` is PI, as a limit of 0 gives (it was 0: the limit had become a whole turn). At a size where the spacing of doubles passes a turn the answer is the angle the stored number is modulo `TAU`, which is exact; no nearer angle is chosen. An angle that is not finite is 0 in `BOUND`, as before. (c) Two infinities of one sign compare as IEEE has it (our ruling: leaves infinity open): `(1E308*10) = (1E308*10)`, `>=` and `<=` are TRUE and `<>` is FALSE, and an infinity against a number or the other infinity is the plain order, as before. NaN, which no cell holds (`finish` makes `#NUM!` of one), is unchanged: every symbol comparison of it is FALSE, `<>` too. (d) `Unit::to_internal` and `from_internal` work the product again, dividing first, where it overflowed, so a number whose true value fits is finite (`NauticalMile.to_internal(1e302)` is 7.3E306, `Hectare.to_internal(2.605578372271685e298)` is 4.0E305, the kilometre at 1e303 and the metre at 1e306 are finite; a number that does not fit is an infinity with its sign), and a zero or an infinity is itself at any dimension and in any unit (`Quantity::with_dim(0.0, Unit::Mile, -128).shown()` is 0, not NaN; `value_in` and `from_shown` too). Tests, each red on main and green now: `crates/calc/tests/it/{math,geometry,operators}.rs`, the unit tests of `fn_math.rs` and `ops.rs` (400,000 random bit patterns for `wrap_turn`), and `crates/units/tests/it/convert.rs` (every unit over 629 powers of ten at four mantissas, every unit at every dimension). Kani proves `fold_remainder` and `fold_modulo` for every remainder, the symbol comparisons of an infinity against IEEE, the conversions of ten units (the hectare, the nautical mile, the kilometre, the metre, the mile, the millimetre, the cicero, the didot, the degree, the elapsed second) finite wherever the value fits, and a zero and an infinity themselves at any dimension (`docs/development.md`, Proofs); the harnesses of the conversions (for the hectare, the nautical mile and the kilometre) and of the comparisons fail on the old code, as checked. **Not proved:** `wrap_turn` and `floor_modulo` as wholes, because Kani over-approximates the float `%` (the harness fails on a remainder no real `%` gives): the exact remainder is the standard library's and is tested; and the conversions of the other 26 units, which the test covers over 2,500 sizes and not Kani. | Found by Kani harnesses on 2026-11-20, replayed on the real code, and fixed; not changed | A remainder, an angle turn or a unit conversion at an extreme number gave a wrong value, and two infinities compared unequal; nothing crashed. |
| F30 | **The `.diacraft` package is built (`crates/native`), and the engine opens and saves it; the window opens and saves it.** `read` opens a package within a memory budget (`Limits::max_memory`, which the engine gives as what the process leaves: for a read the reservation of what is left of the process's budget, never more than `nativefile::open::READ_BUDGET`, the smaller of that budget and the package's own `diacraft_native::MAX_MEMORY`, which is the larger, and which a refusal for memory names; `Limits::for_save` for a save) and `write` writes a model; the entries are in one fixed order at one deflate level, blobs are stored once and numbered by first use, a part of a role this version does not know is kept at its own path and written back, damage confined to blobs opens with a note that counts what was lost, and a save copies the entries that did not change (a part is copied only when every blob it names is still the very allocation it was). The engine reads it by its bytes before the other two readers, saves it in two paths decided by the working file (`.vsdx` and `.odg` splice as before), keeps the identifier ledger and the content stamp (**paid where it is needed and nowhere else**: not at a VSDX open and not at a VSDX save, so both cost what they did; a pending stamp keeps the document the drawing was read as, which the session counts, and the pass is made from it by the first Save As to `.diacraft` or by a question once the drawing is another document), and gives each read and save what the process leaves of the memory budget, the model counted once (a `.diacraft` read holds the same reservation as a VSDX read, and every read takes its room before its file is loaded: a file larger than the room is refused from its size). **The window changed:** its Save dialog is asked for the drawing's own type first (`.diacraft` for a new drawing) and for the other types the engine says it writes, which it asks of `Session::save_as_allows` and keeps no table of, its Open dialog lists DiaCraft drawings first and Open Stencil takes `.diacraft-stencil`, the grid, rulers, guides and connection points of a drawing come from the windows part the model holds (`window_settings_of`), and a file of a newer major version is told as made by a newer DiaCraft. **The review of the package changed:** a save copies an entry only when its headers are the ones this writer writes and its method is the writer's pick (an entry another tool zipped is written afresh), a part named twice by the lists is damage, a blob and a part of an unknown role are inflated once into the allocation the model holds, the directory of the archive is charged by the count the container reads it by (a signature in a comment cannot lower it), and `Limits::for_save(held)` gives a save the memory that is left once the codec's own room for the part in hand is taken out. **The hardening round changed:** the allocation for a blob or a part of an unknown role is first asked of the allocator (`try_reserve_exact`: stable Rust has no `Arc<[u8]>` that is made fallibly, so a thread that takes the room between that and the allocation can still abort the process), and a blob that the address space cannot hold is refused as `out_of_memory` in words; a theme or a part lost by its stamp is let go as a lost picture is and is not remembered for a later save; a `mimetype` entry that has a data descriptor is recognised as the first entry of a `.diacraft` file; and a file another tool zipped keeps its preview on its first save when the drawing is unchanged (it is written again, stored; a preview that is no PNG, is over 4 MiB or fails its checksum, or a file whose document part this writer writes in other bytes than the file held, loses it, which the spec allows; the earlier namespace that a file of a build before the id moved to `diacraft.app` holds in its document part is not other bytes, GATE.b94e.F1: the read keeps the stamp, size and checksum of the part with that namespace read as the current one, and a write takes the part as the file's when it is the file's bytes in either spelling, so an unedited Save, a Save As and a convert of such a file keep the picture, as 58 of 58 files of the gate's sample did not on `b94eb83b`). **The second hardening round changed:** the document part's tree up to 256,000 items (41 MB, which no pool counts) is taken out of the room before the pools are shared, so a read short of memory holds no more than the memory it was given (it held up to that much more; `Limits::read_peak` is the sum, and a counted test holds it to `max_memory` at the default and at the engine's budget); and a hostile identifier ledger is measured: `state.xml` is a tree of at most 2,000,000 items (a row is three, so 666,666 rows), its rows are walked once and a mark is kept only for a page the drawing has, so a ledger costs a read in proportion to its bytes and no more than a tree of that size (200,001 rows, 8.4 MB, read in 0.1 s beside 0.38 s for plain pages of the same size; counted by `ledger_cost.rs`, timed by an ignored test). **A `.diacraft` drawing saved as a VSDX type is written from the model alone** (the `VsdxModel` row of the save calls the VSDX model writer with no file, and the content stamp, not the dirty flag, says whether the thumbnail, `Application` and `modified` are kept), and the file it writes becomes the drawing's own; **every part of that file is written again from the model**, so its content types and the rows between its pages and masters are the model writer's (F1: a part made from fields has the content type of what it is, a stencil or template saved as a drawing the drawing's document type), and **a drawing made from a template has no stamp** (it is a new drawing, read from no file of its own), so a VSDX type saved from its `.diacraft` file has a new file's bookkeeping (no thumbnail, `Application` DiaCraft, `modified` the save's), while the template file itself, opened with `edit_template`, keeps them on a visit that edits nothing. **What the VSDX model writer cannot keep of a name**: the model holds no name of its own for the document part, the indexes, `windows.xml` and the relationships parts, so a model save names them from the relationships that reach them, spelt as the model holds the parts it knows (a page's, a master's or a theme's part, a held part, a source of held rows); a relationship that spells one it does not know in another case than the archive did renames it in case, in the folder as the model's parts spell it, and its `Override` names it so, which the content check counts as one difference. A save into the file keeps every name. **Open:** a `.diacraft` drawing is not saved as an `.odg`, and a drawing opened from an `.odg` is not saved as a VSDX type: each is refused in words, with nothing written. **A drawing opened from an `.odg` is saved as `.diacraft`** (the model holds the held values, the definitions and the rest of the ODF data, F23), and its model is given the named data of the file on disk as it is now beside the package data, so a `.diacraft` made after a save is the file's; **the carrier in a `.vsdx` is built** (`crates/vsdx/src/carry.rs`: the data of the `.odg` that VSDX cannot say, the parts and custom properties of the other format and what a newer `.diacraft` wrote ride in DiaCraft's solution part as one item for the document, one for its parts, and one for each page, master, style and shape that holds some, each a fragment of the model's own vocabulary with the stamp of the native outputs it sits beside, and no cell is copied; the bytes go in parts `/diacraft/blob<n>.<ext>` that the solution part's relationships name, equal bytes once, up to 30,000 of them and 256 MiB each; the solution part is read to 64 MiB, `MAX_SOLUTION_PART_BYTES`; **a read judges each item**: one whose object is gone is dropped silently, one whose stamp no longer matches is dropped, an item that names a part the file lacks, two records that name one part, a name no `.odg` package could hold (a part named `meta.xml` or `settings.xml`, spelt so, rides: the `.odg` reader holds them as bytes when no field can, and the save of such a drawing as a VSDX type was refused), and two items for one object are dropped with a word, a macro project of the other format is read only into a type that allows macros and a part has the role its name says, a part is copied into the model only when its item is good, after its bytes are charged, and is never parsed, and the judge's stamping and the copies an item makes of a shared sheet are charged to a budget of 48 million steps and to the pool, so a crafted list stops in words and does not abort; a save into a file and a save from the model both write it, and a part that holds anything beside the `Data` gets no items; a style is keyed by its name, and by its `ID` where another style has the name, and a save says data was left out only for a page, master, style or shape that holds some, where an unedited save of a file with two styles of one name, or two pages of one `ID`, said it of objects that held none; the data of a file a program other than DiaCraft saves may be left out of it, which the save note will say); **an object frame of an `.odg` that holds a compound file and a picture in a format a VSDX file draws is a VSDX embedding** (`crates/vsdx/src/write/objects.rs`; the object is an entry of the package or inline base64 and is a compound file by a check of a fixed number of steps that reads the header and one directory entry and nothing else (never a stream, never XML, and `draw:class-id` is not read), the replacement is a BMP, EMF, GIF, JPEG, PNG or TIFF by its first bytes, and each of the three VSDX writers (a drawing never saved, one written from the model alone, and a save into a file, which gives a page part it makes the rows its shapes name through `write/page_rows.rs`) writes the object's bytes unchanged as `embeddings/oleObjectN.bin` (N above every number in use), typed by a `Default` for `bin` or, when the package's `Default` for `bin` names another type, by an `Override`, with its own relationship part that holds the one `image` relationship to the picture (the page has none to it), a relationship of the type `oleObject` from the page part, and a `Foreign` shape whose `ForeignData` says `Object` with the attributes that rode with the frame, or `ObjectType` 33280, `ShowAsIcon` 0 and `ObjectWidth` and `ObjectHeight` as its `Width` and `Height`; two frames of one object and one picture share the embedding; an object past 256 MiB, a frame that would pass the 1 GiB of embeddings of a save or the 64 MiB of inline objects, a frame on a master and every other frame (a `draw:object`, an object or a picture the package lacks, bytes that are no compound file, a replacement in another format, a macro project's entry, a folder of entries) ride in the carrier, which leaves out the part an embedding is and the frame's `draw:object-ole` element, so that the model read from the file holds the embedding and no markup that names an entry that is not there; the stamp of a shape that shows a picture no longer holds the name or type of its picture's part or the attributes a writer gives a picture or an object, so the item of a picture frame is no longer stale after a save; a picture is typed by its bytes when the type the manifest claims for it is none a reader draws; the parts a VSDX package holds are typed before the parts the writer makes; measured by tests built in code, by the real `.odg` reader through `.diacraft` to a VSDX type in the engine, by children under `ulimit -v 3000000` (an object of 256 MiB and one byte is refused in words, one of 256 MiB is written), by 10,000 small objects counted (two looks at each frame, the parts put in a map once, at most 640 bytes of each object read, no byte copied, and three parts for each, 2.8 to 3.2 times what 10,000 pictures cost), by the content check of the trip, and by LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 opens 18 of 18 files so written and takes each embedding for an object with its bytes unchanged, measured; **not done:** an object put on a page the file already has is written without its picture by a save into the file, as a picture is (F11), the page item of a drawing the `.odg` reader made was stale after the trip with no object in it (fixed), and LibreOffice draws no picture for an embedding, since its own import carries no fallback; nothing was opened in the VSDX application); **the carrier in an `.odg` is built** (`crates/odg/src/ext/items.rs` and `shell.rs`: the data of a VSDX file that an `.odg` cannot say rides on the shape's own automatic style as the attributes `vsdx` and `foreign` where the shape has one, and as `vsdx` items in DiaCraft's document file for the document, its parts and custom properties and each page, style and group that holds some, each a fragment of the model's own vocabulary with the stamp of the outputs it sits beside, and the bytes of the parts in entries `DiaCraft/blob<n>.<ext>` that the manifest lists beside a row for the folder with a media type, equal bytes once, up to 30,000 entries, 256 MiB each and 512 MiB in all; **a read judges each item** as the VSDX one does, an entry is read only when its item is good and after its bytes are charged, a macro project is bytes and never parsed, and a part name no VSDX package could hold is refused at the save and left out with a word at a read; **a carried text's stamp covers its formatting** (`fp-format`), so a formatting edit made in LibreOffice wins; the assumption of the VSDX writers that the attributes an object frame carried come back in `ForeignData::attrs` after `ForeignType` holds; LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 keeps the attributes, the document file and every entry when it saves such a file and drops the folder when its manifest row is missing, measured on copies, nothing opened in the VSDX application); the package reads and writes a page and a master as a stream of the children of the part's root (`page_bytes`, `save_parts_bytes`; the document part, the manifest and the state stay trees of at most 2 million items), so that a page of any number of shapes is a package of this crate, the blobs a part names are found by a scan of its text, and a refusal of a page part is the codec's (`model_too_complex` and `model_malformed`) and not the container's XML classes; the engine renders the picture of the first page for a changed model, and a stencil has none, and a page of more than 5,000 shapes has none (the 5,000 are the top-level shapes of the page: the members of a group are not counted, so a page of a few groups that hold tens of thousands of shapes has a picture); a `.diacraft` open and save of 20,000 shapes cost 1.8 to 2.1 times a VSDX open and save (the VSDX pair no longer pays for a stamp; the `.diacraft` times are as they were), in the codec (the open is the decode, the save makes every part twice and decodes it once to prove an open would take it); **an edit after a `.diacraft` open cost 1.31 to 1.47 times one after a VSDX open of the same 12,000 distinct shapes (1.25 to 1.4 in the five runs), and costs 1.12 to 1.27 now** (`perf` 7.3.4, release build, 300 moves and 100 fills, 2026-11-21). **Cause:** an edit walks every formula of the page (`calc`'s `list_cross_readers`, `classify` and `reach`: 1.6 million small objects, and 55 to 60 percent of the cycles in `malloc`, `realloc` and `free`, on both sides); the `.diacraft` side retired 4 percent fewer instructions (80.5 against 83.9 billion) and had twice the misses of the translation of addresses (71 against 37.5 million `dTLB` load misses), 16 percent more cache misses and 20 percent more demand fills from memory; a decode that enters nothing in its table, or shares nothing, has the `.vsdx` side's 37 million; one that enters the units with no streams has 54 million; so it is the table's small allocations (a `Vec` for each key and a `Box` of the stream for each unit, all let go together at the end of the decode), and not the walk, the sharing or the format. **Fix** (`sheetxml/src/model/share.rs`): no entry is an allocation of its own, a table is a map from a key to the head of a list, the units in one array that grows by doubling, and the streams in chunks (the first as long as the first stream, each next twice as long, to a mebibyte); the table's memory is charged before it is made (`SHARE_ENTRY_BYTES` is 160, which holds the most the array and the map take for an entry, and a stream that makes a chunk is charged the room of the chunk beyond itself); the translation misses are 37 million on both sides and the instructions are equal. **Left:** 1.12 to 1.27 times (1.20 on the mean of eight runs at one time on separate cores, load 31 to 49), also with a decode that shares nothing: the `.vsdx` reader has the objects of a shape in one block that the walk sweeps in order, the decoder lets go of a tree for every shape and the next shape's objects are made in the chunks it left, over a hundred kilobytes, four jumps of 64 KiB or more for each shape in the walk against none; the maps of cells built early, the units interned, a tree let go a child later and the trees let go in a bunch gained nothing; **The cost is taken out of the edit itself** (release build with line tables, 12,000 distinct shapes, 300 moves and 100 fills, the thread's CPU, both sides pinned to one cache group, load 4 to 12, the least of five runs of each, the three builds run in turn in one session): `calc`'s `reach` allocates nothing (recursion to the parser's depth, a stack of its own only below it; 300 moves 10.8 s to 8.0 s on the `.vsdx` side, 12.5 s to 9.9 s on the `.diacraft` side), and `calc` keeps the readers of other sheets from one edit to the next (`ReaderIndex`, owned by the engine's session and counted in its hold on the process's ledger): an edit finds the shapes it replaced by pointer and puts right what it read of those, and `changes_between_with` compares the page in step with the copy before, so an edit costs what it touched and not 168,000 formulas and 12,000 shapes: **300 moves take 0.069 s on the `.vsdx` side and 0.076 s on the `.diacraft` side (158 and 164 times faster), 100 fills 3.6 and 3.7 ms (890 and 1,070 times), and the `.diacraft` side costs 1.11 times the `.vsdx` side for the moves and 1.01 for the fills (paired runs 1.00 to 1.27 and 0.85 to 1.10)**: the effect of the layout of the decoded model, measured above, is hidden now that an edit does not walk the model; what is left of an edit is the engine's own walks of a page by `ID` (`draw::scan`, `Document::find_shape`, about half of the cycles) and the first edit of a drawing, which makes the index (about 20 ms for 12,000 shapes), and the cost of an edit that adds, takes out or moves a shape, changes a master or a style, or a `Connect` row, which is what it was (the index is made again whole); `affected/oracle.rs` keeps the old walk and a property test of 1,000 generated pages and edits holds the index to it at every step; `crates/engine/tests/it/native_speed.rs` (ignored) holds the `.diacraft` side to 1.25 times the `.vsdx` side on the least of five runs of each, and its last run gave 1.13 for 300 moves and 1.05 for 100 fills (test profile, load 27 to 48); a refusal of a newer major version is told to the window as "not a drawing file" by its kind, and the window says it in words of its own from the engine's; the format version written is 1.1 where the plan said 1.0 (the spec's 2.4: 1.1 is this version); a save holds the model and the package in memory beside each other (the package grows as its parts are written, charged before each is made), so the largest drawings are refused in words; a part named like an entry the package makes (`pages/page9.xml` as an extra part) is refused at the write and not renamed. **A new drawing is a `.diacraft` drawing (NP10):** `file.new`, blank or from a built-in template, and a template file (`.diacraft-template`, `.vstx`, `.vstm`) opened as a new drawing keep the model and no file's bytes (the package a template was read from is let go before the drawing is made, and the model holds every part it held); the model a new drawing starts with is as it was (the content stamp of the blank drawing and of each built-in template is pinned by a test, with the property times cleared, and page 1's `ID` is 1); its first save is `.diacraft` and copies nothing; a Save As to a VSDX type writes the file from the model (`SavePath::VsdxModel`, the VSDX model writer with no file to splice into), with times of its own and lists of its own page and master names in `docProps/app.xml` where a template's cannot be told apart; a `.vsdx` that was opened keeps being saved as a `.vsdx` by the splice writer, silently, and an `.odg` as `.diacraft` or an `.odg`; a `.diacraft` drawing or a new one is saved as an `.odg` or an `.otg` from the model alone, and the refusals that stay name `.diacraft` as a way out. **The `.odg` half in the engine.** `save_path` has its `OdgModel` rows (Call 18's refusal is lifted): `diacraft_odg::write_with(doc, None, ..)` writes a `.diacraft` drawing or a new one as an `.odg` (a drawing) or an `.otg` (a template), the kind the name says always asked for; the engine's own shapes are flattened as a save into a file flattens its new ones (`flatten_model`: a shape that holds ODF data is in the file's form and is left alone, `DrawingSizeType` is kept, the copies take at most a third of what a save may hold), the VSDX package data of DiaCraft's own skeleton that a drawing started from a built-in template holds is left out (`odgfile/skeleton.rs`: only data that is exactly a built-in template's; any other is refused by the writer in words), the thumbnail and the generator follow the content stamp and never the dirty flag, and the file written is the drawing's own (the next save splices into it, tested). An `.odg` read from a file has its content stamp pending, so an `.odg` that visits `.diacraft` and comes back unedited keeps the thumbnail and the generator it was read with. `.otg` is a format to open and save (`file.open` makes a new unnamed drawing from it, `edit_template` opens the template itself, `file.new {template: path}` starts from one, Save As writes one, `file.status` says `otg` and `template`) and no built-in template; `.fodg` is no Save As target (the words point to the `.odg` package). Refusal counts, by class, measured on the engine's 65 drawings (`odg_model_measure.rs`): before, 65 of 65 refused by the engine's table (Call 18); after, 0 refused, 65 written and read back as the same picture (50 plain, 15 with the note `outline_approximated`). What the engine still refuses, in words and with nothing written: a stencil, a background page and the rest of what the writer refuses (the window settings ride inside the file as the `windows` child of DiaCraft's document file); the text formatting a style sheet gives a new shape's text is still a said loss. LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 opens 73 of 73 files written so (headless, copies in a scratch folder, a profile of its own; the control that states two versions is refused: measured, not the VSDX application, and no repair detector was used). The visit of an `.odg` through `.diacraft` is the same content for a drawing with no shape and in every part but `content.xml` for one with shapes (`native_odg.rs`); the shapes' own markup is written as it was read and a hand-built `.odg` of another program differs where the model writer says ODF 1.4 on every part and writes a settings part. A drawing opened from a `.fodg` is saved by Save alone as the flat file it is, and Save As of it stays refused: its visit through `.diacraft` is not offered by the engine. The `.odg` model writer is in the `odg` crate (`write_with(doc, None, ..)` writes the model of a `.diacraft` or of a new drawing as an `.odg` package, with `meta.xml`, `settings.xml`, the manifest and the package entries from the model); the engine's refusal of the save of a `.diacraft` drawing as an `.odg` is lifted, which wires the writer for a `.diacraft` drawing, a new one and an `.otg`. The drawing adopts the model a VSDX file reads as, when that holds everything (a second Save As across in a session is written, the concern 5), a picture an `.odg` held gets the cells of its box (concern 2), the page's own item is fresh (concern 6: its stamp leaves out the `PageContents` and `Rel` entries the writer always makes). **The model writer carries the style sheets of a VSDX drawing** (the concern 1: the root's sheet and names, any links, duplicate or empty names; the first VSDX file that was ever saved as an `.odg` through it, since every one was refused for its root), whose cause was the splice path's rule for a style sheet the file holds applied to the package the model writer makes. **Said, not closed (for 0.3):** the links of a VSDX drawing's shapes to its style sheets and the values they took from them are the `.odg`'s own values after a save across (a warning says how many shapes), so **a VSDX drawing that goes to an `.odg` and back is the same drawing by every cell's number, the style sheets and the data no field reads, but not cell for cell** (units, the form of a geometry formula, the cells a shape took from a style sheet, the style links); closing it needs the `.odg` writer to write a shape's natives from the flattened copy and carry the shape's own sheet (a change in `crates/odg/src/ext/native.rs`), which beside this task has the writer's files for. The 14 files of the first kind validate against the ODF 1.4 schema, DiaCraft draws each as it draws the VSDX drawing, LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 opens each and draws the group, the connection, the formulas, the texts and the picture as DiaCraft does, and the trip VSDX, `.diacraft`, `.odg`, `.diacraft`, VSDX gives the masters as they were and each instance with its link and the values of its cells (the cells of its master that it was written with are its own: the same values). **The cost is counted:** a master used 100,000 times is refused in words at the step that passes the 6,000,000 a copy may take (the steps follow the instances, and are within twice those of as many plain shapes), a group master of 10,000 members costs the same for each member, a master of groups nested forty deep the same for each level, and a master of 5,000 rows of `Character` costs an instance what a master of one costs. **Still refused, in words and with nothing written:** a style sheet with no `Name` (the entry is keyed by the name the file holds it under), a background page, and a picture in a format the `.odg` reader does not draw. A save that would give a file the reader holds more than 20,000,000 items for (about 60,000 instances of a master of five rows) is refused after the file is made, in the reader's words.
