Such a zoom is now refused in words by the commands, and a query given one takes the default zoom, as for a zoom that is no number (`diacraft_engine::snap::takes_zoom`); no zoom at which an alignment works is refused (the harness proves the two the same for every zoom above 0). **Left open:** a subject whose moving shapes lie past about 1E308 inches beyond its box makes the reach an infinity at any zoom (a hostile file's coordinates only, and no crash: a reach capped at the largest number would need the library's window over all the numbers); and the `.odg` reader and writer read an attribute named `xmlns:` (an XML name, but no qualified name) as declaring the default namespace, where a namespace-aware reader refuses the file (a ruling is asked; the writer refuses such a name as no qualified name everywhere but in the declarations of the containers of a base file). No other law of the batch failed on the real code. The VSDX application was not inspected, and nothing of ours was opened in it. | Kani's covers of `a_snap_takes_a_zoom_exactly_when_the_strongest_alignment_reaches_a_distance` and `an_alignment_reaches_at_least_its_own_reach_and_is_never_a_nan` (`crates/engine/src/kani_proofs.rs`); the refusal is held by `a_zoom_at_which_the_strongest_pull_passes_the_largest_number_is_refused` (`crates/engine/tests/it/snap_commands.rs`), which fails on the old code, and by the unit test `a_zoom_is_taken_exactly_where_the_strongest_pull_is_a_distance_on_the_page`; the reading of `xmlns:` by the harness of `declared_by` of six characters (`crates/odg/src/kani_proofs.rs`) | Low: no view goes below 9.6 points to an inch (10%), and the window sends its own; a program that asked for a smaller zoom got a snap with no alignment, and gets a refusal in words now |
| F37 | **What the Kani proofs of the fourth batch found.** Three laws failed on the real code, each reached only by numbers near the largest one or a class no caller passes, none a crash, and each is fixed: (1) the axis a standard glue point of an `.odg` connector leaves its shape by (`draw::odg_glue::axes_of`) took a direction whose two parts add up past the largest number (1.56E308 and 2.39E307) for a diagonal, its tolerance being an infinity, so the point left by both axes; the test is now made on halves, which is exact, and the same test wherever the sum is a number. (2) The set of classes a save across names (`across::classes::Classes`) took a class past `u32::MAX` for the bit 31, which no word names, and found it there; the classes are the vocabulary's 21, so no caller passed one, and such a class is no bit now. (3) A step of Increase Indent from an `IndLeft` below about minus half the largest number, or of Decrease Indent from one above half of it, came to an infinity (its count of quarter inches was no number), which the write of the cell took for 0, so the indent went to the margin; and a step from an indent a quarter inch does not move (past about 2^51 inches) gave the indent back as a step. Such an indent stays now. **Seen, not a bug:** `odg_glue::better` takes its tolerance from the first route's cost, so in a window about the tolerance squared wide two routes are told apart one way and are as good the other (never each better than the other, which the harness proves). No other law of the batch failed on the real code. The VSDX application was not inspected, and nothing of ours was opened in it. | Kani's harnesses `a_glue_point_leaves_by_the_page_axis_its_direction_goes_along_and_both_only_on_a_diagonal`, `a_class_is_in_a_set_exactly_when_it_was_put_in_and_is_a_bit_of_it` and `a_step_of_indent_goes_to_the_next_quarter_inch_and_stays_from_zero_to_a_hundred_inches` (`crates/engine/src/kani_proofs.rs`), each of which fails on the old code; the unit tests `a_long_direction_leaves_by_the_axis_of_its_larger_part_alone` (`draw/odg_glue.rs`), `a_class_past_the_bits_of_the_set_is_never_put_in_or_found` (`across/classes.rs`) and `an_indent_a_quarter_inch_does_not_move_stays_where_it_is` (`formatting/text.rs`), each of which fails on the old code | Low: only a file or a program with numbers near the largest one, or a class no caller gives, reached them; an indent so set went to the margin on a step |
| F38 | **What the Kani proofs of the fifth batch found.** One law failed on the real code, reached only by numbers past half the largest one, none a crash, and it is fixed: the middle of a container's box (`draw::container::Rect::centre`) was the sum of two sides halved, and for a box whose sides sum past the largest number (a shape at 1.7E308 inches, say) the sum was an infinity, so the middle was not in the box: such a shape was in no container, a member of a locked container was refused a move as moving out of it, and the move of a member kept on an edge was an infinity. The middle is now the midpoint of each pair of sides (`f64::midpoint`), the same number as before for every box whose sides are within half the largest number, and in the box for every box of finite sides. No other law of the batch failed on the real code. The VSDX application was not inspected, and nothing of ours was opened in it. | Kani's harness `a_boxs_middle_is_in_it_whatever_its_sides` (`crates/engine/src/kani_proofs.rs`), which fails on the old code; the unit test `a_box_at_the_far_end_of_the_numbers_holds_its_own_middle` (`draw/container/page.rs`), which fails on the old code | Low: only a file or a program with coordinates past half the largest number reached it |
| F39 | **What the Kani proofs of the sixth batch found.** Two laws failed on the real code, none a crash in a release build, and both are fixed. (1) A gradient's angle (`formatting::effects::radians`) was rounded to nine places of a radian after the test for 0, so an angle below 0 by less than about 2.9E-8 of a degree (`shape.gradient` with `angle` -1E-10) was written as `-0` in `FillGradientAngle`; the test is made on the rounded number now, and every other angle is written as before. (2) A Quick Style's place (`formatting::QuickStyle::at` and `variant`) worked the style's numbers out (`99 + column`, `100 + place`) before it asked whether the place is one of the gallery's, so `shape.quick_style` with a `column` or a `variant` near the largest whole number (4,294,967,295) overflowed: a panic in a debug build, which the engine's guard turned into an error, and a wrapped number in a release build, refused in words as any other place is; the numbers are made only for a place of the gallery now. No other law of the batch failed on the real code. The VSDX application was not inspected, and nothing of ours was opened in it. | Kani's harnesses `an_angle_within_a_turn_is_written_as_radians_to_nine_places_and_never_as_minus_zero` and `a_quick_style_is_one_place_of_the_gallery_or_one_variant_and_no_two_places_share_one` (`crates/engine/src/kani_proofs.rs`), which fail on the old code; the tests `a_gradient_angle_a_hair_below_zero_is_written_as_zero_and_not_as_minus_zero` and `a_quick_style_of_a_place_at_the_end_of_the_whole_numbers_is_refused_in_words` (`crates/engine/tests/it/formatfx.rs`), which fail on the old code | Low: only an angle within a hair of 0 below it, and a place no gallery has |

## UI/UX

| # | Gap | Evidence | User impact |
|---|---|---|---|
| U28 | **Fixed:** vector stencil previews use a thin unfilled graphite outline, smooth basic circles and ellipses, and borderless idle tiles with hover and focus feedback. Imported bitmap icons keep their original artwork. The interface polish adds optional regular interface fonts, neutral surfaces, quieter grid lines and consistent control states. The stencil gallery adds a compact stencil chooser and current-stencil filter, uniform square icon cards in a snug panel, and name-only hover tooltips with accessible names. | Native light/dark window captures and existing stencil tests; `docs/ui-parity.md` | The stencil gallery is visually quieter; no feature-parity score changes |
| U1 | **The web build was run in a browser:** headless Chrome 158 and Firefox 160 on Linux, with software WebGL 2 and no WebGPU adapter. It starts under the site's content security policy, draws, takes a connector and labels, makes no request after it loads and stores nothing, and a 400-action random run raised no error. **Not tried:** Safari, Edge, Windows and macOS browsers, a phone or tablet, a real GPU, WebGPU, a large drawing, and a long session | `docs/website.md`, "The web app" (measured, 2026-11-22) | Web use is proven on two engines on one system only |
| U2 | Graphics-adapter fallback logic was not copied from the siblings | | A machine with a poor adapter may fail to start |
| U3 | **Fixed.** The window opened at 1280x720 pt on Wayland, not the 1280x800 asked. Cause: eframe cuts the asked size to the largest monitor's size in points, worked out with winit's scale factor, which Wayland gives as a whole number (2 for a monitor set to 1.15), so a 2560x1440 monitor read as 1280x720. The app now turns the clamp off on Wayland, where a compositor places a window as it likes; X11, Windows and macOS keep it. Seen on a real run: `ui.inspect` reports 1280x800 | Real run | Cosmetic |
| U4 | App-drawn title bar input could not be injected under native Wayland; drag, double click, buttons and resize were tested under XWayland only | Real run | A native Wayland difference could go unnoticed |
| U5 | A second title-bar drag moved the window by an unexpected distance under XWayland, shared pointer suspected, not explained | Real run | Could be a test artefact or a real jump |
| U7 | A hidden window runs no `ui` pass in this egui fork, so control requests take about 0.25 s each while it is hidden | | Slower automation when hidden |
| U8 | A page tab (or any window text) shows a missing-glyph box for a character Ubuntu Light lacks (Han, Arabic, Hebrew, Thai and the like). The bundled faces of `crates/fonts` are those two faces, so only the machine's faces can fill in: that takes the font scan off the window's thread, a face added to egui's families at run time (which resets its glyph atlas), a cap on the bytes held, and a web path with none | | Page names and the title in other scripts read as boxes |
| U9 | The mouse reaches the top-level shapes of a page, and at most 5,000 of them, and by a second click on a selected group the members of its groups (at most 5,000 members, 8 levels down; a group whose `SelectMode` is 0 offers none, nor do the groups inside it, and one set to 2 gives its member to the first click); a page with more shapes says so and leaves the rest to the commands. A shape is hit inside its outline whether or not it is filled (the reference app is said to need the edge for an unfilled one); arcs and curves in a shape's geometry are followed by straight pieces. A turned or flipped shape has its handles on its own box and a drag works along its own axes, and a selected 2-D shape or group has a rotation handle; a group's eight handles and its rotation handle work as a shape's (a drag sizes the group and its members follow it), and a member of a turned or flipped group has no handle. Several shapes are stretched and turned by the handles of the box round them: a corner stretches them about the opposite corner in proportion to their joint box, a side in one axis (in proportion with Shift), the rotation handle turns them as one about the middle of the joint box in steps that get finer the farther the pointer is, the engine snaps the joint box as it does a shape's, each shape comes out by the stretch of the joint box (a shape turned by other than a quarter keeps its angle), a shape with `LockWidth`, `LockHeight` or `LockRotate` set is left alone, and a set with a member in a turned or flipped group has handles that start nothing; the set is flipped only by the keys and menus; a net takes only the shapes wholly inside it (the Advanced option "Select shapes partially within area" is not built); a drag with Ctrl held at the press copies the shapes instead of moving them | `edit/scene.rs` | Some shapes of real files are hit a little wrongly; the members of a turned group are resized, turned and flipped only by commands, which refuse them; how a stretch of several shapes carries one turned by other than a quarter, and what the locks do to a set, are our own rules (no specification says); the Size & Position pane (`shape.size`) still refuses a group, a group's own constant geometry is not scaled, and the locks are not read for one shape's handles or drawn as handle bars. **A net and a lasso are the engine's** (`select.by_region`, `docs/editing-commands.md`, "Selecting by type and by region"): a net of the Pointer Tool or of Area Select, or the loop of Lasso Select (Home, Editing, Select; the Draw tab's too), selects the top-level shapes wholly inside it, or with File, Options, Advanced, "Select shapes partially within area" (off at first) those it touches too, at most 10,000 shapes read; Select by Type selects by type, role and layer (`select.by_type`); a shape on a locked layer is not taken by either, and a click passes over it too |
| U10 | The native Save and Open dialogs were not driven in a real run (they would open on the desktop of the person using the machine); they are tested with a stand-in that answers a path, and the save itself was driven through `file.save_as` and Ctrl+S on a drawing that has a file. A real run drove the window with a stand-in Save dialog that answers a name with no type, which showed the question before an existing file is replaced. | | A difference in how a portal dialog hands back a name (no extension, a folder) would show first in use; the window adds the drawing's own type (`.vsdx` for a VSDX drawing) to a name with none. The dialog sets the extension of the chosen kind on each system through the pinned `rfd` fork, whose macOS panel, Windows dialog and Linux portal were not driven in a real run either (their rules are unit-tested, and the code is built for those targets) |
| U11 | Save runs on the window's thread: the session lives there, and the writer reads the file back before it writes, so a very large drawing holds the window for the time that takes | | A pause while saving a big file; opening is already off-thread |
| U12 | **Fixed in **. The View tab's Ruler, Grid, Guides and Connection Points boxes are the drawing's: the model holds them as the VSDX windows part does, a box turned makes the drawing unsaved and is no undo step (the owner's ruling of 2026-11-22: Ctrl+Z leaves the settings alone; the AutoConnect box and the Snap & Glue options follow the same rule), and every save writes them (spliced into a VSDX file's drawing window, in a `.diacraft` file's model, in an `.odg`'s DiaCraft document file); a drawing with no windows part, of any format, opens with a new drawing's settings, and so does one whose windows part holds no drawing window (an empty `Windows`, or a stencil's window alone, which opened with the grid and the rulers off), and one whose settings did not change writes its windows part as it read it. An `.odg` whose page holds a group of no name with a graphic style or glue points of its own saves a setting turned (the document file had refused an entry for that group, whose data the markup gives). **Left:** a flat drawing (`.fodg`) does not keep them (it has no DiaCraft document file; the save says so), `settings.xml`'s `GridIsVisible` is neither read nor written, and a window that names a page taken out goes from a VSDX file with that page, its flags put in a drawing window of their own | Real run; `crates/engine/tests/it/window_settings.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/tests/it/window_save.rs` | None for VSDX, `.diacraft` and `.odg`; a flat drawing reopens with a new drawing's settings |
| U13 | The native Export dialog was not driven in a real run (it would open on the desktop of the person using the machine); it is tested with a stand-in that answers a path, and the three kinds were written through `file.export` on the control channel and read back (a PNG, an SVG and a PDF rendered by other tools). The picture is made from a copy of the drawing in a session of its own, and scans the machine's fonts for each export. A real run drove the Export dialog with the same stand-in. | | A portal dialog's way of handing back a name would show first in use; the window adds `.png` to a name with none |
| U17 | The window's text box shows one face and size for all of a label's characters, and shows its fields under them as pieces that cannot be typed into, not at the places they stand; a field is inserted from the Field dialog and not typed in the box, and cannot be edited or taken out from it. Characters with no glyph in the face draw as a box | | A label with runs of its own looks plain while it is edited, and a field has to be changed or taken out by a command |
| U18 | While a file is being read the window takes no edits from the person, but a program on the control channel is not held: what it changes in the meantime is replaced when the file arrives. The questions and the progress card are modals; they were driven in a real run, with the file dialogs answered by a stand-in that reads a path from a file, and not with a screen reader | | A program that edits while it opens a file loses those edits without a word |
| U14 | **Fixed.** Opening a file unpacked the package twice: the engine's `file.open` read it, and then `window_settings.rs` read it again for the grid and ruler flags of the windows part (up to 1 GiB of parts, one after the other, not at once; a file over 128 MiB was not looked in). The window now reads the flags from the windows part the model holds (`window_settings_of`, `Document::vsdx`, which the VSDX reader fills), so nothing is unpacked a second time and a file of any size has them | | Cosmetic |
| U15 | A file that a hung disk will not give leaves its reading thread blocked: Cancel stops the window waiting and throws the answer away, but the thread lives until the disk gives up. At most four such threads are allowed alive; past that an Open is refused in words. A thread blocked on the disk holds none of the memory budget (a read reserves it after the file is loaded), so the next Open is not refused for it; a thread that is already parsing when it is cancelled holds the budget until it ends, and an Open made then waits for it and is read when it ends | | A person who cancels four opens on a dead mount must wait or restart |
| U16 | At 480 points wide the View tab's Show, Visual Aids and Window groups draw their labels over each other (seen with and without the new Picture button): the fit gives the groups the compact size, whose measured width the drawn labels exceed | Real run | A cosmetic overlap at the smallest window width |
| U19 | **Settled in **: with a text box open the Font group reads `shape.formatting` with `start` and `end`, so Bold, Italic, Underline and Strikethrough show pressed, not pressed or half pressed (mixed), the Font and Size boxes show the value or nothing, the Font Color bar shows the colour or the slash, and a press on a mixed toggle turns it on. What is left: **a caret with nothing selected shows the run before it but a press formats the whole text** (the inventory's "whole block when only clicked"), so the value sent is the opposite of what that one run shows; with no box open a shape shows its first run; a shape that shows its master's text has none of its own to range over | | A caret in the middle of a mixed text can turn the style off for all of it because the run it stands in has it |

The system clipboard is the text of the labels only, and nothing is read from it: egui offers one kind of thing at a time and a paste is the session's clipboard | | A shape copied here cannot be pasted into another program, and nothing from another program can be pasted as a shape |

| # | Gap | Evidence | User impact |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | Windows is type-checked from Linux (`cargo check` of both programs for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`, with the C allocator's build stood in for, 2026-11-22: measured) and is built and smoke tested by a CI job, which has not run; its window, file dialogs, printing and fonts have not been tried. Its window checks that a GPU runs commands before it draws on it, as on macOS (for a laptop with two GPUs; type-checked, not run). **macOS has run on one Intel Mac** (2026-11-22: measured): built, smoke tested, packaged (the universal zip too), the window drawn and the test suite run (`docs/hardware-parity.md`). Still open there: a person has not typed Cmd shortcuts or used an input method, the Open and Save dialogs or Cmd+Q in it; the app menu, whose items name the program, now `DiaCraft` (the process opened from the packaged app is `DiaCraft`, measured; the menu itself not seen, since a session through ssh cannot capture the screen); printing; Apple silicon. A new drawing's text is drawn in Arial there (measured), not in Apple SD Gothic Neo, Fit to Window is Command+0 (Command+Shift+W is the system's), and the test build has no warning. No key of the window is one the Mac or its app menu reserves (Flip Horizontal is Shift+Command+H there, where Command+H hides the app, and eight more; tested with the Mac's modifiers, not typed on a Mac), and the Windows test build of the window and the engine passes clippy with no warning (measured from Linux). The key marked delete, which sends Backspace on a Mac, deletes the selection there while no text is edited, beside Forward Delete, and every Mac key is one the common Mac layouts can press (measured from 28 layouts' own tables; Center moved from Command+|, which no layout but Latin American could press, to Shift+Command+E, the font sizes to Option+Command+. and ,, and Command+Plus zooms in beside Command+=); checked through the control channel on the Mac, not typed on a Mac keyboard or with another layout selected. FreeBSD is not tried | M0 | macOS: what a person sees and types; Windows unknown |
| P2 | App Nap handling not ported | | macOS only, background control may slow |

## Automation and security

| # | Gap | Evidence | User impact |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | The control port has no password; it binds to `127.0.0.1` only, but any local program can drive the app while it is on | | Local programs can act on the document |
| A2 | Automation roots not ported. `file.open` (M2) and `stencil.open` read, and `file.save`, `file.save_as` and `file.export` write, any path the process can reach: the control channel and MCP are not kept to folders the user granted. `stencil.open` reads through a link to a regular file, as `file.open` does, through the same reading path (a folder, a device and a pipe are refused, a file over the cap is refused) | | A program that can drive the app can read any drawing or stencil the user can read and write a drawing file anywhere the user can write (see A5 for what a save refuses) |
| A5 | `file.save_as` through the control channel or MCP writes a file with a drawing extension (.vsdx, .vsdm, .vssx, .vssm, .vstx, .vstm, .odg, .diacraft, .diacraft-stencil, .diacraft-template) at any path the user can write, replacing a file that is there. It refuses a link at the end of the path unless the link leads to the drawing's own file (a security fix: a link named `x.vsdx` to a dotfile was followed), a folder, a device and a pipe. Folders in the path may be links, and a link swapped in between the check and the rename is not guarded against. `file.export` follows the same rules for `.png`, `.svg` and `.pdf` files, and never writes the open drawing's own file | | An agent could overwrite any file that already has a drawing or a picture extension; granted folders (A2) would close this |
| A3 | A 16 MiB MCP line can cost up to about 2 GiB to parse (about 1.1 to 1.5 GiB for small flat objects), and `run`'s retained JSON Lines params up to about 8 GiB within its limits (about 5.6 GiB for small flat objects) | Measured in a review, and again on the release binary | A hostile local client could exhaust memory on a small machine |
| A6 | A variable-composite (`VARC`) glyph that follows its most references (2,047) to a glyph of 65,535 points costs about 1.3 s of CPU and 21 MB in one `Face::outline_glyph` call, though the path is cut at 262,144 elements and the glyph comes back `Broken` (`crates/fonts`): `read-fonts` draws every leaf whole into the pen, the pen cannot stop the draw, and the walk is bounded only by its own limits (2,048 references, 64 levels, 65,535 points a leaf). Without the cut it was about 134 million elements and 7.5 GB, and the process aborted. The cut (4 x 65,536 elements) and the largest coordinate a point may have (1,048,576 font units, 64 ems of the largest em) are numbers chosen here, not the format's. The renderer keeps the `Broken` answer for a glyph, so the cost is paid once a glyph, and text that names many such glyphs pays for each | `crates/fonts/tests/it/composites.rs` | A hostile font file, from the machine or a drawing, can slow a layout by seconds; it can no longer end the process |

## Project

| # | Gap | Evidence | User impact |
|---|---|---|---|
| J3 | `SECURITY.md`'s private reporting form isn't available while the repository is private | GitHub, 2026-10-31 | None until it is public |
| J1 | `contributors.json` generator absent | Design section 10 | Credits are by hand |
| J2 | **The release workflow and the packaging are written and have not run:** `release.yml` builds, for a `v*` tag, a `.tar.gz`, a `.deb` and an AppImage for Linux on Intel/AMD and on ARM64, a `.zip` and an `.msi` for Windows on both, and a `.zip` and a `.dmg` for macOS universal, and uploads the twelve with `SHA256SUMS` to a draft release (`packaging/`, `docs/releasing.md`). Each installer is installed or mounted, run and removed on its runner; the `.deb`, the AppImage (in a sandbox), the `.msi` (and an ARM64 one built, not installed) and the `.dmg` were tried on Linux, Windows and an Intel Mac, and nothing ARM ran. The downloads are unsigned, with the signing and notarisation steps left as comments for the owner; an icon on `diacraft.exe`, a wizard for the `.msi`, update channels, and file associations on macOS are not done. **The dependencies' licence texts are done:** `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` lists every crate the two programs are built from on Linux, Windows and macOS with its licence text, and is in every download and in the window (File, About, Third-party licences); `cargo xtask licenses --check` is a step of `ci` and of the Linux job of the release; the web build's crates (`wasm32-unknown-unknown`) are in `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES-WEB.txt`, which the web app's window shows and the app's page links; the release workflow's `site` job builds the website with the app into an artifact and has the Cloudflare deploy as a comment for the owner, and has not run on GitHub | Design section 10 | Windows and macOS ask for a confirmation at the first opening |
| J4 | **Open question for the owner: LGPL protocol files in the Linux build.** `winit` (the pinned fork) uses `wayland-protocols-plasma` for KWin's blur on Wayland. The crate is MIT, but the protocol description files its macro turns into Rust code come from KDE's plasma-wayland-protocols, whose files say `LGPL-2.1-or-later` (the crate carries the LGPL text in `plasma-wayland-protocols/COPYING.LIB`). `AGENTS.md` says no LGPL code is depended on, and `cargo xtask deny` cannot see it (the crate says MIT). The notices list it, with the LGPL text and a note, as a reviewed file (`REVIEWED_FILES` in `xtask/src/licenses/policy.rs`) and the check passes. The choices: accept it (interface descriptions, not code, and a licence text that ships), or take the KWin blur out of the winit fork so the crate leaves the build. Nothing else compiled into a program has a licence outside `deny.toml`'s list | `cargo xtask licenses`, 2026-11-22 (measured) | A Linux download carries generated code derived from LGPL files, with the licence text and no offer of source |
| J5 | **The browser version opens, saves, exports and saves a PDF of the print through the browser's own file controls; what it cannot do is written here.** The engine reads and writes a file by its path, so a file chosen in the page is put in the engine's memory under a path the page makes (`crates/engine/src/memfs.rs`: at most 64 files and 768 MiB, counted in the process's memory ledger; a file read once is moved out to the reader and a stencil that is open is copied each time) and the commands run on that path with the budgets and the refusals they have on the desktop; what a command wrote there is taken out and handed to the person (`FileHost`, `crates/ui-egui/src/file_host.rs`; the page's side is `apps/diacraft-web/src/web_files.rs`). **Open** is File, Open, Ctrl+O or a drop on the page, for every type the desktop opens, for stencils (More Shapes, Open Stencil) and for pictures (Insert, Pictures). **Save, Save As, Export and Save as PDF** download the file under the name the window's card asks for (the card also chooses the type), or write it in place through the File System Access API where the browser has it, and a write that fails is downloaded instead and said; the check after a save, the save card, the macro question and the save note are the desktop's. A file is judged from its size before a byte is read, against a budget of 1 GiB (`diacraft_vsdx::WEB_MAX_MEMORY`; 2.6 GB on the desktop): a drawing of 20,000 shapes opened and one of 40,000 was refused in words, with the drawing that was open still open (measured in headless Chrome, 2026-11-22). The module links with an 8 MiB stack (`.cargo/config.toml`), and the deepest legal files open in it. **Not done, and unverified:** the real dialogs of the File System Access API (headless Chrome cannot show them: its paths were run against a script that stands in for `showOpenFilePicker` and `showSaveFilePicker`); Safari, Edge, Windows and macOS browsers, phones and tablets; a printer (a page cannot reach one, and the browser's own print would print the canvas and not the drawing, so Print saves the PDF a printer would be given). **Left as they are:** a Save of a drawing with no handle downloads the file again each time, and the browser may rename it; a tab has no file to compare, so a file changed by something else is not noticed; one file at a time is opened (a drop of several opens the first and says so); a read holds the page's thread, so the progress card shows for a frame and Cancel cannot stop a read that has begun; the browser's memory only grows, so a tab that has read a large drawing keeps what it grew to until it closes | `docs/website.md`, "Files in the browser" and "Measured, "; headless Chrome 158 and Firefox 160 on Linux (measured, 2026-11-22) | A drawing is lost when the tab closes unless it was saved; a very large drawing does not open in the browser |
| J6 | **Open question for the owner: the `.wasm` carries the names the file formats require.** The module holds the namespace addresses and content types of the VSDX and OpenDocument packages it reads (for example `http://schemas.microsoft.com/…` and `application/vnd.ms-…`, which `AGENTS.md` allows as names needed for interoperability), command ids of the ribbon that end in the reference product's name inside longer words, and, the file-type labels and the trademark line the owner allowed on 2026-11-23. The desktop downloads carry the same. **The licence list is no longer in the module**: it is a file of `app/assets/`, held to the site's name check except the copyright lines `cargo xtask brands` keeps as the licences have them. `cargo xtask site` scans the site's text for vendor names and `--web` scans the app's page, script and style, but not the module's bytes, because the strings above are required and `cargo xtask brands` holds the source they come from. If the public site may carry no such string at all, the web build would need the readers (and so the names) left out of it, which is also the way to a smaller file | `strings` on the module (measured, 2026-11-22) | None for a person using the app; a public file that holds the names a public repository's source holds |
| J7 | **The web app loads in parts and fetches what its first frame does not need; what this leaves open is written here.** **A visitor who has the app cached starts it later on a fast link:** a module the browser compiles from a stream the page made has no address, so the browser cannot keep its compiled code for the next visit, as it does for one file it fetched itself, and the parts are read through the page's script and checked before the program starts; measured in headless Chrome and Firefox, a returning visit's first frame was 23 to 40 milliseconds later on a link with a round trip of 20 milliseconds and about 100 later with none at all, and as early or earlier at 60 milliseconds (the parts need no request; the one file was asked about each visit); a first visit is 25 to 26% earlier on a 9 Mbit/s link. Getting the code cache back needs one URL for the whole module, which a CDN that serves static files cannot make out of parts, or a service worker or `IndexedDB` to keep the module, which the privacy page says the app does not use. **Hack (0.31 MB, 1.7% of the module) was not moved out:** two dialogs only draw it, but its bytes are in a crate's source (the build tool would read them out of the cargo checkout) and the window would install its faces again after the first frame. **The cover is 2.4 MB and shown at most 427 by 240 points:** the splash copy of it (0.2 MB, 836 by 470) would serve at one pixel to the point, and a smaller cover for the web is a picture task (`docs/branding.md`). **An edge's own Brotli level is not known:** the harness's server stands in for it at quality 5, and Cloudflare Pages was not run (the release workflow's `site` job has not run on GitHub, and its `deploy` job has not run against Cloudflare). **The loader was run in headless Chrome 158 and Firefox 160 on Linux only** (Safari, Edge, Windows and macOS browsers, phones, a real GPU and a real network were not tried), and it needs `crypto.subtle` for the digests (every https page and `localhost`; elsewhere it checks lengths only and says so in the console). **A build made by `trunk build` alone** (no `cargo xtask site --web`) has no manifest or parts: its page starts with trunk's own script, and its window says in words that it has no splash picture, cover or licence list | `docs/website.md`, "Loading in parts" and "Measured, " (measured, 2026-11-23) | A first visit is faster; a returning one may be a little slower on a fast link; the cover and the licence list come when asked for |
| J8 | **The web app can be opened in a window of its own, takes the keys a browser would act on, has Full Screen with the keyboard locked, and asks before a tab with an unsaved drawing is closed or reloaded: closed.** A tab keeps Ctrl+N, Ctrl+T, Ctrl+W, Ctrl+Tab and the like for the browser and never tells the page; a window of its own (an installed web app, or `chrome --app`) and full screen with the keyboard locked pass them to the page (measured in Chrome 158 with real key presses: `docs/website.md`, "Measured, "). The app has a web app manifest (its start page and scope are `/`, the root of its own site) and no service worker (Chrome reports no installability error), the File view's Home page offers the window (a button where the browser gives an install prompt, the steps in words where it does not), the page takes every chord of the command key and every function key that a command has (except Ctrl+C, X and V), Ctrl+W closes the window after the question about changes, and the View tab's Full Screen locks the keyboard. **Found and fixed on the way:** in a tab, Ctrl+R (Rotate Right) reloaded the page and lost the drawing, and Ctrl+U, Ctrl+H, Ctrl+J, Ctrl+F, Ctrl+D, Ctrl+K and Ctrl+- were acted on by the browser as well as by the window. **Not measured:** macOS (an Intel Mac with Chrome, Edge and Safari is at hand, and a key sent into its logged-in session could land in another app), Windows, Edge, Safari, an installed window opened from the install prompt (the `--app` window is the same kind), a real keyboard. **Settled in ** (owner, 2026-11-23: "Yes, ask before leaving"): a tab keeps its keys, and a person who stays in a tab and presses Ctrl+W closed it with no question about unsaved changes, because the web runner's `beforeunload` handler never ran (its event name has the word `on` in front of it, and it would have saved nothing: no storage is built). While the drawing has unsaved changes the page now has a handler for `beforeunload` of its own, and the browser shows its own "Leave site?" box when the tab is closed or reloaded or the page navigates away (measured in Chrome 158 and Firefox 160: `docs/website.md`, "Measured, "); a saved drawing, a new one and an idle page ask nothing, and Close (Ctrl+W) asks DiaCraft's question and takes the browser's away before it closes the window. **Not measured:** Safari, Edge, Windows, macOS and a phone or tablet (a browser may show no box there). **Ctrl+Q and Ctrl+Shift+Q** went to the page in Chrome on Linux and did nothing, and Firefox quits on Ctrl+Q; `Ctrl+Shift+T` is kept by Chrome only while a tab has been closed. **Ours** (no specification gives them): the place of the offer, Full Screen, Ctrl+W as Close. |
| J9 | **The site and the web app are kept in the browser as long as they safely can be; what this leaves open is written here.** A file is kept for a year if and only if its name is the hash of its own bytes: the parts and the data of the app, the glue, the style and the icon (Trunk's three files, named again by their SHA-256 by `site --web`) and the site's pictures of `assets/hashed/`. `cargo xtask site` and `site --web` read `_headers` as Pages applies it and refuse the reverse of that rule, two cache headers for one address and a page with no policy, and `cargo xtask deploy-check <BASE_URL> [<BASE_URL>]` asks each deployed site (the pages, the app) what it sends (`docs/website.md`, "The web app", "Caching"). **Open:** (1) **Pages may not read a splat in the middle of a path** (`/page-*.css` of the app's own `_headers`): its documentation shows a splat at the end only; the first run of `deploy-check` after the deploy shows it, and a rule Pages ignores leaves the glue, the style and the icon (0.29 MB) asked about on every visit and breaks nothing. (2) **The snippets of the glue** (`app/snippets/eframe-<hash>/inline0.js`, 280 bytes) are asked about on every visit: the hash in their folder's name is of the crate's name and version, and not of the file, so a rule that kept them could keep a changed file under the same name; keeping them means rewriting the glue's imports and naming the glue again. (3) **`styles.css`, `download.js`, `icon-512.png` and `social.jpg`** are asked about too (13 KB of conditional requests, and a 124 KB icon that only an install asks for); hashing the style and the script into `assets/hashed/` is simple for the check to hold and costs a rename and a rewrite of every page at each edit. (4) **What Pages compresses** is Cloudflare's own list: its documentation says it serves Gzip and Brotli whenever possible, and found the `.wasm` ending is what makes it compress a part; neither has been seen on the real edge, which the same first run shows. (5) `deploy-check` follows the links of the home page into the pages they name, and asks `curl` for its answers, so a server that answers a browser differently from `curl` shows as it answered `curl`. (6) **The release workflow's `deploy` job does not run `deploy-check`**: it has not run against Pages, and items (1) and (4) are what it may find, and a failing check after a good deploy would turn the run red with nothing to do in it; the owner or the coordinator runs it by hand after the first deploy, and adds it as the last step of the `deploy` job once it passes (`docs/website.md`, "Deploy to Cloudflare Pages", step 7). |
